# Changelog

All notable development updates, decisions, and discoveries for the IzzyAgents x Bullet Digital Media engagement.

Format: newest entries on top. Each entry uses UK dates (DD/MM/YYYY) and 24-hour times. Currency in USD.

Entry types:
- **Added** - new code, features, files, or capabilities
- **Changed** - modifications to existing behaviour, scope, or structure
- **Removed** - deletions, deprecations, or retired components
- **Decision** - confirmed scope, architecture, or product decisions
- **Discovery** - new information learned about Bullet's processes, platforms, or constraints
- **Fixed** - bug fixes or corrections

## [Unreleased]

### 26/08/2026 - S1-26b/c review round 6: the postcode normalizer stops being patched and starts being specified

Round 6 returned five blocking findings at `a0658d7`. Every one reproduced by execution before any code was touched. The headline is not any single bug - it is that `normalize_postcode` has now produced a blocking finding in **three consecutive rounds**, and the reviewer named exactly why: *"the postcode normalizer is fixed in intent, and the test that would notice it broke is standing next to the branch it does not enter."*

- **Discovery (the mechanism, instrumented rather than guessed)**: `TestPostcodeFormInvariance` parametrizes nine pairs. Instrumenting which of them actually ENTER the alphabetic-drop branch added in round 5 gives **exactly one** (`75008 Paris`/`Paris 75008`), and that pair varies only on the ORDER axis - the one axis where the branch is correct. Seven pairs short-circuit in the UK regex at step 1; the eighth carries a digit in every token, so the drop is a no-op. Deleting the branch outright kills three tests, so it was NOT revert-green in the crude sense and the mutation manifest could not have caught it. **The subtler failure: the branch is covered, but only along a safe axis.** The declared invariant named four axes and the parametrize list exercised the branch on one.
- **Discovery (the root cause is the METHOD, not the care)**: a normalizer tested by example is tested by examples chosen by whoever wrote the fix - who by construction does not know which axis they missed. That is why round 4 fixed order and broke separator, and round 5 fixed separator and broke it differently while adding a lossy COLLIDE (`1011 AB` and `1011 CD`, two different Amsterdam postcodes, collapsed to one key - a false-merge axis that did not previously exist). No amount of diligence fixes this; only a different method does.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_identity_key_properties.py` - property tests over GENERATED inputs for the six invariants (separator-independence across 13 cores x 8 separator forms, order-independence on the international token path, no-lossy-collision over 5000 samples, the malformed-UK contract, UK-unchanged, placeholder rejection). It earned its place before it was even committed: it found a split that **27 hand-picked examples did not** - `AB12CD` matched the UK branch while `AB 12 CD` fell through to the token path, so one postcode keyed two ways.
- **Changed (P0, `normalize_postcode` rebuilt, not patched)**: tokenize into maximal alpha runs and digit runs - splitting on the alpha/digit TRANSITION as well as on separators, which is what makes the token set identical for `1011 AB`, `1011AB` and `1011-AB` - then SORT, dropping nothing. The two steps do different jobs and both are load-bearing: the tokenizer delivers separator-independence, the sort delivers order-independence, and keeping every token is what makes it non-lossy. Plus: the UK pattern is now probed on the separator-stripped form as well as the raw string, and a US ZIP+4 reduces to its ZIP5 before tokenizing (a sorted 9-digit result cannot be positionally truncated). Sorting is safe here in a way it was not in round 4, because the token set is canonical BEFORE the sort.
- **Decision (deviating from the reviewer's sketched mechanism, with evidence)**: the review sketched "tokenize alnum runs, drop an alphabetic token only when it is not adjacent to its digit block, truncate an all-numeric 9-digit result to 5". Implemented LITERALLY, that satisfies three of its own four required pairs and **fails `('75008 Paris','Paris-75008')`**, because adjacency-keeping preserves `PARIS` on both sides and the order still differs. Adding a sort to it still fails four cases including `VLT 1117`/`VLT1117`, another of its own required pairs, because alnum-run tokenizing leaves `VLT1117` as one token and `VLT 1117` as two. So the reviewer's CONTRACT - every test case they specified - is implemented exactly, and the mechanism differs. Recorded here and in the PR reply rather than left for them to discover.
- **Fixed (P1)**: the keyed path never fell back to email. 0013's own docstring confirms the premise ("pre-existing rows simply carry NULL `identity_key` and fall through to CREATE"), so every pre-0013 row was permanently invisible to every later keyed signing and detection was order-dependent - a regression against S1-26, whose predicate was `WHERE email`. **And the first cut of that fix caused a worse regression, caught by the DB suite the moment Docker came up**: an unrestricted email fallback matched "Brand Gym" Hackney against Croydon (one `ops@` mailbox, different postcodes), turning a CONFIDENT franchise separation into an ambiguous flag AND suppressing the GHL lookup behind it. The fallback is now restricted to `identity_key IS NULL` rows: a row with a DIFFERENT key is evidence of difference, a row with NO key is absence of evidence, and conflating the two destroys the separation the key just made.
- **Fixed (P1)**: the subordination guard cannot engage on an empty candidate set (`collision` is None when `candidates` is empty), so a sibling excluded from that set - A's row keeps `ghl_subaccount_id IS NULL` after a lost create response - was refused nothing and re-entered via the GHL leg, which was structurally blind to bar 4. `address` is now SENT on create and `_classify_ghl_hit` applies `addresses_materially_diverge`, with "we hold an address the location cannot corroborate" downgrading to UNDECIDABLE.
- **Fixed (P1, the claim rather than the code)**: bar 4 is INERT in the case round 5 introduced it for. `Company.Address` and `Company.Zip` are read from the SAME HubSpot company record, so one owner with one company record and two deals gives two rows byte-identical on name, postcode, phone AND address - every bar clears and site 2 auto-links. The round-5 test seeded two DIFFERENT addresses, which is **the same shape of test round 5 itself rejected on the phone bar**. Claims softened in `_pick_sibling` and migration 0013, and the gap PINNED by `test_one_company_record_two_deals_still_auto_links` so nobody mistakes it for closed. **The reviewer's suggested guard is not implementable today and this was verified, not assumed**: `clients_payload`'s header records (verified 28/07 against a real signed document) that Bullet's documents link ONLY the deal, so `hubspot_company_id` resolves to None - a guard built on it would be inert in exactly the way bar 3 was when built on a surname the INT template never populates. It needs the `Existing_Client_Identifier` field or the HubSpot Service Key, both outstanding client asks. That is what makes the round-5 client question load-bearing rather than informational.
- **Fixed (P1)**: `make review-gate` was not run by anything - `ci.yml` was untouched by the round-5 PR. **Deferring this last round was wrong**: the mechanism built to stop round 6 would not have survived its first refactor, and the 25/25 claim was a point-in-time measurement on one laptop. Both halves now run in CI with `fetch-depth: 0` (the static gate resolves a merge base and EXITS rather than reporting a vacuous pass over an empty diff).
- **Fixed (same pass)**: a pure prefix collision flagged an INNOCENT client against a row positively established as a different business AND suppressed that client's own GHL lookup, so its real existing location was never found - `collision` is now set only when a candidate actually cleared bar 1. The `already_succeeded` branch returned `ghl_subaccount_id=None` with `skipped=True`, which Inngest records as clean success: no failed action, no flag, no retry, a client permanently without a sub-account, and `already_succeeded` appeared nowhere in the test file - it now recovers the id from the action row, repairs the client row, and falls through to the create path when neither holds an id. `_clear_possible_duplicate` was revert-green (every DB-link test seeds the returning row fresh, so `AND possible_duplicate = true` matched zero rows) - now has a test that arrives pre-flagged. `names_materially_diverge`'s empty-stem branch (round 2, finding 6) had no killing test and no manifest entry.
- **Fixed (the gate's own false-pass vector)**: `review_gate_mutate.py` had no clean-source baseline - `_node_id_resolves` proved a test EXISTS, not that it PASSES, so a test already red for an unrelated reason exited 1 under mutation and recorded KILLED. The same false-pass class the round-5 work existed to end, living inside the tool that checks for it. Now baselines every unique `must_fail` on unmutated source (cached) and reports ERROR on a red one. **Verified by deliberately reddening a test**: it reports `does NOT pass on unmutated source - a kill would be spurious` instead of KILLED.
- **Fixed (smaller)**: `test_ghl_subaccount.py` still described the CEL key as "identity_key or the unique client_id" in one place (its sibling was corrected in round 5, this one was missed - the same stale-claim class, in a file that round edited); `pytest.raises(Exception)` narrowed to `SQLAlchemyError` so it cannot accept an exception raised before the code under test; `render.yaml`'s API-only `preDeployCommand` scope documented with the reasoning (two concurrent `alembic upgrade head` calls collide, and the worker's un-migrated failure mode is retryable where the API's is a permanently-lost signing).
- **Plan**: `~/.claude/plans/s1-26bc-round6-fix.md`.

### 25/08/2026 - S1-26b/c review round 5: the review gate, built before the fixes and proven against them

Round 5 at `7a018bd` returned five blocking findings. CI was 5/5 green and honest (609 passed, 0 skipped on a real Neon branch), so the suite was not the thing that failed. Every one of the five reproduces - verified by executing the code rather than by trusting the review.

- **Discovery (the pattern behind five rounds, stated plainly)**: the findings are not five unrelated bugs, they are four mechanical causes. (1) **Tests written to confirm, not to falsify** - the headline franchise test seeds `+44 7700 900111` vs `...900222`, so what it demonstrates is the phone bar working, not franchise logic working; equalize the phones and the assertion flips to a merge. (2) **Fixing the reported instance, not the invariant** - round 4's postcode fix made the key order-independent and separator-dependent, a regression of the same class it fixed. (3) **New logic judged against the happy path, not the real input distribution** - `contact_name_agrees` was built on `Client.LastName`, a field this module's OWN header warns is UK-template-pinned. (4) **Half-connected seams** - `clients.address` is persisted by this PR and seeded in the headline test, but never selected into `_SIBLING_SELECT`.
- **Discovery (the reviewer's method, reverse-engineered)**: six repeatable techniques, not intuition. **Revert-green testing** (delete the fix, run the suite, passes = finding). **Grep the literal our own comment names** - `_is_sequential_digits`'s comment names `"1234567890"` and the suite contains zero occurrences of it, so our documentation hands them the query. **Flip one seeded value** to test whether a test passes for the reason it claims. **Trace an input to its source** rather than assuming a field is populated. **Treat every docstring as a falsifiable claim.** **Build sequences, not states** - finding 5 is a lost-response timeline, not a bad state. Running underneath all six: **absence vs disagreement**, which nearly every finding in every round has turned on.
- **Added**: `apps/api/scripts/review_gate.py` + `make review-gate`, mechanizing the four techniques that are mechanizable. **G1 comment-literal coverage** - a fix comment naming example data must have that data in a test. **G4 no conditional assertions** - an `assert` behind an `if` that reads the system under test. **G5 no defaults on discriminating fixture params** - a fixture that defaults `phone`/`contact_*`/`postal_code` makes every seeded row agree, pinning any bar that reads it in one direction. **G6 diff-scoped PII** - net-new live identifiers counted against the merge base, which is exactly how the reviewer found this round's PII item.
- **Verified (the gate's own acceptance test - run against the UNFIXED code before any fix landed)**: it independently reproduces the review's findings, including its arithmetic. G1 surfaces `AB12 3CDE`, `E81AAX`, `75008 Paris` and `1234567890` - the four literals behind finding 3, whose guards are revert-green. G4 finds `test_ghl_subaccount.py:2039`. G5 finds `_seed_client`'s six pinned defaults. G6 reports the client Workspace mailbox as **merge-base 2, HEAD 5** (the review's own figures) plus the two live GHL location ids at `sprint-1-34a-test-results.md:53`. 24 findings total, all real after two false-positive classes were tightened out (docstring prose leaking into G1 via `"""`-delimiter matching; CamelCase Python class names matching the GHL-id shape in G6 - now requires a digit and rejects CamelCase).
- **Decision**: **build the gate BEFORE the fixes, and require it to reproduce the known findings first.** A gate that cannot catch what we already know is present is not a gate, it is a formality - and after five rounds a formality is what we can least afford. The three techniques it cannot mechanize (invariant statement per normalizer, source-trace per new signal across BOTH PandaDoc accounts, sequence walk per handler) ride in the PR body as answered checklist items, so the reasoning is visible to the reviewer rather than assumed.
- **Decision**: the gate is additive to `/pre-pr-review`, not a replacement. It catches the mechanical classes; the skill catches the ones needing judgement. Both run before a PR.
- **Changed (CLAUDE.md, so the gate is actually a gate)**: added step **8a - `make review-gate` is REQUIRED and must be clean** to the ticket lifecycle, plus a new **"The review gate"** section documenting the four static checks, the mutation-manifest contract, and the four things it cannot check (invariant statement, source trace across BOTH PandaDoc accounts, sequence walk, docstring claim audit) which now go in the PR body as answered questions. Step 8 also gained an explicit **"Docker must be UP or this means nothing"** warning: without Postgres ~223 db-marked tests SKIP and the run still reports green, which is precisely how rounds 1-2 shipped. Without this documentation the gate would have been a script nobody ran - the tooling equivalent of the revert-green guards it exists to catch.
- **Decision (the durable contract)**: **a new guard without a manifest entry is not finished.** Adding an entry is a two-line diff; skipping one has cost a review round every time. `UNPROVEN` (the named test skipped) is explicitly NOT a pass - the runner reports it separately from `KILLED` for exactly that reason.
- **Decision (CI, deliberately deferred)**: the gate is **local-only for now**, not wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. The static half is fast and deterministic and could go in cheaply, but the mutation half rewrites source files and re-invokes pytest once per guard (18 runs today, growing per ticket), and G1 is a heuristic that could block an unrelated PR on a false positive. Wiring it into CI is worth doing as its own change, with its own ticket, rather than riding a review-fix PR - and it needs `fetch-depth: 0` so the merge-base diff resolves. Recorded here rather than left implicit, because a gate that only runs when someone remembers is the failure mode this whole entry is about.
- **Discovery (verified by execution, not review-reading)**: `normalize_phone` returns `""` for `+44 20 7700 0000`, `020 7000 0000` and `0800 100 1000` - the `len(set(tail)) <= 2` filler check rejects real UK landlines, so bar 2 is permanently unsatisfiable for those clients and a genuine returning client gets a duplicate sub-account on **every** signing, silently. Also confirmed: `normalize_postcode("E8, 1AA")` -> `"1AAE8"` while `"E8 1AA"` -> `"E81AA"` (one business, two keys); `contact_name_agrees("Sarah", None, "Sarah", None)` -> `True` (bar 3 collapses to first names); `"Bjørn Fitness"` -> `bjrnfitness` vs `"Bjorn Fitness"` -> `bjornfitness` (NFKD does not decompose ø/ß/ł/æ).
- **Added (G2, the mutation manifest)**: `apps/api/tests/mutation_manifest.toml` + `scripts/review_gate_mutate.py` + `make review-gate-db`. Each guard declares the test that KILLS it; the runner breaks the guard, runs only that test, and asserts it FAILS. Three outcomes, and the third is the important one: `KILLED` (defended), `SURVIVED` (revert-green, a finding), `UNPROVEN` (the named test SKIPPED - explicitly **not** a pass, because a skipped test cannot fail and treating skips as green is what let rounds 1-2 ship).
- **Fixed (finding 2)**: `normalize_postcode` no longer sorts tokens. It drops purely-alphabetic tokens when a digit-bearing one is present and joins in ORIGINAL order, and `_UK_POSTCODE`'s separator is relaxed to `[\s,.\-]*`. All four axes now agree: order, separator, case, attached town. The invariant is written into the docstring so it is testable rather than assumed, and `TestPostcodeFormInvariance` pins the concrete canonical output too (migration 0013 warns stored keys are a snapshot of this normalizer, so the output is contract, not implementation detail).
- **Discovery (the gate earning its keep on its first run)**: the mutation runner caught a **revert-green guard inside the round-5 fix itself**. The relaxed UK separator survived deletion - every test case then written for it (`E8, 1AA`, `E8-1AA`) canonicalizes correctly via step 3 even with the separator unrelaxed, so none of them could kill it. The case that actually distinguishes the two is a comma-separated line carrying a FLAT NUMBER: `Flat 2, E8, 1AA` keys as `E81AA` relaxed and `2E81AA` unrelaxed, because the unrelaxed UK branch misses and step 3 then keeps the flat number as a digit-bearing token. Added as the killing case. **This is the exact class of finding that produced five review rounds, caught before the PR instead of during it.**
- **Fixed (finding 4)**: `contact_name_agrees` requires first AND last non-blank on both sides, plus a placeholder-name denylist (`head office`, `accounts department`, `front desk`, ...) matching the posture already taken on placeholder postcodes and phones. Recorded rather than left to be discovered: where the surname is never populated (an INT template lacking that merge token), bar 3 is permanently unsatisfiable and the unkeyed path never auto-links there - the safe direction, and stated explicitly.
- **Fixed (finding 1, pure half)**: new `identity_key.addresses_materially_diverge` - a DISQUALIFIER, true only when both addresses are present and differ. Round 2 correctly showed address cannot CORROBORATE (it collapses with the postcode); that argument does not transfer to disqualification, since a differing address can only ever refuse a link, never grant one. Wired through `_SIBLING_SELECT` (which persisted `address` but never queried it - a half-connected seam), the client SELECT, and `_pick_sibling` as bar 4. **Absence ABSTAINS here, the opposite of `names_materially_diverge` where absence IS divergence** - documented at both sites with the reasoning, since an unexplained asymmetry is exactly what the reviewer hunts.
- **Fixed (same pass)**: `normalize_phone` was rejecting real UK landlines (`len(set(tail)) <= 2` treats `"+44 20 7700 0000"` as filler), so bar 2 was permanently unsatisfiable for those clients and a genuine returning client got a duplicate sub-account on **every** signing, silently - arguably worse than any of the five blockers, and not one of them. Now rejects by shape: a single repeated digit, or an alternating two-digit block. Unicode fold gained an explicit transliteration table for the letters NFKD cannot decompose (ø/ß/ł/æ/þ/đ) - the set the INT account introduces. `test_flagged_row_dashboard_claim_holds` now asserts the flag FIRES before asserting what it implies. Three net-new occurrences of the client Workspace mailbox and the two live GHL location ids at `sprint-1-34a-test-results.md:53` redacted (the pre-existing June occurrences left alone, per the round-2 precedent of redacting forward rather than rewriting published history).
- **Verified**: **675 passed, 0 skipped** (Docker Postgres up - a skipped run is what let rounds 1-2 ship, so 0-skipped is the bar). **25 of 25 mutations KILLED, 0 survived, 0 unproven.** `make review-gate` PASS (G1/G4/G5/G6 all clean). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` + `make typecheck` + `make codegen` clean, no drift. Migration 0013 down/up round-trip re-verified after the `lock_timeout` addition.
- **Fixed (`/pre-pr-review`, self-inflicted - the round-5 fix introduced its own regression)**: the first cut of the zombie fix rolled back UNCONDITIONALLY in `_record_failure`. That closed the zombie and silently discarded the possible-duplicate flag, which is deliberately uncommitted and rides the terminal commit - so every failed GHL create would have dropped the human's only signal that a signing was ambiguous, in exactly the case they most need it. Now `try: fail_action() except SQLAlchemyError: rollback(); fail_action()` - the ordinary path keeps the flag, the DB-error path still recovers.
  - **Two further findings inside that one fix, both from the gate rather than from reading.** (1) Pinning the recovery on `PendingRollbackError` alone was wrong: which exception surfaces depends on who notices the broken transaction first - SQLAlchemy raises `PendingRollbackError` if it has already deactivated it, but if the statement reaches Postgres first the server rejects it and asyncpg's `InFailedSQLTransactionError` arrives wrapped as a `DBAPIError`. The savepoint test fixture reproduces the SECOND, so the narrow catch left the zombie open on the very path it was recovering. Caught only because the new test actually ran the path. Now catches `SQLAlchemyError`, which covers both. (2) The regression test for the flag was written against the **wrong path**: it used a DB-sibling collision, whose flag is written BEFORE `begin_action` and therefore swept into `begin_action`'s own commit, so no later rollback can lose it. The mutation SURVIVED, proving the test could not fail. Retargeted to the GHL-UNDECIDABLE flag, which is the one genuinely written after `begin_action`. **A test on the wrong path reports a guard defended while leaving it wide open - the exact failure mode this entire review round is about, caught on ourselves before the PR rather than in round 6.**
- **Fixed (`/pre-pr-review`, three parallel adversarial passes - and the gate's own first real catch was on US)**: the mandatory gate was run as three concurrent reviews (failure-paths + stale claims; tests-that-pass-for-the-wrong-reason; the new tooling itself). It returned **nine P0s across two clusters**, every one reproduced by execution rather than by reading.
  - **Two P0s in the DIFF, both caused by our own G5 fix.** Removing `_seed_client`'s shared `phone` default - correct in itself - left those tests with NO phone on either row, so **bar 2 refused first and bars 1 and 3 stopped deciding anything**. Deleting `names_materially_diverge` from `_pick_sibling` outright left all 672 tests passing; so did disabling the `require_contact_name` wiring - including in the very test whose docstring says *"without this test, a future edit that weakens bar 3 back to phone-only would silently reopen round 2's finding 5 and no test would catch it."* Five tests re-armed by seeding the signals that must CLEAR so the bar under test is the sole refusal, and **bars 1 and 3 now have manifest entries** (they had none, which is why nothing noticed). Also: `GhlNotConfiguredError`'s raise sites were revert-green (both tests asserted the base `RuntimeError`, satisfied by either type); the F5 phone bar silently withdrew the at-least-once backstop for phone-less clients while the docstring still claimed it held (a deliberate trade - those clients also fail DB bar 2 - now stated and tested); and the numeric-JSON coercions in `_location_phone`/`_location_postcode` had no test at all.
  - **Seven P0s in the GATE TOOLING itself, which had never been reviewed.** The worst: **`make review-gate` exited 0 when every mutation was UNPROVEN**, so the default invocation was green with 9 of 19 guards never executed - the tool contradicting its own founding premise. A stale `must_fail` node id reported KILLED rather than ERROR (rename a test class and its guard could be deleted with the gate staying green); with `CI=1` and Postgres down, conftest's own fail-closed guard raises `UsageError` → rc 4 → **every** db-marked guard would have reported KILLED. The mutation runner could also corrupt a working tree: `try/finally` does not cover SIGTERM/SIGHUP, a concurrent run permanently clobbers a shared file while both report success, and the restore blindly overwrote edits made during the run. All fixed: UNPROVEN now fails (with an explicit `--allow-unproven` laptop escape), pytest exit codes 2-5 are ERROR not KILLED, node ids are `--collect-only`-verified before mutating, sidecar files + signal handlers + atomic writes + a hash check before restore + an exclusive `flock`, a per-test timeout, and ambiguous patterns rejected. `review_gate.py` gained: an unresolvable base ref now EXITS instead of reporting clean over an empty diff (a shallow CI checkout would have done exactly that), and G1 now scans untracked files - a brand-new module is precisely the pre-commit state it exists to cover.
  - **Stale claims corrected** (the reviewer treats every docstring as falsifiable): five places still said the GHL leg judges on "name AND postcode" when it now needs phone; four still said address "does not vote"; the module header still said "TWO bars" where `_pick_sibling` applies four; `normalize_postcode`'s numbered steps did not match the code's actual order ("00000 ABC" behaves differently under each - the code's order is the safer one, so the docstring was renumbered and the case tested); the subordination rationale's reason (a) had become flatly false; the collision log said "divergent names" when the refusal may now come from phone, contact name or address; the not-reused log recorded `has_location_postcode` but nothing about the deciding phone; and `config.py`/`ghl/client.py`'s error-model block still named the removed bare `RuntimeError`.
- **Deferred, logged not dropped - needs a card (proposed S1-47, "harden the review gate's heuristics")**: G1's `_looks_like_example_data` rejects 48 literals in this diff that appear in no test (all-caps tokens like `TBD`, lowercase multi-word placeholders like `front desk`, underscore-bearing ids like `cus_Nx8k2Lp`), and its quote regex is defeated by a prose apostrophe; G4 catches 2 of 8 conditional-assert shapes (misses attribute/subscript conditions, `orelse`, and asserts nested one level deeper); G5 reads only `kwonlyargs` on functions named `_seed*`, missing the commoner positional-default form and `_make_*`/`_insert_*` helpers; G6 scans only `.py`/`.md` (so a JSON fixture or `render.yaml` leak is invisible) and compares repo-wide COUNTS, so relocating a live id from a scratch note into shipping source reads as clean. None of these make the gate report clean on something it currently flags - they are coverage gaps in a tool that is already additive to `/pre-pr-review` - but they are exactly the "a gate you trust more than it deserves" risk, so they are written down rather than assumed fixed.
- **Plan**: `~/.claude/plans/s1-26bc-round5-review-gate.md` - the gate, the five blockers with their fixes, the same-pass items, and the client question the code cannot answer (does any one person sign for more than one site, sharing a phone or head-office postcode? Bullet's "franchisees are separate clients" answer is about franchis**ees**; the code applies it to franchise **sites**).

### 24/08/2026 - S1-26b/c review round 4 (post-rebase condition 4): four blocking findings fixed

Lead review at `df8874e` (the post-rebase commit). Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES, four findings blocking merge plus several one-line fixes. Confirmed good and untouched: all 7 round-2 findings and every round-1 headline item re-checked against the code and still genuinely present; the rebase itself (all 7 handlers single-arg); no SQL injection, no authz gap, no secrets; the conftest CI fail-closed guard and the `_UNSET` fake sentinel.

- **Fixed (finding 1)**: `ghl/client.py` `find_location_by_email` was still gating retriability on `status_code == 429 or status_code >= 500`, missing 401/403/408 - `_RETRIABLE_STATUS` was wired into `create_location` only. Since the lookup runs BEFORE the create on every attempt, a key blip there raised `GhlClientError` -> `NonRetriableError` and dead-lettered every in-flight signing before the create-path fix was ever reached, making it unreachable. Now both methods share `_RETRIABLE_STATUS`. The test that claimed coverage (`test_transient_4xx_is_retriable_not_dead_lettered`) only ever called `create_location` - parametrized it over both methods so the claim is actually true.
- **Fixed (finding 2, PII/live-tenant-ids)**: this PR's own S1-34a payload (rides along, see the 03/08 entry) had added real Bullet franchise names (in the GHL snapshot-list discovery, and separately as `test_identity_key.py`/`identity_key.py` docstring example names), a real HubSpot deal id, a real GHL agency company id, a real GHL snapshot id, and several live Render deploy/service/env-group ids to a public repo - the same class of exposure the round-2 fix's own redaction bullet (03/08, "Removed (PII)") already existed to stop. All redacted to `<redacted>` markers or fictional stand-ins ("Brand Gym Hackney"/"Brand Gym Croydon", already this codebase's established fictional-franchise naming from the multi-site planning doc) across `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/sprint-1-34a-test-results.md`, `apps/api/tests/test_identity_key.py`, `apps/api/tests/test_clients_payload.py`, and `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/identity_key.py`'s own docstring example. Scoped to exactly what THIS PR's diff added (`git diff main...HEAD`) - the pre-existing `docs/CHANGELOG.md:100` entry and the June Render-Blueprint-setup entries predate this branch and are left alone, per the round-2 precedent of redacting going forward rather than rewriting already-published history.
- **Fixed (finding 3)**: `identity_key.normalize_postcode` step 3 joined international-postcode tokens in WHATEVER ORDER they appeared, so "75008 Paris" and "Paris 75008" - the same French business, entered two different ways - produced two different keys and silently failed to match for the live INT account (verified by the reviewer: three distinct keys for one business). Now tokenizes on whitespace, strips each token, and joins them back in SORTED order - order-independent by construction, and since only whole tokens are reordered (each token's own character sequence is untouched) it cannot create a NEW collision between two postcodes that merely share characters. Also anchored `_UK_POSTCODE` on both sides (one-line finding): unanchored, it matched a SUBSTRING of a malformed value ("AB12 3CDE" -> "AB123CD", "E81AAX" -> "E81AA"), minting a confident wrong UK key instead of falling through to the fail-safe.
- **Fixed (finding 4)**: the NULL-identity-key email-fallback path (`allow_link=False`) was DETECTION-ONLY even when phone also corroborated, so a genuine returning client - same business, same `ops@` email, same phone, a signing with a blank `Company.Zip` - still got a GUARANTEED duplicate sub-account: leg A (DB sibling) found the prior row and refused to link it purely because the identity-key path was unavailable, and leg B (GHL lookup) is deliberately subordinate to any leg-A collision so it never got a chance to re-decide either.
  - **First cut (superseded before push, see below)**: simply dropping `allow_link` and reusing the keyed path's bar (name + phone) everywhere. Caught during doc cleanup, not by a test: `_SIBLING_BY_EMAIL_SQL`'s own comment records that round 2 (finding 5) already tried exactly this and reverted it, because that bar leans on the postcode to separate franchises and the postcode is precisely what the email fallback lacks - two sites sharing one `ops@` mailbox and one head-office number would have linked again with no flag.
  - **Actual fix: a new third bar, not a reopened old one.** The email fallback now also requires `identity_key.contact_name_agrees` - the SIGNING CONTACT's full name (`Client.FirstName`/`Client.LastName`, independent of the company record and of the phone/email contact channel) - alongside phone, via a new `require_contact_name` parameter on `_pick_sibling` (`True` only when unkeyed). Two franchise sites sharing one mailbox and one head-office line are still expected to have DIFFERENT people signing their own site (the client's "franchisees have no shared access" answer); the SAME person signing twice, with the same email, name and phone, absent a postcode, is the strongest evidence available that this is one business re-signing. This demands MORE evidence than bars 1+2 alone (closing round 2's gap) while still linking the genuine case round 4 found broken - the keyed path is untouched (`require_contact_name=False`, zero behaviour change, zero regression risk there).
  - `_SIBLING_SELECT` now also fetches `contact_first_name`/`contact_last_name` for candidates. New regression test `test_null_identity_key_email_fallback_flags_when_contact_name_disagrees` is THE test for this: same email, same business name, same phone, DIFFERENT signing contact -> still flags, does not link - proving a future edit that weakens bar 3 back to phone-only would be caught. `test_identity_key.py` gains `TestContactNameAgrees` for the new pure helper directly.
- **Fixed (one-line findings)**: `normalize_phone` accepted `"1234567890"` (the most common filler number in existence) as a corroborating signal - its 9-digit tail is `"234567890"`, not in `_PLACEHOLDER_PHONE_TAILS` (which only held `"123456789"`). Added `_is_sequential_digits`, rejecting any tail that is a run of consecutive digits (mod 10) in either direction - by SHAPE, not by growing a denylist one rotation at a time. Three stale docstrings fixed to match the code they describe: `worker/events.py` claimed the CEL concurrency key falls back to `client_id` (it falls back to `"email:" + email`, verified against a local Inngest dev server); `0013_clients_identity_key.py` still called `address` a corroborating signal alongside phone (round 2 made it phone-only); `ghl/client.py`'s `FakeGhlClient` docstring still said `lookup_result` defaults to `None` (the `_UNSET` sentinel replaced that default two rounds ago).
- **Also updated for consistency (not itself a reviewer finding)**: the 03/08 CHANGELOG "Known, NOT fixed" note (c) claiming the postcode token-order bug was still open, and its note (a) claiming the NULL-key row "always flags rather than linking" - both were made stale by findings 3 and 4 above, corrected in place.
- **Deferred, logged not silently dropped**: (1) `.github/workflows/ci.yml:165-180` - Neon branch creation is `continue-on-error: true` and both the migration step and pytest fall back to `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL` on a Neon blip, so CI can run `alembic downgrade base` against SHARED STAGING on a transient failure, destroying client-identifying data. Reviewer's own call: separate ticket, not blocking this PR. Needs a card. (2) Persisting `hubspot_deal_id`/`hubspot_company_id` (already extracted by this PR) and refusing to auto-link when they differ would close the franchise case more directly than name+postcode+phone alone - reviewer's suggestion, already tracked as open follow-up #3 ("HubSpot id storage") in `project_active_state.md`; not added to this PR's scope.
- **Outstanding, needs verification before merge**: confirm `bullet-cron-staging` is still SUSPENDED (the lead's own merge-checklist item, restated in this review round) - not verified this session (no Render API credentials loaded).
- **Verified**: 609 passed, 0 skipped (Docker Postgres up, schema rebuilt from empty against this branch's migration chain). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean, `make typecheck` clean, `make codegen` no drift. New/updated tests: `test_ghl_client.py` (retriable-status parametrized over both methods); `test_ghl_subaccount.py` (`_pick_sibling`'s email-fallback coverage split into links-when-corroborated, flags-when-phone-disagrees, and the new flags-when-contact-name-disagrees regression test - the original fixture's shared default phone AND contact name meant the first cut's fix was accidentally untested by the existing suite); `test_identity_key.py` (`TestContactNameAgrees`).

### 21/08/2026 - S1-26b/c rebased onto main, and the Inngest check that had never been run now passes

Lead review of the multi-site plan (21/08) set four conditions on this branch. Two were already met, one is done here, one needs him. Corrections to the record first: this branch was NOT "committed but never pushed" - `b8f04f3` was the tip of `origin/feat/s1-26bc-identity-key` with **PR #14 open** against it, and GitHub reported it CONFLICTING, which independently proved the rebase was mandatory rather than housekeeping.

- **Changed (rebase, condition 1)**: rebased the four commits onto `main` at `5c6b9ac`. Four CHANGELOG conflicts, all resolved by keeping both sides - each side checked against `main` first to confirm no entry would be duplicated (none would). **Handler signatures verified after the fact rather than assumed**: git auto-merged `client_record.py` and `ghl_subaccount.py` with no conflict, which is exactly where the risk sat, because a silent keep-ours would have reinstated the retired 2-arg `(ctx, step)` form and with it the silent fan-out failure S1-34b took five review rounds to fix. Grepped all seven handlers: every one is single-arg, and `inngest.Step` appears nowhere in `worker/`. The auto-merge was correct because this branch's edits are in the function BODIES, not the signature lines.
- **Verified (condition 2 - the check the plan required and nobody had run)**: the CEL concurrency key `has(event.data.dedup_key) ? event.data.dedup_key : "email:" + event.data.email` is **accepted by Inngest**. Booted `inngest-cli dev` locally, synced the app (`PUT /api/inngest` -> `{"ok": true, "modified": true}`), then - because S1-34a proved a successful sync is NOT proof of registration - queried the dev server's GraphQL API for what actually landed. `functionCount=7`, and the ternary is stored **verbatim with a computed hash** (`35ykkkq0b43dd`), meaning Inngest parsed the expression rather than storing an opaque string. Registration is all-or-nothing, so seven functions IS the proof. This was the one change on the branch that could have failed every function's registration at once (the S1-26a bug class); it does not.
- **Verified (condition 3 was already satisfied)**: the lead asked for a `preDeployCommand` running `alembic upgrade head` so the deploy-window 500 is closed permanently rather than per-merge. It was already added in round 2 (finding 7) on `bullet-api-staging`. Confirmed it is on the API service ONLY - deliberate, because Render runs the hook per service and two concurrent `alembic upgrade head` calls can collide (alembic takes no lock by default), while the worker's failure mode without it is a retryable Inngest error that self-heals once the migration lands rather than a user-facing 500. Render aborts the deploy if the command fails, so a bad migration leaves the OLD code serving instead of half-migrating. **Note for later: only staging services exist in `render.yaml`; prod will need the same line or the gap reopens there.**
- **Verified (gates, with the DB actually up)**: **598 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed**. The round-2 entry below records 560 at `b8f04f3`; the delta is the rebase pulling in S1-34b's tests from `main`, not new tests here - Docker confirmed running first, because a run that turns the db-marked tests into skips is what hid two rounds of findings on this branch. `ruff check` clean, `ruff format --check` clean (141 files), `make typecheck` clean across `packages/shared` and `apps/dashboard`. Local DB migrated `0012` -> `0013`.
- **Outstanding (condition 4, the lead's)**: he asked for **one more review round after the rebase** - the rebase is not mechanical, `ghl_subaccount.py` carries 207 changed lines on this branch, and round 2's seven findings have not been independently re-verified since. Force-pushed `b8f04f3` -> `80fb34b` with `--force-with-lease`; PR #14 now points at the rebased history and no longer conflicts. Also on that merge's checklist: **confirm `bullet-cron-staging` is still suspended**, since this will be the next push to `main` and a blueprint re-sync may resume it (see the S1-34e card for the fallback).

### 07/08/2026 - S1-34b review round 5: MERGE AFTER MINOR FIXES, and the headline cron fix could not fire

Round 5 on `3369c8a`. **No P0 or P1 survives.** The reviewer confirmed the round-4 deletion was right and, more usefully, confirmed it did not RELOCATE the problem - tracing `capture_exception` to the wire (`BackgroundWorker.submit` uses `put_nowait` on a 100-slot queue and drops rather than blocks; `before_send` runs inside `capture_internal_exceptions()`), and failing to falsify four separate claims. Two proposed P1s were downgraded after measurement. CI 5/5 green (run 31167622092).

- **Fixed (the one to fix before merging - my round-4 "fix" was cosmetic)**: `bullet-cron-staging` has exactly one envVar entry, `fromGroup: bullet-staging-env`, and that group declares `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` but **no `SENTRY_DSN`** - the DSN exists only as a per-service key on `bullet-api-staging`. So `init_sentry` would have returned at its first line forever, and "the one process whose job is recovery reported nothing" was recorded as Fixed while the recovery job still reported nothing. The code comment even said "the cron's own env group"; it has no own group, it shares one. Gave the cron service its own `SENTRY_DSN` / `sync: false` entry - deliberately per-service, because adding it to the shared group would also hand it to `bullet-worker-staging` and could be shadowed on `bullet-api-staging` by its own declared-but-empty key. **Four of the reviewer's lenses landed on this independently.**
- **Fixed (secret leak newly EXPOSED by round 4)**: now that the cron initialises Sentry, its exceptions reach Sentry for the first time - and the Slack call was unguarded to the top. `raise_for_status()` embeds the full `hooks.slack.com/services/T.../B.../<secret>` URL in the message, `ExcepthookIntegration` captures it, and `scrub_event` cannot help because an exception message sits in the STRUCTURAL zone where the denylist deliberately does not apply, and `_SIGNED_URL_RE` matches neither `pandadoc.com` nor a token query param. Read access to Sentry would have become the ability to post into the alerting channel. Replaced with a `SlackPostError` carrying only the status code.
- **Fixed (uncapped exception message regex-walked on the event loop)**: `max_value_length` defaults to `None` in the pinned 2.61.1 so nothing truncates, `_EMAIL_RE` is quadratic on an unbroken `[\w.+-]` run with no `@`, and `ghl/client.py` put the full raw `response.text` into the message. The reviewer MEASURED rather than assumed: a realistic 15.7 KB JSON body costs ~1.07 ms and a 28 KB HTML 502 ~1.32 ms (punctuation breaks the run), but a 20 KB body with one unbroken token costs **~1,911 ms**. Reachable, not typical. Capped twice - `max_value_length=2048` in `init`, and the raw body clipped at source in `GhlError`.
- **Fixed (a test that proved neither property it claimed, and which I recorded as mutation-verified)**: `test_reconcile_pandadoc` stubbed `api_accounts` to `[]`, so `main()` early-returned two lines after `init_sentry` and `_run()` was never reached - there was no reconciliation to be stopped, and moving `asyncio.run(_run())` inside the `try` still passed. Rewritten to configure an account so `_run()` genuinely executes, and re-mutated properly: MOVING the real call inside the `try` now fails it. My earlier mutation added a second call instead of moving the real one, which is why it looked verified.
- **Fixed (the vendoring tripwire passed vacuously)**: `getattr(SentryMiddleware, hook)` resolves through inheritance, so a DELETED override compared the SDK base against itself - exactly the mutation that had just happened to `before_response`. Now asserts `hook in SentryMiddleware.__dict__` first; verified by deleting an override, which now fails.
- **Fixed (docs describing coverage that did not exist)**: the round-4 CHANGELOG claimed tests pinned `send_default_pii` and both scrub hooks. They did not - I dropped that assertion as out-of-scope and left the claim standing. Assertions added. Also corrected `sentry.py`'s reference to `worker/inngest_sentry.py`, a path that does not exist - introduced while fixing a different factual error.
- **Changed (denylist parity)**: `x-inngest-signature` went into the Python list only, while the comment still claimed it "mirrors the dashboard denylist exactly" and `sentry-scrub.ts` calls it a SHARED CONTRACT. The divergence is defensible - the dashboard never sees that header - but it was undocumented. Now stated.
- **Removed (dead scaffolding + comment bloat)**: the `patch.object(..., "flush", ...)` left over from the deleted flush, and the `api_accounts` monkeypatch that patched an attribute the code never reads. `inngest_sentry.py`'s comment-to-code ratio had **regressed to 2.81:1 after being flagged**, with seven sentence fragments byte-identical to this file - one narrative in three places. Collapsed to the two RULES plus a pointer here; the file is 146 lines, down from 217. The PROVENANCE block now enumerates all five divergences instead of claiming none, and the deferred-import comment in the cron - which argued with a real project rule on a rationale that does not hold, since `get_settings` is `lru_cache`d so importing the name reads nothing - is gone.
- **Added**: **S1-34e** (`fabe6a98-ab20-4333-b28e-6ceac746852f`) for the four dead-ended real signings. Round 4 asked for a ticket id and did not get one; this is it. Deliberately records NO client names or document ids - this file is committed and that board is shared, and those are real customers. It is framed as a DATA BACKFILL rather than a replay, because a naive `POST /admin/pandadoc/replay/{id}` would provision duplicate GHL sub-accounts for clients Bullet has since onboarded by hand.
- **Verified**: **469 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed**; ruff + format + typecheck clean. Every new guard mutation-verified, including the two that were previously claimed verified and were not.

### 07/08/2026 - S1-34b review round 4: the flush blocked, so it is gone

Fourth review on `c843371`. All four round-3 P1s confirmed genuinely fixed by the reviewer, who re-verified the core fix independently and **tried and failed to falsify it**. CI 5/5 green (run 31162020018). One blocking item, and it is a deletion.

- **Fixed (P1, blocking - same duplicate-sub-account outcome as round 3, different door)**: bounding the flush to 0.5s stopped it RAISING but not BLOCKING. `before_response` is a SYNC `MiddlewareSync` hook invoked inline (`await transforms.maybe_await(m.before_response())`), so the coroutine never yields, and `sentry_sdk.flush` waits on the **process-wide** transport queue (`BackgroundWorker.flush` -> `_wait_flush` -> `_timed_queue_join` -> `queue.all_tasks_done.wait(...)`), not on this run's event. Under a GHL or R2 outage burst every failing run pays that wait serialised on one transport thread - and the stall does NOT pause httpx timers, so a GHL POST the server ALREADY ACCEPTED can blow its 10s read deadline, raise a transient error and be retried. Nothing is memoised, so the retry re-POSTs the location, and the S1-26d indexing lag means the returning-client check cannot see the orphan. It also stalls `/healthz`, the PandaDoc webhook and the dashboard API on the same dyno.
  **The fix is a deletion**: the flush is gone, and with it the entire `before_response` override and the `_captured` flag that existed only to gate it - plus the two tests that existed only to cover them. On a long-lived Render web service the BackgroundWorker drains continuously, so the flush bought nothing to begin with. One fewer hook that can raise. `sentry_sdk.flush_async` exists if it ever needs revisiting, but "nothing to flush" beats "flush cheaply".
- **Fixed (P2-1, request bodies were reaching Sentry)**: `send_default_pii=False` does NOT gate request bodies on FastAPI - `integrations/fastapi.py` sets `request_info["data"]` unconditionally and `max_request_body_size` defaults to `"medium"` (10 KB). With Inngest failures now captured, that body IS the event payload: for `store_sales_knowledge` a whole `SalesCallSummary` including `notable_quotes[].quote` (verbatim client speech), `.speaker`, `pain_points` and `red_flags`, none of which are denylisted keys. Set `max_request_body_size="never"`. The module docstring asserted the false version of this in TWO places; both corrected.
- **Fixed (P2-2, a replayable credential logged beside the body it authenticates)**: `x-inngest-signature` was in neither `SENSITIVE_HEADERS`, sentry's `DEFAULT_DENYLIST`, nor our `DENYLIST_KEYS`. `_validate_sig` performs no freshness check in 0.5.18 and the body is JCS-canonicalised before the HMAC, so the (signature, body) pair is **replayable against the public `/api/inngest`** by anyone with Sentry read access. Added to `DENYLIST_KEYS` with a test.
- **Fixed (P2-3, the contract guard checked names, not signatures)**: `hasattr` alone would miss an ARITY change within `>=0.5.18,<0.6`, which raises `TypeError` at the SDK's call site - **outside** each hook's own `try/except`, so our guards cannot catch it - and `function.py` converts that into a retriable `CallResult(err)` over an already-successful run. Now compares parameter arity and kinds against the SDK base. Deliberately NOT names: the SDK dispatches positionally, and ours says `output` where the base says `result` because that is what upstream's experimental middleware says.
- **Fixed (P2-4, the secret control had no test)**: `include_local_variables=False` was unasserted, so a silent revert would reopen plaintext secret capture with a fully green suite. Added tests pinning it, `max_request_body_size`, `send_default_pii` and both scrub hooks.
- **Fixed (P2-5, an under-count next to a live secret control)**: the comment claimed SEVEN modules bind `settings = get_settings()` as a frame local. **Fifteen do.** An under-count there reads as a bounded blast radius, which is exactly the wrong impression for a control that exists because the radius is wide.
- **Fixed (operational - the one process whose job is recovery reported nothing)**: `crons/reconcile_pandadoc.main()` never called `init_sentry()`, so a crash in the safety net for missed signings was invisible outside the Render cron log. It now initialises Sentry. Still a no-op until a DSN is set on the cron's own env group.
- **Fixed (docs, and this one mattered most of the small items)**: `test_handler_binds_to_the_sdk_call_shape` claimed to be "strictly stronger than counting positional parameters". **That is false.** `(ctx, step=None)` binds fine but has two positional params, so only the arity test catches it; `(ctx, *, step)` has one positional param, so only the bind test catches it. They are COMPLEMENTARY, and a maintainer trusting that sentence would have deleted the arity test and reopened a real hole. Also corrected: the `sentry.py` claim that `sentry_sdk` "is never imported" (now false - `inngest_sentry.py` imports it at module top and `_inngest.py` imports that unconditionally; only the REGISTRATION is DSN-gated), and a stale "279 passing tests".
- **TRIPWIRE - write this down, because merging alone does not switch observability on**: `render.yaml` declares `SENTRY_DSN` with `sync: false` and it is currently EMPTY, so the middleware is INERT on staging today. That is why this change is materially safe to merge now - the P1 above cannot fire while no DSN is set. It also means **the moment someone pastes the DSN into the Render env group is the moment this module first executes in production**, with no deploy artefact and no CI run behind it. The reviewer raised this in round 3 and it was dropped without comment; recording it here so the next person meets it stated rather than discovered.
- **Deferred (P2-6 and P2-7, at the reviewer's own recommendation)**: they listed seven P2s and advised taking 1 through 5 in this PR. 6 (two guards can pass vacuously - the `args`-sentinel dispatch with a catch-all `else`, and the registry scan asserting one direction only with non-recursive `iter_modules` and no non-empty assertion, while its docstring claims "set-equality") and 7 (bare `Mock()` throughout the middleware tests, so an SDK rename of `ctx.attempt` or `result.error` raises `AttributeError` that the new `try/except` swallows - capture stops entirely and every test still passes) are NOT fixed here. Both are real and both are the same bug class as the P1s; they need their own follow-up rather than being silently dropped.
- **Fixed (`/pre-pr-review` gate, and it caught this PR's own theme being violated)**: the new `init_sentry(settings)` in the reconcile cron was UNGUARDED. `sentry_sdk.init` can raise on a malformed DSN, and before that line existed the cron ran regardless - so a typo'd env var would have silently disabled the recovery job for missed signings. That is precisely the "telemetry must never be able to stop the work" rule the middleware hooks follow, violated from the other end while fixing it. Now wrapped, with a test pinning BOTH properties (Sentry is initialised, and Sentry failing does not stop reconciliation), each mutation-verified.
- **Verified**: **467 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed**; ruff + format + typecheck clean. Mutation-verified every new guard: an arity change on `transform_output` fails the contract test; reverting `include_local_variables`, `max_request_body_size` or the `x-inngest-signature` denylist entry each fails its own test; and un-guarding or removing the cron's `init_sentry` each fails the cron test.

### 06/08/2026 - S1-34b review round 3: the observability tool could corrupt what it observes

Third review on `a4210dc`. The signature fix itself was verified correct by the reviewer against `inngest==0.5.18`; **what blocked the merge was the Sentry middleware bolted on beside it**. Every finding below was reproduced in SDK source before being fixed.

- **Fixed (P1, the serious one - telemetry could DUPLICATE the jobs it watches)**: no middleware hook was wrapped. Confirmed in source that this is not merely lost telemetry - `inngest/_internal/function.py:181-186` returns `CallResult(err)` **over an already-successful `call_res`**, and `errors.py:266-269` makes any non-Inngest exception **retriable**. Since no handler uses `ctx.step.run` nothing is memoised, so the retry re-executes every side effect: the GHL location POST, the R2 upload, the `client.created` emit. With the S1-26d indexing lag that can mint a **duplicate sub-account in Bullet's LIVE agency**. Every hook (and `__init__`) is now wrapped in `try/except Exception` and logs via `client.logger.exception`. **The rule is now explicit in the module docstring: no hook may ever raise.**
- **Fixed (P1, unbounded flush on the hot path)**: `sentry_sdk.flush()` with no argument falls back to `shutdown_timeout` (**2.0s**) and BLOCKING-joins the transport on the event loop shared with the PandaDoc webhook and the dashboard API - on every invocation. Now `flush(timeout=0.5)`, and only when something was actually captured, so the success path costs nothing.
- **Fixed (P1, the one that should sting - the middleware had NEVER executed anywhere)**: it is gated on `sentry_dsn`, which is empty in CI, in local dev, and in the 30/07 end-to-end run, and both tests asserted only class membership in `client.middleware`. Not one line of the module had ever run; **staging would have been its first execution** - the same shape as the bug this ticket exists to fix. Added tests that instantiate `SentryMiddleware(fake_client, None)` and drive all three hooks directly, including cases where `sentry_sdk` itself raises, asserting the run result is untouched and the failure is logged.
- **Fixed (P1, live secret exposure - pre-existing, widened by this PR)**: `include_local_variables` defaults to **True** and `init_sentry()` never disabled it; `Settings` has **no** `SecretStr` fields; and **fifteen** modules bind `settings = get_settings()` as a frame local. So the first captured exception in any of those frames would ship `ghl_agency_api_key`, `inngest_signing_key`, `resend_api_key` and `email_token_secret` to Sentry in plaintext. `scrub_event` could not save us - it matches on KEY names, and these arrive nested inside a `settings` repr. Added `include_local_variables=False`. The `SecretStr` refactor is the structural fix and gets its own ticket.
- **Added (L1 - `on_failure` shares the one-arg contract)**: `function.py:153-158` assigns `handler = self._opts.on_failure`, so an `on_failure` declared `(ctx, step)` passed every guard and would fail at RUNTIME - on the branch that only runs when something is already broken. All three guards now iterate every callable the SDK may invoke, not just `_handler`.
- **Added (L2 - `FUNCTIONS` is hand-maintained)**: a decorated-but-never-appended handler is silently never served - the event fires, nothing runs, no error anywhere, the same failure shape as S1-34a. A scan of the worker package now asserts no decorated function is MISSING from the registry. Note this is one-directional and non-recursive, not set-equality - an earlier version of this bullet overclaimed it; tightening the scan is round-4 P2-6, deferred.
- **Added (L3/L4/L5)**: assert the hook names still exist on `MiddlewareSync` (vendoring turns what would have been a loud `ImportError` into a **silent no-op** if 0.5.19+ renames one); tag `ctx.attempt` so one failing job stops raising ~5 indistinguishable events and a recovered blip stops reading as terminal; record vendoring provenance (upstream path, version, dates) so the next SDK bump is diffable.
- **Changed (L6, docs hygiene)**: the 03/08 hardening block was buried inside the `### 29/07` entry below the 30/07 one - promoted to its own dated entry. The "branch stays local and unpushed" note was false as of 06/08 and is corrected in place rather than deleted, since it records a real decision that was later reversed.
- **Decision (06/08, owner - we did NOT take the suggested PR split)**: the reviewer suggested landing the signature fix now and the middleware separately. Declined, and disclosed here rather than silently: the split was a **Suggestion**, distinct from the four P1 blockers, all of which are fixed as written. **Part 4 of the S1-34b ticket IS "Sentry capture"**, so splitting would close the ticket without meeting its own acceptance criteria; the middleware work turned out to be ~100 lines, mostly tests; and the "ship the P0 today" benefit is largely illusory while staging is deliberately not taking real signings pending the arming decision.
- **Verified (`/pre-pr-review` gate RUN - it found one thing)**: the middleware now holds per-invocation state (`_captured`), which is only safe if the SDK instantiates it per run. Confirmed in source that `MiddlewareManager.add()` does `middleware(self.client, self._raw_request)` and `from_client` is called per incoming request, so the flag cannot leak between concurrent runs. That guarantee was undocumented and untested, so it now has a test pinning it - if a future SDK shares instances, one failing run would make every later successful run pay the flush, permanently.
- **Verified**: **466 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed** (was 456 before this round; the DB tests genuinely run - Docker went down mid-session and produced a 190-skipped run, which was rejected rather than recorded). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` + `make typecheck` clean.
- **Verified (6 mutations, each caught by the intended test)**: removing the `try/except` from `transform_output` fails the no-hook-can-raise guard; restoring the unbounded always-on `flush()` fails the bounded-flush test; dropping the `ctx.attempt` tag fails the tag test; removing a function from `FUNCTIONS` fails the registry scan; and the two pre-existing signature mutations (`(ctx, step)` and `(ctx, *, step)`) still fail their respective guards. A guard that cannot be shown to fail is not a guard.
- **Verified (production shape, not just the test shape)**: booted in CLOUD mode with a signing key and a Sentry DSN, then instantiated `SentryMiddleware` against the REAL `inngest_client` (not a fake) and drove `before_response` - all 7 functions register and the middleware survives. The whole point of P1-3 was that this module had never executed anywhere, so verifying it only through fakes would have repeated the mistake.
- **Deferred (owner, 06/08 - NOT dropped)**: merging does **not** recover the four dead-ended real signings. Their `onboarding_events` rows exist with `processed_at IS NULL`, their runs exhausted retries weeks ago, and the reconcile cron keys on row ABSENCE (`ON CONFLICT (event_type, external_id) DO NOTHING`) so it skips them permanently - the lookback window excludes them anyway. These are EXISTING clients whose data belongs in the dashboard, so it is a data-backfill job, not a fresh onboarding: a naive replay would provision real GHL sub-accounts and possibly fire real emails and Zaps for clients onboarded by hand weeks ago. Needs its own ticket and John's sign-off.

### 03/08/2026 - S1-34b hardening pass: verified against the SDK, not against the claims

**Hardening pass 03/08/2026 (pre-push).** Re-verified against the installed SDK rather than against the original claims, because "the handlers are now callable" is exactly the kind of assertion that is easy to state and easy to get wrong:

- **Verified (every SDK surface the vendored middleware touches actually exists)**: `MiddlewareSync.__init__(client, raw_request)`, and the hooks `before_response` / `transform_input(ctx, function, steps)` / `transform_output(result)`. A mistyped hook name would silently never fire, so this was checked by reflection, not by reading. Also confirmed the SDK invokes all three POSITIONALLY (`m.transform_output(result)`), so our parameter naming cannot break it.
- **Verified (the vendored file was a faithful copy AS AT 03/08)**: a comment-stripped diff against `inngest.experimental.sentry_middleware` showed the executable code IDENTICAL - only docstrings and an `__all__` differed, so nothing had been dropped in the copy. **SUPERSEDED 06/08**: the review-round-3 hardening deliberately DIVERGES from upstream (guarded hooks, bounded conditional flush, attempt tag), because upstream lets a hook raise and a raising hook replaces an already-successful run result. Upstream is therefore no longer a byte-for-byte reference - diff behaviour, not text, and see the provenance block in the module.
- **Verified (`ctx.step` really is available)**: `inngest.Context` is a dataclass with fields `attempt, event, events, group, logger, run_id, step`. The claim that step tooling survives the signature change is true, not assumed.
- **Verified (`middleware=` clobbers nothing)**: the SDK's default middleware list is empty, so registering `SentryMiddleware` adds rather than replaces.
- **Verified (CLOUD mode, which is what staging actually runs)**: booted with `INNGEST_DEV` unset plus a signing key and a Sentry DSN - the app imports, the middleware registers, and all 7 functions serialise. Dev-mode-only verification would have missed a cloud-mode-only failure at deploy time.
- **Verified (all 7 handlers are entered, not just the 4 with tests)**: invoked every registered handler with a stub context and confirmed each reaches its BODY - every failure is a business/config error (fail-closed `NonRetriableError`, or a DB constraint from stub data), none is a call-boundary `TypeError`. Under the S1-34b bug all 7 failed at the boundary.
- **Added (a real gap the arity test missed)**: `test_handler_binds_to_the_sdk_call_shape` uses `Signature.bind` to prove each handler is callable as `handler(ctx)`. Counting positional parameters passes a handler declared `(ctx, *, step)` - one positional arg, still uncallable, still kills the entire fan-out. Only 4 of the 7 handlers were invoked anywhere in the suite; `create_client_record`, `create_ghl_subaccount` and `capture_meet_transcript` had NO call-shape coverage at all.
- **Verified (mutation, both guards)**: reinstating `(ctx, step)` on `store_signed_pdf` fails the arity test with the intended message; a keyword-only `(ctx, *, step)` on `capture_meet_transcript` fails ONLY the new bind test - confirming the gap above was real and is now closed.
- **Verified (lockfile + rename completeness)**: `uv lock --check` consistent, resolved `inngest==0.5.18`; no surviving reference to the retired two-argument contract anywhere in `src` or `tests`; `render.yaml` byte-identical to `main`, matching the owner's decision to keep `bullet-worker-staging` for now.

Branch `feat/s1-34b-inngest-handler-signatures`, cut from `main` (deliberately NOT stacked on the open S1-26b/c PR, so this P0 can merge first).

### 30/07/2026 - S1-34a CHAIN 1 PASSES END TO END (first time ever) - proven locally against real services

Verified the S1-34b fix before pushing anything, by running the whole chain **locally** rather than deploying: local `uvicorn` + a local **Inngest dev server** (`npx inngest-cli dev`, no Docker needed - the docker-compose route is unavailable on this machine), pointed at the **staging Neon DB** and the **real** PandaDoc / GHL / R2 credentials. This keeps `main` untouched and needs no Render change, while exercising the identical code path.

- **Verified (Chain 1 END TO END, the S1-34a Step 2 gate - PASS)**: fired `pandadoc.signed` for the real completed test document `3fochaKNzfg5j9HHAMErEQ` ("Test Gym Ltd") into the local dev server. The full fan-out ran and **every step succeeded on the first attempt with 0 retries**: `onboarding_events.processed_at` backfilled; `clients` row created (business_name `Test Gym Ltd`, email `team@izzyagents.ai`, legal_entity `Izzyagents`, `current_step=signed`); **GHL sub-account `ZmNeBXTJl7LrEIM93Lkv` created**; **signed PDF stored in R2**; both `platform_actions` rows `success` with real `external_id`s. This is the first time any Inngest function has completed successfully in the project's history.
- **Verified (external state, not just our DB's claims)**: `GET /locations/ZmNeBXTJl7LrEIM93Lkv` returned the live location with `companyId=bSRotoCaFhV3OaASsF5L` **and `snapshotId=XJnHQZZCXH3Cw9hI0z0k`** - so **snapshot cloning works**, which was specifically unproven (the 21/07 smoke created a location WITHOUT a snapshot). The R2 object `signed-agreements/<client_id>/<doc_id>.pdf` is a real **3,921,462-byte** file whose first bytes are `%PDF-`, so the PandaDoc PDF **download** path (also never previously exercised) works too.
- **Verified (S1-34a Step 3 idempotency - PASS)**: replaying the same event produced **no** duplicate client, action, document or GHL location, and the client kept the same `ghl_subaccount_id`. Counts stayed at 1/2/1.
- **Discovery (GHL location search has INDEXING LAG - narrows a real S1-26 gap)**: `find_location_by_email` returned `None` for the sub-account we had just created, even though `GET` by id confirmed it existed with that exact email. Initially this looked like a broken lookup, but searching a long-established location's email returns 1 result - so the **email filter works; newly-created locations are simply not searchable for some minutes**. This closes S1-34a retest item #3 (previously only the empty-result envelope had been proven) and clears the `CONFIRM PRE-PROD` marker on `ghl/client.py`'s `find_location_by_email` with a caveat rather than a clean pass. **Consequence:** the GHL lookup is check 2/2 of S1-26's returning-client guard and is the backstop for S1-25's at-least-once window - a create that succeeds but whose response is lost, retried seconds later, will NOT find the orphaned location and could create a duplicate. The window is narrow (the DB-sibling check 1/2 and the `platform_actions` idempotency key both still apply, and the lookup works fine for genuinely returning clients signing later), but it is real and previously assumed closed. Follow-up ticket raised.
- **Cleanup**: the throwaway GHL sub-account was **deleted** and confirmed gone (delete -> 200, follow-up `GET` -> 403), so nothing test-related remains in Bullet's live agency. The local `uvicorn` + dev server were shut down. NOTE: the staging Neon rows (1 `clients`, 2 `platform_actions`, 1 `documents`) and the R2 object were deliberately LEFT in place as evidence of the passing test; the client row's `ghl_subaccount_id` now points at a deleted location, so purge these before S1-35 acceptance.
- **Note (superseded)**: this entry originally recorded that the S1-34b branch would stay local and unpushed pending the S1-26b/c PR. That is no longer true - the owner reversed the order on 06/08 so S1-34b ships FIRST (it is cut from `main`, carries none of the S1-34a payload, and is the gate for staging running anything unattended). Pushed as `a4210dc`.

### 29/07/2026 - S1-34b: Inngest handler signatures fixed - the fan-out is callable for the first time (code-complete)

- **Fixed (P0, Part 1)**: dropped the unused `step` parameter from all **7** Inngest handlers (`create_client_record`, `create_ghl_subaccount`, `store_signed_pdf`, `capture_meet_transcript`, `summarise_sales_call`, `store_sales_knowledge`, `noop_function`), so they match the 0.5.x contract. Confirmed from the SDK source that the alias is `FunctionHandlerAsync = Callable[[Context], Awaitable[T]]` (exactly one argument) and that `Context` is a dataclass carrying a `step` field, so **step tooling remains available as `ctx.step`** - nothing is lost. Signature-only change; no function body referenced `step`.
- **Changed (P0, Part 2)**: pinned `inngest>=0.5.18,<0.6` (was `>=0.4,<1.0`, the range that let a breaking handler-API change install silently under code written for 0.4). Lockfile regenerated; resolved version unchanged at 0.5.18.
- **Discovery (Part 3 - the tests encoded the WRONG contract, they did not merely miss it)**: three test files DID drive the real invocation path, but called `_handler(ctx, None)` - faithfully matching the retired 0.4 two-argument API. So the suite gave **false confidence**: it exercised a calling convention the SDK had stopped using, and passed. Corrected the three call sites (`test_signed_pdf.py`, `test_sales_knowledge.py`, `test_sales_summary.py`) to the single-argument form. This is the more precise root cause of the blind spot than "no test covered it".
- **Added (Part 3)**: `tests/test_inngest_handlers.py` - a registry-wide guard asserting every `FUNCTIONS` entry's handler takes exactly one positional argument and is async, plus a real invocation of `noop_function._handler(ctx)`, plus a non-empty-registry guard so the parametrised test can never pass vacuously. **Verified the guard actually fails on the bug**: temporarily reinstating `(ctx, step)` on `create_client_record` produced `AssertionError: create-client-record handler takes 2 positional args (['ctx', 'step']); the Inngest 0.5.x contract is exactly 1 (ctx)`, then the fix was restored.
- **Added (P1, Part 4)**: vendored the SDK's experimental Sentry middleware into `observability/inngest_sentry.py` (its own docstring recommends copying rather than importing `inngest.experimental`, so an SDK patch cannot silently change our error reporting) and registered it on the client. It hooks `transform_output` -> `capture_exception`, tags each event with `inngest.function.id` / `run.id` / `event.name` / `app.id`, and flushes on `before_response`. Registration is gated on `sentry_dsn` so local dev / CI do not log "Sentry SDK is not initialized" on every run, mirroring `init_sentry()`'s no-op-without-DSN posture. No import cycle: the middleware imports only `inngest` + `sentry_sdk`, keeping `worker/_inngest.py` free of `bullet_api.worker` imports.
- **Decision (P2, Part 5 DEFERRED by owner 29/07)**: the `bullet-worker-staging` removal was implemented in `render.yaml` and then **reverted** - the owner chose to keep the service for now and retire it later, so `render.yaml` is left byte-identical to `main` and the manifest continues to match the live environment. The finding stands and is recorded here for whoever picks it up: the service runs the S1-19 stub (log two lines, `sleep` forever) and can NEVER run a job, because Inngest is **HTTP-push** - Inngest Cloud invokes functions by POSTing to `bullet-api-staging /api/inngest`, and a Render background worker has no HTTP listener. It is therefore an idle, billable dyno whose name actively misleads during an incident (it was checked first during this very investigation). Removing it cannot affect the fan-out. If compute isolation is ever wanted the correct shape is a second **web** service serving only the Inngest endpoint, not a background worker. Render does not auto-delete a service dropped from a blueprint, so retiring it is a manual dashboard action.
- **Verified (SDK surface fully checked, no second break hiding behind the first)**: audited every Inngest API the code touches against 0.5.18 before trusting the fix - handlers use only `ctx.event.data` and `ctx.run_id` (both valid fields), and `Concurrency`, `Throttle`, `TriggerEvent`, `Event`, `Context`, `Inngest` and `NonRetriableError(message)` all exist with compatible signatures. So the arity break was the ONLY 0.4 -> 0.5 incompatibility in our usage.
- **Verified (`/pre-pr-review` gate PASSED, 2 findings fixed + 1 self-correction)**: (1) **lens 6 rename completeness** - the `render.yaml` header comment still listed `bullet-worker-staging` among the declared services after its block was removed, so the manifest contradicted itself; rewritten to record the removal and why. (2) **lens 7 untested branch** - `_make_client()` gained a `if settings.sentry_dsn` conditional with no coverage; added both-way tests asserting `SentryMiddleware` IS registered with a DSN and is NOT without one. (3) **self-correction on lens 1 concurrency** - the middleware docstring originally claimed per-request middleware instantiation prevents tag leakage between concurrent runs, which is **wrong**: isolation actually comes from Sentry's ASGI integration wrapping each request in `isolation_scope()` (verified in `sentry_sdk 2.61.1`, `integrations/asgi.py:222`). Comment corrected, including the caveat that the guarantee holds only while functions are driven over HTTP.
- **Verified (local, re-run 03/08 with Docker Postgres up so the DB tests GENUINELY RUN)**: full suite **456 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed**. The original figure recorded here was "259 passed, 190 skipped" - corrected, because 190 skipped is exactly the condition that let two rounds of review findings survive on the sibling S1-26b/c branch, and a count nobody can reproduce is worse than no count. `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` clean; app boots with all 7 functions registered (`noop-test`, `create-client-record`, `create-ghl-subaccount`, `store-signed-pdf`, `capture-meet-transcript`, `summarise-sales-call`, `store-sales-knowledge`). Live end-to-end proof still pending deploy: staging auto-deploys from `main`, so the fix must merge before the Test Gym Ltd signing can be re-driven.

### 29/07/2026 - S1-34b created: the fan-out has NEVER executed (Inngest 0.5 handler-signature break)

Follow-on to the S1-34a live test below. The second bug was identified exactly and a combined infra ticket was raised: **S1-34b** (`d975b97c-cd9e-4e73-ab97-1cbf5b55a943`, Sprint 1 list).

- **Discovery (P0, the blocker - every Inngest handler is uncallable)**: all **7** functions are declared `async def fn(ctx: inngest.Context, step: inngest.Step)` (the 0.3/0.4 two-argument API), but the installed **`inngest==0.5.18`** invokes handlers with a **single `ctx`**. Pulled the failed run from the Inngest REST API (`run_id 01KYPAGA2M3GTNYB9X7WEHGKKP`, status Failed): `TypeError: create_client_record() missing 1 required positional argument: 'step'`. So **no Inngest function has ever executed successfully in any environment**, and every Sprint 1 fan-out ticket (S1-25/25a/25b/26/27/29/30) is unverified end to end. Ruled out by standalone verification against staging: PandaDoc fetch, `extract_client_fields` (all values correct), the `clients` UPSERT and the `onboarding_events` backfill all work. The test document is correct and does NOT need re-signing.
- **Discovery (why it was invisible)**: the pin `inngest>=0.4,<1.0` allowed a breaking handler-API change to install silently, AND no test exercises the real invocation path - every test calls the pure `*_core` helper or asserts declarations via `fn.get_config("")`, so 279 green tests caught none of it.
- **Verified (Sentry works; the gap is worker capture only)**: sent a live test event to the staging DSN and it landed in the Sentry project, so `init_sentry` + DSN + project are correct. The reason no errors ever appeared is that the **Inngest SDK catches handler exceptions internally** and returns them as a JSON body to Inngest Cloud, so they never bubble up the ASGI stack where Sentry's FastAPI integration hooks. Fix is to vendor the SDK's `SentryMiddleware` (its own docstring recommends copying it into source rather than importing `inngest.experimental`) and register it on the client; confirmed compatible because the middleware manager is typed `list[Middleware | MiddlewareSync]`.
- **Discovery (`bullet-worker-staging` is a reserved dyno that never got a purpose)**: it still runs the S1-19 stub (log two lines, sleep forever). `render.yaml` documents this as deliberate ("no separate TCP server is needed here... kept so Render keeps a long-running dyno available for future Inngest SDK worker mode"). The architecture is correct - Inngest is **HTTP-push**, so Inngest Cloud invokes functions by calling `/api/inngest` on the **API** service, and a background worker with no HTTP listener can never receive those calls.
- **Decision (owner, 29/07)**: the placeholder is **in scope for S1-34b** - it cannot stay a stub. Recommended resolution is to remove the service and its `render.yaml` block, leaving a comment recording why no worker dyno is needed; the alternative (convert to a `web` service serving only the Inngest endpoint, for compute isolation) is not justified at Bullet's signing volume. Deleting a Render service is destructive, so it needs explicit sign-off at implementation time.
