- **Decision (owner, 29/07)**: automating the Inngest app sync is **out of scope** - a manual `PUT /api/inngest` is accepted for now. Revisit as a post-deploy step (relates to S1-34) if another environment silently breaks the same way.
- **Discovery (infra drift)**: `render.yaml` defines **staging services only** (no prod services exist yet), and a stale `bullet-dashboard-staging` static_site is live alongside the real `bullet-dashboard-staging-web` web service, so live naming has drifted from the manifest. Reconcile in S1-34b.

### 29/07/2026 - S1-34a LIVE TEST: real ROOT CAUSE of the fan-out stall found (Inngest never synced) + a 2nd bug

Ran the first true end-to-end Chain 1 test on staging: lead cleared a throwaway document, so a real "Test Gym Ltd" doc (`3fochaKNzfg5j9HHAMErEQ`, UK, `team@izzyagents.ai`, `E8 1AA`, GBP 850, no HubSpot deal linked) was signed to completed. Findings materially change the 28/07 diagnosis.

- **Discovery (THE root cause - staging app was NEVER synced to Inngest Cloud)**: the webhook received + HMAC-verified + persisted the event (`onboarding_events` +1), but the fan-out never ran - because the staging app was not registered with Inngest Cloud. Render API logs showed **zero** `POST /api/inngest?fnId=...` calls (that is how Inngest invokes functions). This, NOT the sandbox/production PandaDoc key, is why all 4 earlier real signings AND this test dead-ended at `processed_at IS NULL`. The 28/07 "sandbox-key 404 -> dead-letter" hypothesis was WRONG (the functions were never invoked at all). Fixed by triggering a sync: `PUT https://bullet-api-staging.onrender.com/api/inngest` -> `{"ok": true, "modified": true}` (the `modified:true` proves it was unregistered); Inngest immediately began invoking the function.
- **Discovery (bullet-worker-staging is a placeholder)**: the `bullet-worker-staging` Render service still runs the S1-19 placeholder command ("bullet-worker-staging placeholder - replace in S1-19", sleeps forever). It does NOT run the Inngest functions - those are served by the **API** service (`inngest.fast_api.serve` in `main.py`) and invoked by Inngest Cloud over HTTP. So the placeholder is not fatal to the fan-out, but the "worker" service is dead weight and the S1-19 worker wiring was never completed. Cleanup/decision needed (drop the service, or repoint it).
- **Discovery (2nd bug - `create_client_record` 500s immediately)**: after the sync, Inngest now calls `bullet-api-create-client-record` but it returns **500** in ~3-13ms and retries. That timing is far too fast for the PandaDoc fetch (~500ms) or any DB work, so the error is at the Inngest invocation layer, BEFORE the app's I/O. Verified independently that fetch + `extract_client_fields` (email/company/postcode/legal-entity/fee all correct) + the client UPSERT + the `onboarding_events` backfill all succeed standalone against staging - so it is not the document, the key, extraction, or the SQL. The app sends exceptions to Sentry (not stdout), so the traceback needs the Sentry (or Inngest Cloud) run detail to pin down. Under investigation.
- **Note (test method)**: the completed test event was emitted before the sync, so it did not auto-process; re-emitted `pandadoc.signed` directly to Inngest to retrigger the (now-invoked) fan-out. The test document is correct and does NOT need re-signing.
### 03/08/2026 - S1-26b/c review round 2: the legs contradicted each other

> **BRANCH TOPOLOGY - READ BEFORE REVIEWING THE DIFF.** `feat/s1-26bc-identity-key` was cut from `test/s1-34a-live-integration`, **not from `main`**, so it inherits commit `ee3cbe8` and this PR therefore also lands the **S1-34a** payload: `apps/api/scripts/{ghl_get_company_id,smoke_ghl_subaccount,smoke_summary_embeddings}.py` plus `docs/sprint-1-34a-test-{plan,results}.md` and their CHANGELOG block. That commit touches **ZERO shipped source** - no `apps/api/src`, no `apps/dashboard/src`, no `packages/` - it is smoke scripts and documents only, so it carries no production-code risk. It rides along because of how the branch was created, not because S1-34a work was mixed into this ticket.
>
> Consequences, so nothing is a surprise later: (1) **S1-34a needs no separate PR** - `test/s1-34a-live-integration`'s tip IS `ee3cbe8`, already an ancestor here, so a PR from it would show an empty diff; delete that remote branch after this merges. (2) **This does NOT close the S1-34a ticket.** What lands is only its artefact; the actual live verification is still outstanding - re-running Chain 1 on staging once S1-34b deploys (it has so far only ever been proven from a laptop), Meet capture pending the domain-wide-delegation authorisation in Bullet's Google Admin, and purging the staging test rows before S1-35 acceptance. Those produce CHANGELOG entries, not a diff.

A second review on `e8c4490` returned 7 blocking findings. **All four testable ones were reproduced in code before being fixed**, not taken on faith.

**Why there was a round 2 - the pattern, not the bugs.** Round 1 was fixed finding-by-finding and each guard was mutation-tested IN ISOLATION, so every fix looked proven. Nothing re-derived the COMPOSED flow afterwards. So when the corroboration bar was added to the DB leg, nobody re-checked whether the GHL leg could still rescue what the DB leg refused - it could, and on that exact path its guard was worthless, because rows sharing an `identity_key` share the postcode BY CONSTRUCTION, collapsing the check to a name test bar 1 had already passed. Reproduced as `flagged=True, ghl_id=loc_studio_one`: flagged as a duplicate AND merged. Worse, the round-1 lesson was not applied to the round-1 fix: the new franchisee test used the SAME dud-fake pattern (`FakeGhlClient` with no `lookup_result`) that round 1 had just called out, and an audit found **27 tests** built the fake that way, so the entire returning-client email leg was invisible to the suite.

- **Fixed (F1)**: the GHL leg is now SUBORDINATE to the DB leg - a collision this run skips GHL reuse entirely and the lookup does not even fire. Verified by removing the subordination, which fails 3 tests including the franchisee case.
- **Fixed (F2)**: the corroborating signal was not independent of the key. `Company.Address` and `Company.Zip` are the SAME HubSpot company record, so address agreed exactly when the key agreed - including in the franchisee case the bar existed to block. Corroboration is now the PHONE (`Client.Phone`, the signing contact) only; `clients.address` is still persisted for audit but does not vote. **Every docstring now states that this NARROWS the franchisee case to "same brand, same head-office postcode, same signing contact" rather than preventing it** - a franchisee signing both studios personally still auto-links, and no comment claims otherwise.
- **Fixed (F3)**: `FakeGhlClient.lookup_result` now defaults to a sentinel that RAISES, so a test cannot silently skip the reuse leg; 25 call sites declare their intent explicitly and the franchisee test arms both legs. This kills the CLASS, not the instance - round 1 fixed one dud test and the pattern survived.
- **Changed (F4)**: three docstrings claimed the at-least-once duplicate-create window was closed while the code said otherwise. The window is OPEN - `/locations/search` is eventually consistent, so a create whose response was lost cannot see its own orphan - and is owned by **S1-26d** (card `2e36827c-…`, raised 30/07 during the Chain-1 test).
- **Fixed (F5)**: the NULL-identity-key email fallback DETECTS and flags, never merges. Linking there conflated franchises, because the guard leans on the postcode to separate them and the postcode is exactly what is absent.
- **Fixed (F6)**: `names_materially_diverge(None, None)` was False, so two unidentifiable signings counted as name agreement - contradicting `identity_name`'s guarantee. An empty stem on either side is now divergence.
- **Fixed (F7)**: the deploy window could lose signings PERMANENTLY and SILENTLY - the clients INSERT raises `UndefinedColumn` before `begin_action`, so no `platform_actions` row exists, retries burn into dead-letter, and `reconcile_pandadoc` cannot re-emit (its `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` sees the event row). Added `preDeployCommand: uv run alembic upgrade head` to `render.yaml`, and `ProgrammingError` now stays RETRIABLE and logs loudly rather than dead-lettering.
- **Fixed (P2s)**: the CEL concurrency fallback is now `"email:" + event.data.email` rather than `client_id` (a per-row private bucket serialises nothing) - **verified live**: registration returned all 7 functions and both event shapes, with and without `dedup_key`, scheduled runs. 408 joins 429 as retriable so an agency-key blip cannot terminally fail every in-flight signing. `possible_duplicate` now CLEARS on a corroborated link instead of saturating the board before S1-26e lands. The flag write between the committed `in_progress` row and its terminal state is now guarded, closing the zombie the comment beside it reasoned about. Postcode filler is rejected by SHAPE (single repeated character, no digit at all) rather than by a blocklist that "TBA" and "99999" walked past. Phone filler likewise. `schemas.py` no longer claims exactly one candidate field is populated - both can be.
- **Fixed (test quality)**: the surviving-key test discarded its emitter, leaving the "emit the SURVIVING key" line unpinned; and nothing asserted `clients.address` persisted, so dropping it from the INSERT stayed green while degrading every auto-link to a flag. Both pinned, both mutation-verified.
- **Changed (the guard that should stop a round 3)**: `conftest.py` now FAILS the run when the database is unreachable and `CI` is set, instead of converting 223 tests into skips and reporting green. That skipping is precisely how both rounds of findings survived - S1-26b/c was reviewed at "279 passed / 193 skipped", and the bugs lived in the 193. Verified both ways: exit 4 with a clear message under `CI=1`, ordinary skips locally.
- **Fixed (P2, auth blips)**: 401 and 403 join 408/429 as RETRIABLE. Dead-lettering them meant one rotated-or-briefly-unavailable agency key TERMINALLY failed every signing in flight, each then needing a human to re-drive. A genuinely wrong key still dead-letters, just via Inngest's retry budget instead of instantly - a few minutes of backoff buys back the whole blip case.
- **Removed (PII)**: the 28/07 entry named four real Bullet clients and their PandaDoc document ids. This file is committed to a GitHub repository and those customers did not consent to appearing in our engineering history, so the names and ids are redacted; they remain recoverable from `onboarding_events` and the PandaDoc console for anyone who needs them. Redacted going forward on this branch rather than by rewriting published history.
- **Changed (docs matched to reality)**: `docs/sprint-1-34a-test-plan.md` still asserted "PandaDoc: SANDBOX accounts" and `docs/sprint-1-34a-test-results.md` still listed the `R2_*` and Google vars as "not yet set" - both contradicted by CHANGELOG entries in the same commit. Each now carries a dated SUPERSEDED note in place rather than being silently edited, because they are point-in-time test records and rewriting them would destroy the audit trail they exist for.
- **Known, NOT fixed (logged rather than quietly dropped)**: (a) the identity-keyed and email-keyed `dedup_key` tiers are disjoint buckets while the sibling scan crosses them, so one business whose first document carries `Company.Zip` and whose second does not can run both concurrently - **narrowed by the F5 change**. **UPDATE 24/08 (review round 4, finding 4): the NULL-key row no longer "always flags rather than linking" - it now links when phone also corroborates**, so this residual only bites when phone does NOT corroborate either (two sub-accounts, one flagged - visible, not silent). (b) The GHL-adopt path sets no `parent_client_id`, so that reuse has no dashboard trace; it now only fires when the DB leg found no collision at all, and surfacing it belongs with **S1-26e**. (c) ~~`normalize_postcode`'s town-extraction is UK-only, so "75008", "75008 Paris" and "Paris, 75008" still give one INT business three keys.~~ **FIXED 24/08 (review round 4, finding 3)**: step 3 now tokenizes on whitespace and joins the tokens back in sorted order, so "75008 Paris" and "Paris 75008" both normalize to "75008PARIS" - order-insensitive by construction. See `identity_key.normalize_postcode`.
- **Removed (`/pre-pr-review` findings)**: `normalize_address` and its tests, and the `address` column from both the client and sibling SELECTs. Once corroboration went phone-only nothing read any of it, so it was an exported, tested helper with no caller - which reads as load-bearing to the next person. `clients.address` is still PERSISTED (audit, and whoever resolves a duplicate flag), it is simply no longer fetched by a decision path that ignores it.
- **Verified**: **560 passed, 0 skipped**. Mutation testing now covers the CROSS-LEG interaction (removing the subordination fails 3 tests), not just each guard alone - the gap that let round 2 happen. ruff + format + typecheck clean.


### 03/08/2026 - S1-26b/c review fixes: the franchise separation now holds END TO END

PR review on `c05e2ee` returned 9 blocking findings plus 4 tests that cannot fail. Every one was reproduced against the diff before being fixed. The headline: **only HALF the returning-client check had been re-keyed** - the DB sibling lookup used the new identity key, but the GHL backstop ~130 lines later still reused any location `find_location_by_email` returned, so the franchise separation the ticket exists to deliver was undone immediately after it was made.

**TWO PLACES THIS DIFF DOES NOT TRANSCRIBE THE REVIEW.** Nothing was skipped and nothing was replaced with something weaker, but two fixes interpret rather than copy, and both are recorded here so a reader (or the reviewer) meets them stated rather than discovered:

1. **Finding 1 was EXCEEDED, because the fix as written would not have worked.** The review asked to "only accept a GHL email hit when the returned location's name does not materially diverge from `client.business_name`". A name guard alone does not fix the case the finding is about: "Brand Gym" Hackney and "Brand Gym" Croydon have IDENTICAL names, so the guard passes and Croydon still reuses Hackney's sub-account. The postcode is the only thing that separates them, so the check corroborates on name AND postcode, and `postalCode` is now sent on create to make that possible.
2. **Finding 3 was DEVIATED FROM, on the international account.** The review asked to "return `None` for anything malformed and denylist common placeholders" after validating a UK postcode. Applied strictly that returns `None` for `75008` or `D02 X285`, which would disable returning-client matching for EVERY client on the INT PandaDoc account - which is live. So the rule is UK-regex-first, then the placeholder denylist and a minimum length, then keep whatever remains. This is the one place the diff knowingly does something other than what the review says; if the reviewer prefers the strict reading, it is a one-line change and INT clients simply fall back to email dedup.

- **Fixed (P1, the core defect)**: the GHL lookup no longer reuses a hit on the strength of the email. Both returning-client checks now demand CORROBORATION, because both lookup keys are lossy: the identity key truncates the name to 6 chars, and email is shared across a brand's franchises. A DB sibling is accepted only when the FULL normalized names agree; a GHL hit only when the location's name AND postcode both agree. **Note a pure name guard would NOT have fixed this** - "Brand Gym" Hackney and "Brand Gym" Croydon have identical names, so only the postcode separates them, which is why `postalCode` is now sent on create (below). This EXCEEDS finding 1 as written; see deviation 1 at the top of this entry.
- **Changed**: `_build_location_payload` sends `postalCode`, making the sub-accounts we create SELF-IDENTIFYING. That is what gives a later signing a second signal to corroborate against, and what keeps S1-25's at-least-once backstop working - a location we created and then lost the response for carries both the name and the postcode we sent, so a retry recognises its own orphan instead of duplicating it.
- **Decision (three-way outcome, not two)**: a GHL hit resolves as same-business (reuse), different-business (own sub-account), or UNDECIDABLE (own sub-account + `possible_duplicate` flag naming the suspected location). Undecidable means either side lacks a postcode - which is every one of Bullet's ~100 legacy sub-accounts. **The trade-off was taken deliberately**: a genuinely returning client with a legacy location now gets a second sub-account plus a flag rather than a silent reuse. Reusing the wrong location is the only unrecoverable outcome (the client's assets, contacts and workflows land inside another client's account), whereas a spare empty sub-account is visible in the dashboard and deletes cleanly - proven twice, 21/07 and 30/07.
- **Decision (flag ambiguity, not every non-reuse)**: the first implementation flagged EVERY uncorroborated hit. A new test caught that a postcode MISMATCH is a confident "different site", not an ambiguity - flagging it would have put a review badge on every franchise signing, and a flag that fires on the normal path is one the team stops reading. Only inconclusive evidence now raises the flag.
- **Fixed (P1 finding 4, and a CORRECTION to an earlier call in this same entry)**: auto-linking on name + postcode alone is gone. A DB sibling link now requires a SECOND corroborating signal - phone or address line - present on both rows and matching; when only name and postcode agree, the signing is flagged and provisioned its own sub-account. This is the reviewer's fix implemented as written. **SUPERSEDED by review round 2 (see the entry above): the address does NOT corroborate anything**, because it comes from the same HubSpot company record as the postcode and therefore agrees exactly when the key already does. Corroboration is the phone alone.

  **Why this reversed mid-ticket**: the earlier instruction "name + postcode is fine, John will not answer" was given two days before this review, by the SAME person who then wrote the review. The review is the later word and supersedes it, so the interim fix they specified is what ships. The intermediate position - keep auto-link, rely on dashboard visibility to catch a bad merge - was wrong and has been removed; visibility is a backstop for what the code cannot decide, not a substitute for a check the code can make. Logged because the reversal is the useful part: an older verbal steer does not outrank a written review from the same reviewer.

  Concretely: `Company.Zip` is the COMPANY postcode, not the studio's, so a franchisee running two studios who enters only the brand ("F45 Training", "BFT") plus their head-office postcode produced an identical key AND identical normalized names - and studio 2 was silently linked into studio 1's sub-account. There is now a regression test for exactly that scenario.
- **Added**: `clients.address` on migration 0013. `Company.Address` was extracted and then discarded, like `postal_code` before it; it is persisted now because it is one of the two signals the corroboration bar accepts, so a client whose phone changed can still link on unchanged premises.
- **Added**: `normalize_phone` (digits, compared on the last 9 so "+44 7700 900123" / "07700 900123" / "+447700900123" agree - the same business re-typing its own number must not read as a different one) and `normalize_address` (same fold/lowercase/alphanumeric treatment as names; deliberately does NOT expand "St" -> "Street", because failing to corroborate flags for review rather than merging).
- **Fixed (P1)**: the sibling query judged only the EARLIEST row at a key (`ORDER BY created_at ASC LIMIT 1`), so a genuine returning client could be permanently invisible behind an unrelated business that reached the shared key first - repeating on every future signing for that business. It now fetches all candidates (bounded at 50, with the cap logged rather than silently truncating) and picks the earliest whose full normalized name matches.
- **Fixed (P1)**: a NULL `identity_key` had lost DB-side dedup entirely - email was no longer a sibling key at all, which is a regression against S1-26 rather than a new safeguard. The sibling query now falls back to email when the key is NULL, under the same name-divergence guard.
- **Fixed (P1)**: `dedup_key` fell straight through to the unique `client_id` for NULL-key clients, giving each its own concurrency bucket and reopening the concurrent double-create the old `event.data.email` key had closed. It now falls back through `email:<lowercased>` first.
- **Fixed (P1)**: `client.created` events already QUEUED at deploy carry no `dedup_key` and would all collide in one null bucket. The concurrency key is now the CEL ternary `has(event.data.dedup_key) ? event.data.dedup_key : event.data.client_id`. **CEL has no `??` and Inngest does not document one**, and a rejected expression fails the ALL-OR-NOTHING registration for every function (the S1-26a outage), so this was verified live rather than assumed - see Verified below.
- **Fixed (P1)**: `normalize_postcode` did no validation. `"London E8 1AA"` keyed differently from `"E8 1AA"` (one business keying two ways), and `"N/A"` survived as `"NA"` - a real-looking key every placeholder-using client would SHARE. Now: a UK postcode found anywhere in the string wins, otherwise a placeholder denylist plus a minimum length reject the junk, otherwise the stripped form is kept so INT clients still key (`75008`, `D02X285`). That last step DEVIATES from finding 3's "return `None` for anything malformed"; see deviation 2 at the top of this entry for why and how to revert it.
- **Fixed (P1)**: `possible_duplicate` had no dashboard surface at all - `git grep` over `apps/dashboard` returned zero hits, so the entire dedup guard was inert as a human-review mechanism (its only output was a `log.warning`). Added a "Possible duplicate" badge on `/clients`, a notice on `/clients/[id]` naming the candidate, and a "Returning client" line whenever `parent_client_id` is set - so an AUTO-LINK is visible too, since that is a merge decision the automation makes on its own and this is the only place a human would notice a wrong one.
- **Added**: `possible_duplicate_ghl_id` on migration 0013 (amended in place - 0013 is not on `main`, so no 0014 is needed), recording WHICH sub-account a flag suspects. Without it the reviewer's "the flag is inert" point survives even with a badge: someone would have to hunt the agency for the candidate.
- **Fixed (P2)**: the `ON CONFLICT` SET was a no-op, so `identity_key`/`postal_code` were written on first insert only. A pre-0013 row - or one whose first extraction could not compute a key - kept a NULL key forever while the re-emitted event carried a real `dedup_key`. Now `COALESCE`-filled on conflict (fill-if-NULL, so a hand-corrected value is not clobbered), and the emitted `dedup_key` reads the SURVIVING key from `RETURNING`, not the freshly-computed one.
- **Fixed (P2)**: the key was computed from `business_name` alone though the line above established a `legal_entity` fallback, so a document carrying only the signed legal-trading-name silently opted out of matching. New `identity_name()` helper is now the single source of that expression, used by the key, the divergence guard and the GHL location name. It rejects `LEGAL_ENTITY_PLACEHOLDER`, which is a marker rather than a name - keying on it would unite every unidentifiable signing.
- **Fixed (P2)**: ASCII-only tokenizing meant "Café Gym" and "Cafe Gym" produced different keys (the accented letter was silently deleted, not folded). Now NFKD-folded. Relevant with the INT account live.
- **Changed (P2)**: `names_materially_diverge` keeps its strict equality and the docstring was corrected to say so. The leniency lives in the key's 6-char truncation; this function's job is to catch the false matches that leniency creates, so strict is the safe side.
- **Removed (P2)**: the stale `CONFIRM PRE-PROD ... No live GHL access yet` marker on `find_location_by_email`, contradicted by this same PR's own test-results doc. Replaced with the 21/07 + 30/07 live verification AND the S1-26d indexing-lag caveat, so callers know a `None` is not proof no location exists.
- **Discovery (the tests encoded false confidence, again)**: four tests could not fail. `test_multi_email_unite_same_key` compared `compute_identity_key(x)` with the identical call (`x == x`). `test_returning_client_links_to_root_not_intermediate` gave the intermediate the SAME sub-account id as the root and tied `created_at`, so both assertions held whichever row won. `test_franchise_separation_different_postcode_not_linked` passed only because `FakeGhlClient` defaulted `lookup_result=None`, so the email leg that breaks it in production never fired. All corrected, plus 18 new cases covering the mixed-name sibling set, the email fallback, every row of the corroboration table, postcode validation and the replay backfill.
- **Verified (the new guards actually fail on the bug)**: rather than trusting green, seven mutations were applied one at a time and the suite re-run - always-reuse a GHL hit (6 failures), judge only the earliest candidate (1), remove the email sibling fallback (2), drop the name guard (5), restore the no-op `ON CONFLICT` (1), drop the `dedup_key` email tier (1), remove postcode validation (17). Every mutation was caught by the intended test, and all were reverted. **One mutation initially reported a false pass** because ruff had reformatted the block the patch targeted, so the patch silently did not apply; re-run against the real source it failed correctly. Worth remembering: a mutation test that "passes" means nothing unless the mutation actually applied.
- **Verified (local, DB tests genuinely RUN)**: Docker Desktop was started, so Postgres came up and migration 0013 applied cleanly - the suite is **552 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed** at commit `e8c4490` (an earlier draft of this entry said 520, which was a mid-work count taken before the last tests landed - corrected here so the entry, the commit message and the PR body all state the same figure). The DB-marked cases at the heart of this fix had never executed on a developer machine before. `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` + `make typecheck` + `make codegen` (no drift) + dashboard build all clean.
- **Verified (the CEL concurrency key, live)**: registered against a real Inngest dev server - `PUT /api/inngest` returned `{"ok": true, "modified": true}` with all **7** functions listed, so the expression is accepted and registration is not broken. Then fired BOTH event shapes: a legacy `client.created` with no `dedup_key`, and a current one with it. Both produced scheduled runs with distinct run ids and both were dispatched to the app, proving the ternary EVALUATES for both shapes rather than merely parsing. With the S1-34b handler-signature fix applied TEMPORARILY (and reverted immediately - S1-34b owns it), `create_ghl_subaccount` executed and reached our code, failing only on the unset local `GHL_COMPANY_ID` with the correct `NonRetriableError`. Also observed the per-client cap holding later runs behind in-flight ones.
- **Fixed (self-caught on a second pass, the exact class the review was about)**: the new API fields shipped with NO endpoint test - `test_client_detail.py` never asserted `possible_duplicate_ghl_id` or `parent_client_id`, and `test_clients_list.py` never asserted `possible_duplicate`. That is the same defect as finding 8 one layer down: a dashboard badge bound to a field the API might not return is a silently dead warning, and nothing would have failed if the SELECT dropped it. Added 5 endpoint tests (clean default, client-collision candidate, GHL-location candidate, auto-link parent, list badge field); mutation-verified by hardcoding the new columns to NULL, which failed 2 of them.
- **Verified (`/pre-pr-review` gate PASSED - finding 9; it was skipped last time, which is how findings 1-7 got through)**: six further findings, all FIXED, none deferred. (1) **Lens 2, zombie `in_progress`** - the GHL flag branch committed mid-flight, adding a raise point between the already-committed `in_progress` row and its terminal state; anything raising there bypasses `_record_failure` and strands the action forever. The flag now rides the terminal commit, matching the DB-sibling branch and making the flag atomic with the outcome, so "flagged but no action row" is unreachable. (2) **Robustness** - `_location_postcode` required a `str`, so a JSON-numeric postcode (`75008`, common for INT) read as "no postcode" and a perfectly corroboratable location would have been flagged for nothing; `int` is now coerced, with `bool` explicitly excluded (it is an `int` subclass and would stringify to "True"). (3) **Lens 1/5, derived data** - `identity_key` is derived AND stored, so any future normalizer change silently splits the population: old rows keep keys the new code cannot produce and a genuine returning client stops matching. A warning in the migration now requires a recompute alongside any such change; this is free only because 0013 has never been applied outside local dev. (4) **Lens 8, no silent caps** - the 50-candidate bound had no test; added one that pins the limitation honestly (a match beyond the cap IS missed) and asserts it is logged, mutation-verified by silencing the log. (5) **Lens 6** - two docstrings still named `_ghl_hit_is_corroborated` after it became `_classify_ghl_hit`. (6) **Hygiene** - `_pick_sibling` was typed `list` -> `tuple[object | None, ...]`, which makes attribute access an error under any checker; now `Sequence[Row] -> tuple[Row | None, Row | None]`.
- **Note (merge ordering, the reviewer's closing point)**: `render.yaml` has no `preDeployCommand` and `alembic upgrade` runs only via `make db-upgrade`. The client queries select the new columns unconditionally, so `/clients` and `/clients/{id}` 500 between deploy and migration, and `create_client_record` raises `UndefinedColumn` - untranslated, so it retry-exhausts into dead-letter. **Migration 0013 must be run WITH the merge.** The general fix is a follow-up ticket, since this pattern will bite every future migration.
- **Note (out of scope, ticketed)**: the merge action (`POST /clients/{id}/merge` + a dashboard button repointing a flagged client at the existing sub-account) is **S1-26e**, to land before S1-35 acceptance - it is what turns the flag from a nag into a 5-second fix, and what makes "create when uncertain" cheap. Also still open: `hubspot_deal_id` is extracted but persisted nowhere.
- **Decision (declined for now, 03/08)**: an address-fallback for the identity key was proposed and **not taken** - when `Company.Zip` is empty, a postcode could be recovered from `Company.Address` (already extracted, currently unused for keying) because the new `normalize_postcode` finds a UK postcode anywhere in a string. It would shrink the NULL-key population for free. Owner's call was to ship as is; a client with no postcode keys NULL and falls back to email dedup, exactly as S1-26 did, so nothing is blocked. Recorded because the mechanism already exists and this is a cheap win whenever the NULL-key population turns out to matter.
- **Discovery (unmeasured, flagged honestly)**: we do NOT know Bullet's real `Company.Zip` fill rate, nor how many of the ~100 legacy GHL sub-accounts carry a `postalCode`. Both drive how often the undecidable-verdict flag actually fires, and both are answerable with read-only probes (PandaDoc document sample; GHL agency location scan). Offered and declined for now. Note the flag only fires when the email lookup RETURNS a hit - a returning client or a shared mailbox - not on an ordinary new signing, and the population decays because every sub-account created from now on carries a postcode.

### 29/07/2026 - S1-26b + S1-26c: returning-client identity key + linked_objects extraction (code-complete)

Implemented both returning-client tickets on one branch `feat/s1-26bc-identity-key` (scope confirmed with the user). No production traffic touched; this is a code + schema change awaiting review.

- **Fixed (S1-26b, extraction)**: `extract_client_fields` (`worker/clients_payload.py`) now reads the HubSpot ids from the document's top-level `linked_objects` list (`[{provider, entity_type, entity_id}]`, the real signed-doc shape - only the deal is linked) via a new `_linked_object_id` helper, with the old dot-notation `metadata` read kept as a defensive fallback for older/other templates. So `hubspot_deal_id` is now captured (previously missed) and company/contact resolve to None on real docs without regressing any doc that still carries them in metadata. Docstring updated.
- **Changed (S1-26c, identity key)**: the returning-client / duplicate check (`worker/ghl_subaccount.py`, `create_ghl_subaccount_core`) is **re-keyed from `email` to a normalized identity key** - `first6(normalize(business_name)) + "|" + normalize(postcode)` (new pure module `worker/identity_key.py`). This UNITES a business signing under different emails and SEPARATES franchises (same brand, different postcode) that email-keying conflated. `create_client_record_core` computes the key and persists it on insert.
- **Added (dedup guard + fail-safe)**: because the key truncates the name to 6 chars, a prefix+postcode collision between two DIFFERENT businesses (e.g. "Fitness First" vs "Fitness Studio" at the same postcode) is caught by `names_materially_diverge` on the FULL normalized names; a divergent match is NOT merged - the row is flagged `possible_duplicate` (+ `possible_duplicate_of`) and provisioned as its own client for a human to review. A NULL identity_key (no usable name/postcode) matches nothing and falls through to CREATE.
- **Changed (concurrency correctness)**: the per-function dedup concurrency guard is re-keyed from `event.data.email` to `event.data.dedup_key` (= identity_key when present, else the unique client_id, carried on the `client.created` event), closing the window where two different-email signings for the same business could both pass the check and create duplicate sub-accounts. Still two constraints (Inngest's max).
- **Added (schema, migration `0013_clients_identity_key`)**: `clients.postal_code` (the extracted `Company.Zip` was previously logged and discarded), `clients.identity_key` (indexed, non-unique), `clients.possible_duplicate` (bool, default false), `clients.possible_duplicate_of` (self-FK). Additive + nullable/defaulted; no backfill needed (staging `clients` is empty; pre-existing rows carry NULL key -> CREATE).
- **Changed (dashboard surface)**: `GET /clients/{id}` returns `possible_duplicate` + `possible_duplicate_of`; `GET /clients` returns `possible_duplicate` so the board can badge duplicates. OpenAPI + TS client regenerated (`make codegen`).
- **Added (tests)**: `tests/test_identity_key.py` (29 cases: normalization, first-6, empty-postcode fail-safe, franchise-separation, prefix-collision divergence); `linked_objects` extraction cases in `test_clients_payload.py`; identity-keyed returning-client + franchise-separation + prefix-collision-flagged + missing-postcode cases in `test_ghl_subaccount.py`; identity_key/postal_code/dedup_key persistence in `test_create_client_record.py`; the concurrency-cap declaration test updated to `event.data.dedup_key`.
- **Verified (local)**: `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard TS) clean; `make codegen` regenerated with no residual drift; `test_openapi_contract` green; pure tests **67/67** and full local run **279 passed, 193 skipped** (the DB-marked tests skip - no local Docker/Postgres in this environment). The DB-backed cases (returning-client link, franchise separation, prefix-collision flag, missing-postcode) verify in CI, which runs `alembic upgrade head -> downgrade base -> upgrade head` then pytest against Postgres. `/pre-pr-review` + CI-green Verified bullet still to follow.

### 29/07/2026 - S1-26c: returning-client identity key LOCKED (first-6 name + postcode) + card created

- **Decision (returning-client identity, lead, 29/07/2026)**: the identity/dedup key is **first 6 characters of the normalized business name + normalized postcode** ("a mix"). Supersedes the plain full-name+postcode proposal (27/28-07) and the earlier HubSpot-company-id idea (dropped). Normalization: lowercase, strip whitespace/punctuation, drop a leading "the", drop trailing company suffixes (ltd/limited), then first-6 of the name; postcode uppercased with the internal space removed (`E8 1AA` -> `E81AA`). `identity_key = first6(normalize(name)) + "|" + normalize(postcode)`.
- **Decision (dedup guard)**: 6 chars is deliberately lenient, so two different gyms sharing a name prefix + postcode can collide (e.g. `Fitness First` vs `Fitness Studio` at the same postcode both -> `fitnes|<postcode>`). On an `identity_key` match where the FULL normalized names materially diverge, **create a new client + raise a "possible duplicate" flag for a human** rather than auto-merge (merges are hard to undo). Clean matches auto-link; no match creates new.
- **Added (ticket)**: created Flow card **S1-26c** (`12059340-bc00-46be-aca6-4f6ff0870460`, Sprint 1 list) capturing the decision, algorithm, fail-safe, schema (indexed `identity_key` column + possible-duplicate flag), edge cases, and acceptance/tests. Split out of **S1-26b**, which now retains Part 1 only (optional `hubspot_deal_id` extraction from `linked_objects`, off the critical path); a cross-link note was added to S1-26b.
- **Open question (still with John)**: could two genuinely different clients ever share the same company NAME and postcode? If not, the key is safe.

### 28/07/2026 - S1-34a DISCOVERY: staging webhook already live on production PandaDoc + fan-out not completing

While checking whether completing a test document would trigger Bullet's live Zapier/Pabbly onboarding, inspected the production PandaDoc webhook subscriptions and the staging DB (both read-only). Two material findings:

- **Discovery (onboarding automation is HubSpot-driven, not a PandaDoc webhook)**: the ONLY webhook subscription on both the UK and INT production PandaDoc workspaces is **ours** - `"Izzy Agents"` -> `bullet-api-staging.onrender.com/webhooks/pandadoc` (ACTIVE, trigger `document_state_changed`, our shared secret). There is **no Zapier or Pabbly webhook** registered in PandaDoc. Every real signed gym agreement carries `linked_objects: [{provider: "hubspot", entity_type: "deal"}]`, so Bullet's real fan-out (Slack/Asana/Stripe/Xero/Drive) is almost certainly triggered via **HubSpot** (deal-stage), not a raw PandaDoc trigger. Implication: a test document created **standalone from the template** (no HubSpot deal linked) very likely does NOT enter the live onboarding - but this must be confirmed against how the actual Zap/Pabbly workflow triggers before completing any production document.
- **Discovery (staging webhook silently capturing real production signings since ~22/07)**: our `"Izzy Agents"` subscription has been live on the production workspace and has already received **4 real completed client agreements** (client names and PandaDoc document ids deliberately NOT recorded here - this file is committed to a GitHub repository, and Bullet's customers did not consent to their business names appearing in our engineering history; the ids are recoverable from `onboarding_events` and the PandaDoc console by anyone who needs them). All were **HMAC-verified + persisted** to `onboarding_events` (proves the receiver + signature verify + account routing work against genuine production webhooks), BUT **none were processed**: `processed_at IS NULL` on all four, and `clients` / `platform_actions` / `documents` are all **empty**. **Root cause CONFIRMED (not an Inngest gap)**: the Inngest serve endpoint is healthy (`GET /api/inngest` -> `function_count: 7, has_event_key: true, has_signing_key: true, mode: cloud`), and `create_client_record` **fetches the full document from PandaDoc** via `api_key_for(account)` before creating the client - with a **PandaDoc 404 mapped to `NonRetriableError` -> dead-letter immediately** (see `worker/client_record.py:438-486` docstring). All 4 docs are on the **`uk`** account, but staging's UK key was **SANDBOX** until it was switched to production today - so the worker fetched each **production** document with a **sandbox** key -> **404 -> dead-lettered**, no client, no fan-out. The webhook + verify + persist + routing are therefore all proven working against real signings; only the fetch failed, purely due to the sandbox/production UK-key mismatch. **The 28/07 production-key switch resolves this** - a fresh signing (or replay) now fetches successfully. To PROVE the full fan-out end to end without touching a real client, run the throwaway "Test Gym Ltd" document through replay (its own new external_id, so no collision with the 4 dead-lettered rows). Note: the 4 dead-lettered events will NOT auto-reprocess (Inngest dead-letter), so switching the key does not retroactively provision the 4 real clients' GHL sub-accounts - good.
- **Data-handling note**: the 4 real events contain client PII (names, emails, phones) in the client-owned staging Neon DB. Decide with Bullet whether to purge the non-test real-client rows from staging once the fan-out is verified.

### 22/07/2026 - Decision: returning-client identity = business name + postcode (drop HubSpot)

- **Decision (returning-client identity, S1-26b)**: John confirmed HubSpot isn't used much, so keying the "one email, one client" / returning-client check on the HubSpot Company ID is out. New direction: key client identity on **normalized `Company.Name` + `Company.Zip` (postcode)** - both already extracted into `ExtractedClientFields` (`business_name`, `postal_code`), so no new data source is needed. This separates franchises (e.g. Brand Gym Hackney E8 vs Brand Gym Croydon CR0 - different name+postcode, redacted example - review round 4 finding 2) and unites one business's multiple emails (same name+postcode), which plain email cannot. Pair it with a **fail-safe**: a confident name+postcode match is treated as returning; an ambiguous one (name close / postcode differs) **creates a new account and raises a "possible duplicate" flag** for a human to merge - never auto-merge (merges are hard to undo).
- **Removed (client ask)**: the HubSpot API key is **no longer needed** - dropped from the Bullet ask list. Consequently the S1-26b Part 1 fix (read `hubspot_deal_id` from `linked_objects`) drops off the critical path - it becomes optional, only if Bullet later wants the HubSpot linkage for other reasons.
- **Open question to John**: could two genuinely different clients ever share the same company name AND postcode? (If not, name+postcode is a safe key.)

### 22/07/2026 - S1-34a DISCOVERY: real PandaDoc document shape breaks HubSpot-id extraction

Inspected a real completed production document (read-only) via Bullet's live PandaDoc key and reconciled it against `extract_client_fields` (`clients_payload.py`). Live testing caught a real contract mismatch the sandbox/unit tests could not.

- **Discovery (extraction mostly correct)**: email, company name/address, contact name, `monthly_service_fee`, `deal_currency`, `pandadoc_template_id`, and `document_creation_source` all extract correctly from the real doc - the client/company/deal data lives in **tokens** (`Client.Email`, `Company.Name`, `Deal.MonthlyServiceFee='850'`, `Deal.Currency='GBP'`) and our code reads them from tokens, which is right. (Also re-confirms Bullet bills in GBP.)
- **Discovery + Bug (HubSpot ids)**: `hubspot_deal_id` / `hubspot_company_id` / `hubspot_contact_id` are read from `metadata`, but a real document's `metadata` only carries `source_process_uuid` + `document__creation_source`. The HubSpot linkage lives in **`linked_objects`**: `[{provider: "hubspot", entity_type: "deal", entity_id: "<redacted, real HubSpot deal id - review round 4 finding 2>"}]` - and **only the deal is linked** (no company, no contact). Consequences: (1) `hubspot_deal_id` is currently **missed entirely** (fixable - read `entity_id` from `linked_objects` where `provider=hubspot` and `entity_type=deal`); (2) `hubspot_company_id` / `hubspot_contact_id` are **not present on the document at all**.
- **Decision impact (one-email-one-client)**: the earlier recommendation to re-key returning-client detection on `hubspot_company_id` is **not directly possible** - the company id isn't on the document. Viable paths: key on `hubspot_deal_id` (available, but a deal is per-engagement, so a returning client signs a NEW deal with a NEW id -> does not identify returning clients), OR resolve deal -> company via the **HubSpot API** (needs HubSpot API access) and key on the company. So the identity re-key now depends on obtaining HubSpot API access.
- **Discovery (key environments differ)**: the `PandaDoc - UK`/`INT` keys on the access sheet are Bullet's **production** keys (they list real client agreements), whereas staging currently runs Bullet's **sandbox** keys. A full end-to-end replay therefore needs either a throwaway document in the sandbox environment, or a deliberate switch of staging to the production keys plus a throwaway test deal - replaying a real client's document would provision a real GHL sub-account.
- **Follow-up ticket (proposed)**: fix `extract_client_fields` to read `hubspot_deal_id` from `linked_objects`, and settle the returning-client identity strategy (deal id vs HubSpot-API-resolved company) now that company id is known to be absent from the document.
