### 16/07/2026 - fix(dashboard): same-origin API proxy so the session cookie works cross-host

- **Fixed**: login succeeded at the API but the dashboard bounced straight back to `/login`. Root cause: the API sets an HttpOnly, host-scoped `session` cookie (`SameSite=Lax`, `login.py`), and the dashboard middleware gates every route on `request.cookies.get('session')` - but the dashboard (`bullet-dashboard-staging-web.onrender.com`) and API (`bullet-api-staging.onrender.com`) are DIFFERENT hosts, and `onrender.com` is a public suffix, so a shared parent-domain cookie is impossible and the middleware could never see the cookie. Worked locally only because `localhost:3000` / `:8000` share the host `localhost` (cookies are per-host, not per-port). Surfaced now because the dashboard had never been deployed before 16/07.
- **Changed**: added a Next.js `rewrites()` proxy in `apps/dashboard/next.config.ts` - `/api/backend/:path*` -> `${API_PROXY_TARGET}/:path*`. With `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=/api/backend` (relative) the browser only ever talks to the dashboard's own origin; Next proxies to the API server-side, so the `session` cookie lands on - and is sent back from - the dashboard host, the middleware reads it, and `SameSite=Lax` is fine (now first-party). This also sidesteps CORS entirely for the browser. Local dev is unchanged (both env vars unset -> falls back to direct `localhost:8000`, rewrite is a no-op). Two new dashboard env vars on Render: `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=/api/backend`, `API_PROXY_TARGET=https://bullet-api-staging.onrender.com`.
- **Fixed (follow-on, same PR)**: the dashboard middleware (`middleware.ts`) gated `/api/backend/*` too - its matcher ran on all non-`_next` paths, so it redirected the login POST to `/login` (307) before the rewrite could proxy it, and no cookie was ever set. Excluded `api` from the middleware matcher (`/((?!api|_next/...).*)`) so it gates PAGE routes only; the `/api/backend/*` proxy is authenticated by the backend API itself via the forwarded session cookie.
- **Note**: the long-term production alternative is co-locating dashboard + API under a shared registrable domain (e.g. `*.bulletdigitalmedia.com`) so the cookie can be parent-scoped; the proxy is the correct fix for the onrender.com split-host staging (and works for prod too).

### 16/07/2026 - Sprint 1 staging deployment brought live + integration config (Render, ops)

The staging environment was brought from "3-weeks-stale / never fully deployed" to a live, configured staging stack running current `main`. All work done via the Render REST API using the client-workspace API key the lead provided; no code changes. Tracked against S1-25c, S1-34, S1-34a and the Sentry client-action card.

- **Fixed (root cause of a 3-week deploy blackout)**: every staging deploy since 17/06 had been `update_failed`. The app mounts the Inngest endpoint at import (`main.py:75` `inngest.fast_api.serve(...)`), which raises `SigningKeyMissingError` when `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` is unset - and the `bullet-staging-env` group had only 3 vars (`DATABASE_URL`, `NODE_VERSION`, `PYTHON_VERSION`); no Inngest keys, no anything. So uvicorn couldn't load the app -> exit 1 -> Render kept the pre-17/06 (auth-era) build. Set `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` (+ `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY`) from the client's Inngest Cloud account -> deploy went live on `a2a7e3c`.
- **Discovery**: the `inngest_client` (`worker/_inngest.py`) already tolerates a missing key (only adds it when present), but `serve()` in `main.py` does not - an inconsistency. Follow-up: `main.py` should boot gracefully (loud warning) when Inngest is unconfigured rather than hard-crash.
- **Fixed (staging DB was 6 migrations behind)**: ran `alembic upgrade head` against the staging Neon DB - was stuck at `0006`, now `0012` (0007 unique pandadoc doc id, 0008 oe pandadoc_account, 0009 clients index, 0010 sales_call_transcripts, 0011 anthropic enum, 0012 openai enum). Discovery: render.yaml has NO migration step (build=uv sync, start=uvicorn), so migrations are manual. Follow-up: add `preDeployCommand: uv run alembic upgrade head`.
- **Added (dashboard deployed for the first time)**: the dashboard had never been deployed - `bullet-dashboard-staging` was a `static_site` serving a placeholder ("Next.js scaffold lands in S1-16"), even though S1-21's render.yaml declared it `type: web`. Render can't convert static->web, so created a NEW web service `bullet-dashboard-staging-web` (https://bullet-dashboard-staging-web.onrender.com) alongside the placeholder (non-destructive). The render.yaml-declared build was wrong for the pnpm monorepo; the working config: rootDir=repo-root, `NODE_VERSION=22.14.0` override (a dep needs >=22.13), build `npm install -g pnpm@10.17.0 && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm --filter @bullet/dashboard build`, start `cd apps/dashboard && pnpm exec next start -p $PORT`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://bullet-api-staging.onrender.com`. Set `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` on the API to the dashboard origin(s). Live and serving the real Next.js app first-build. Follow-ups: retire the placeholder + rename to the clean URL; fix render.yaml's dashboard block to match via a small PR.
- **Added (integration credentials loaded onto `bullet-staging-env`)**: PandaDoc sandbox API keys (UK + INT) + base URL; `OPENAI_API_KEY` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (both live-verified via a real model-list call before loading - the OpenAI key from a screenshot failed first read, an OCR `l`->`I` misread, corrected from a text paste); `GHL_AGENCY_API_KEY` (the `pit-…` agency token from the auth sheet); `RESEND_API_KEY` (+ `EMAIL_FROM=onboarding@resend.dev`, the Resend dev sender, per lead - no domain verify needed for testing); `SENTRY_DSN` (api) + `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` (dashboard) + `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT=staging`; a freshly-generated `EMAIL_TOKEN_SECRET` (replacing the insecure `dev-insecure-change-me` default) + `EMAIL_CONFIRMATION_BASE_URL` (staging URL, was `localhost`).
- **Decision (sandbox for staging)**: staging uses Bullet's PandaDoc SANDBOX API keys (same `api.pandadoc.com` host); live keys are a production-only item, not a staging blocker. Sandbox covers end-to-end demo/testing.
- **Deferred (client/vendor, do not block the demo)**: R2 (`R2_*`) - client must add a card to enable R2 on their Cloudflare account (free tier, ~$0); this is the one real gate for the transcript/PDF storage smokes. PandaDoc webhook shared keys (UK + INT) - client creates the webhooks (sandbox is fine) then sends the keys. GCP project id - for the Meet transcript auto-capture. Sentry org slug + auth token - optional, only for readable dashboard stack traces. GHL `company_id` - derived by us via the agency key (their "0-011-229" is a billing CRN, not the API id).
- **Process**: everything set via the Render REST API; env-group backups snapshotted to scratch before edits; long API keys verified with a live call before loading (OCR-from-screenshot is error-prone). Cards S1-34 (auto-deploy confirmed), S1-25c (deploy-gate cutover), and the Sentry client-action card moved to Completed; the remaining live-smoke + prod items consolidated under S1-34a.

### 06/07/2026 - repo hygiene: rename the colon-named meeting note (Windows-breaking path)

- **Fixed**: renamed `meeting_notes/onboarding/Onboarding – 2026:03:18.txt` -> `Onboarding – 2026-03-18.txt` (colons are illegal in NTFS filenames, so the old name could never exist on a Windows checkout). The invalid path forced the Windows-side sparse-checkout workaround (`!meeting_notes/` + sparse index) and caused recurring git index corruption symptoms: phantom `D package.json` status entries after checkouts/stashes, the colon file itself falling out of the index as a staged deletion, and stale `.git/index.lock` files from IDE git integrations choking on the path. Pure rename (R100, content unchanged), staged at the index level since the old path cannot be materialised on Windows. After this lands, Windows clones no longer need the sparse-checkout exclusion.
- **Decision**: we decided to go ahead with the rename in this PR (alongside S1-27b/c) rather than a separate GitHub-web-UI commit to main, so it rides the normal review + CI path.

### 06/07/2026 - Decision: GCP project for Meet capture is client-owned

- **Decision**: we decided to go ahead with creating the Google Cloud project for the S1-27 Meet transcript capture (`bullet-onboarding-automation`, per infrastructure.md B9) under **Bullet's Google organisation**, not IzzyAgents' - consistent with the client-owned posture for Render, R2, and Sentry. IzzyAgents does the setup work inside it (service account `onboarding-bot`, Meet + Calendar APIs, Pub/Sub + Workspace Events wiring). Consequence: a new Bullet-side prerequisite BEFORE the domain-wide-delegation authorisation request can be prepared - Bullet must either invite `team@izzyagents.ai` into their Google Cloud org with project-creation rights, or create the empty project themselves and grant Owner/Editor on it. No authorisation request has been sent to Bullet yet (the service account / Client ID does not exist until this lands).

### 06/07/2026 - S1-27b + S1-27c: transcript link wrong-client 409 + typed useClients() hook (combined PR)

The two follow-ups split out of the S1-27a PR #9 review. They touch DISJOINT surfaces (`apps/api/.../transcripts/` vs `apps/dashboard/`), so they ship together in a SINGLE PR/branch (`feat/s1-27bc-transcript-race-and-useclients`) - one review, two tickets.

**S1-27b - fix wrong-client race on transcript link (lost race must 409, not 200):**
- **Fixed**: the lost-race branch of `POST /transcripts/{id}/link` (`transcripts/router.py`). When `link_transcript_to_client` returns None (another request linked the transcript between our initial unlinked-check and the guarded `WHERE client_id IS NULL` UPDATE) the code assumed the winner used the SAME client and returned **200 echoing the requested client** - even though the transcript was actually linked ELSEWHERE (a silent wrong-client success). Now it re-reads the actual winner: same client -> idempotent 2xx (recovery re-emit); **DIFFERENT client -> 409**. Mirrors the already-linked branch (already correct). The S1-27a dashboard's `onError` already maps 409 -> `invalidateQueries(['transcripts','unlinked'])` (verified in the code), so the UI stale-refreshes and recovers.
- **Added (tests)**: two lost-race tests in `test_transcripts_endpoints.py` that SIMULATE the race (monkeypatch `link_transcript_to_client` to return None while linking the row to a DIFFERENT client -> 409 + no wrong-client emit; and to the SAME client -> idempotent 200). They exercise the endpoint's lost-race branch (the fix); the true DB-level race itself is S1-27's guarded UPDATE, unchanged. The existing already-linked-different-client -> 409 and same-client-idempotent -> 200 tests still pass. Hardened in verification: the fake race-winner now also commits the winner's `documents` row (as the real winning request would), so the same-client test asserts the recovery RE-EMIT actually fires from the winner's committed state (`transcript.linked` with the winner's `document_id`) instead of silently exercising the no-document degraded path.

**S1-27c - extract typed useClients() hook (dashboard):**
- **Added**: `hooks/use-clients.ts` - a single typed `useClients()` hook (+ shared `clientsQueryKey`, `CLIENTS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`) wrapping `GET /clients` (8s abort, 10s poll). Routed BOTH the S1-31 board (`clients-table.tsx`) and the S1-27a picker (`unlinked-transcripts.tsx`), plus the picker's two cache invalidations, through it - so those call sites cannot drift apart. (Acknowledged residual: it does NOT stop a NEW call site from hand-writing `['clients']` again; the guard against that is using `useClients()` everywhere, not the constant.)
- **Not needed (already done)**: the card's 2nd item (combobox stale-client desync) was already fixed on `main` in the PR #9 round - the Attach button gates on the resolved client (`clients.find(id) ?? null`). Confirmed; nothing to change.

- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **429 passed** (incl. the 2 new lost-race tests, against post-S1-26a-merge `main`); `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard) clean; `make codegen` no drift (S1-27b changes internal status logic only - no response-model change); `pnpm --filter dashboard build` green. Playwright `clients-list.spec` + `unlinked-transcripts.spec` both pass (`clients-list` needs a warm run - the pre-existing cold-Next-compile login-timeout flake, not this change).
- **Fixed (PR review - 5 findings, none blocking; CI was 5/5 green on `44a4475` incl. the two DB-marked race tests)**: (P2) extracted the same-vs-different-client rule into ONE shared helper (`_linked_conflict_or_idempotent`) used by BOTH the already-linked branch and the lost-race re-read, so the two copies cannot drift. (P2) tightened the lost-race 404 comment: besides a row deleted mid-request, `client_id` can also reset to NULL via the clients FK `ON DELETE SET NULL` (migration 0010) if the winner's client is deleted before our re-read - "back to unlinked", not "gone"; neither path is reachable today (no delete endpoints) but the comment now names both. (P2) added the previously-untested 404 branch's two tests (row deleted mid-race -> 404 no emit; reset-to-unlinked end state -> 404 no emit). (P3) `_fake_lost_race_link` now mirrors the real link path column-for-column (caller's `link_method` + `linked_by`, guarded `WHERE client_id IS NULL` UPDATE, ON CONFLICT-guarded documents insert) instead of a simplified double. (P3) restored the poll-interval rationale the hook extraction dropped: 10s is the S1-31 card spec and the Playwright e2e wait windows are tuned to it - deliberately NOT the shared 7s constant, and now documented so a future "unify to 7s" cleanup does not break the e2e.

### 06/07/2026 - S1-26a: PR review fixes (throttle + citext-casing claim)

Addressed the two P2s from the PR review (verdict: MERGE AFTER MINOR FIXES, no P0/P1 across 7 lenses). On branch `feat/s1-26a-ghl-concurrency-limit`.

- **Added (throttle - reverses the 02/07 "throttle judged unnecessary" decision)**: a keyless `throttle=inngest.Throttle(limit=5, period=10s)` on `create_ghl_subaccount`. The reviewer surfaced the concrete case the earlier dismissal missed: dropping the global cap left the aggregate GHL start-rate unbounded, and a `reconcile_pandadoc` nightly pass can heal several dropped signings at once - each fanning out to a DISTINCT `client.created` (distinct `client_id` + `email`), so neither `limit=1` keyed concurrency cap bounds that burst. `throttle=` is rate-over-time and a SEPARATE Inngest param, so it does NOT count against the 2-constraint concurrency limit that was the S1-26a bug (verified: `get_config` shows `throttle` present alongside the two concurrency caps). Semantics, precisely (per Inngest GCRA docs): at most `limit + burst` run STARTS per period window - with the SDK-default `burst=1` that is up to 6 starts in a single 10s window, sustained 5/10s. It bounds starts, NOT in-flight calls: under sustained GHL slowness overlapping runs can still exceed the old in-flight cap of 3, so the throttle is a WEAKER politeness bound than the dropped Concurrency cap, not a restoration of it - judged sufficient because the DB idempotency key + returning-client check own duplicate-create correctness and the cap's only job is politeness.
- **Decision (throttle rate: sustained 5/10s + default burst 1, approved by the lead 06/07/2026)**: keyless (caps the whole function, not per-client). The value 5 borrows its magnitude from the sibling workers' global `Concurrency(limit=5)` caps as a familiar scale, though the mechanisms are NOT equivalent (starts-per-window vs in-flight runs - see the semantics bullet above). Chosen over re-adding a concurrency cap (would be a 3rd constraint, the original bug) and over a per-key throttle (the burst is across DISTINCT clients, so only a global bound helps).
- **Changed (docs - narrow the citext-casing overclaim, P2)**: the module docstring + per-email decorator comment previously read as if the citext DB sibling SELECT + GHL lookup CLOSE the email-casing race. They close only the SEQUENTIAL case (a returning signing that arrives after the first has committed its id); two rows whose emails differ only in CASE arriving TRULY CONCURRENTLY can still both pass the returning-client check before either commits, because the per-email concurrency key is a raw string vs citext. Comments now say exactly that. Normalising the key to `lower(event.data.email)` would shut the window but needs the Inngest CEL key evaluator's `lower()` support confirmed first - logged as a follow-up, NOT attempted here (a wrong guess risks re-breaking `/fn/register`, this ticket's exact bug).
- **Added (test)**: `test_create_ghl_subaccount_declares_global_throttle` - asserts the keyless throttle is declared at `limit=5`, `period=10s`, AND `burst=1` (locking the real per-window ceiling of `limit + burst = 6` as a deliberate choice, so a silent SDK-default bump cannot change the enforced rate unnoticed), and that `rate_limit is None` (throttle queues-and-drains; `rate_limit` DROPS excess runs, which would strand a backlog-heal's real signings). The existing `..._declares_concurrency_caps` guard (exactly 2 keyed caps, no global concurrency cap) stays green - throttle is separate, so the registry `<=2 concurrency constraints` guard is unaffected.
- **Decision (follow-up, S1-26b proposed)**: normalise the per-email concurrency key to `lower(event.data.email)` to close the truly-concurrent different-casing window, pending confirmation that Inngest's key CEL evaluator supports `lower()`. Scope widened per the round-4 review: the unclosed window risks not just a duplicate GHL location but ALSO a silently missed `parent_client_id` link (neither concurrent row sees the other as a committed sibling, so the S1-26 returning-client tree is never wired) - both consequences are now stated in the code comments.
- **Fixed (PR review round 4 - wording/config-accuracy, no behaviour change)**: (1) the decorator comment, module docstring, and this entry's original wording overclaimed that the throttle "restores" the dropped cap's protection - it bounds run STARTS only, not in-flight concurrency; all three corrected. (2) The documented rate said exactly 5/10s but Inngest GCRA admits `limit + burst` starts per window (6 with the default `burst=1`); comment + test now state and assert the true ceiling. (3) Fixed a garbled sentence in the module docstring ("back that case up" -> "cover that case"). (4) Retagged this entry's non-sanctioned "Deferred" bullet to "Decision" (the same tag class was already removed from the 02/07 entry in the previous review round).
- **Verified (live - closes the round-4 blocker)**: re-ran the live `/fn/register` boot verification WITH the throttle in place (the earlier live check predated it): Inngest dev server via `npx inngest-cli dev` + `uvicorn` with `INNGEST_DEV=1`, SDK sync `PUT /api/inngest` returns **200**, the dev server registers **all 7 functions**, and its parsed config for `create-ghl-subaccount` shows `throttle {limit: 5, period: 10s, burst: 1}` + exactly 2 concurrency constraints + `rateLimit: null` - server-side confirmation of both the throttle acceptance and the burst=1 semantics. Zero `registration_failed` / `error registering` lines in either log.

### 02/07/2026 - S1-26a: fix create_ghl_subaccount Inngest concurrency limit (3 > max 2) - code-complete (local)

Fixed the latent pre-prod blocker discovered during S1-27a (CHANGELOG 17/06/2026): `create_ghl_subaccount` declared THREE Inngest concurrency constraints (global `limit=3` + per-client + per-email), but Inngest allows a MAX of 2. Registration is all-or-nothing, so this one invalid function failed the WHOLE `/fn/register` sync - so NONE of the fan-out workers (`create_client_record`, `create_ghl_subaccount`, `store_signed_pdf`, `capture_meet_transcript`, `summarise_sales_call`, `store_sales_knowledge`) would register. Never caught because the tests call the cores directly and the live Inngest path had not been exercised.

- **Discovery (corrects the 17/06 diagnosis)**: the 17/06 S1-27a entry called the same `registration_failed: At least one function is invalid` log only "most likely" the 3 constraints and wrongly concluded it "does not affect prod (Inngest Cloud registration is separate)". Root cause is now CONFIRMED (not hypothesised) and the "separate" claim was wrong: the `/fn/register` max-2 validation is enforced identically by the local dev server and Inngest Cloud. See the live verification below.

- **Fixed**: dropped the global `Concurrency(limit=3, scope="fn")` throughput cap from `create_ghl_subaccount`; kept the two correctness guards (`event.data.client_id`=1 + `event.data.email`=1). The cap only bounded simultaneous runs (rarely >1-2 at Bullet's signing volume); the DB idempotency key (`platform_actions` ON CONFLICT) + the S1-26 returning-client check prevent duplicate GHL accounts regardless, and Inngest auto-retry covers a rare GHL rate blip. Updated the module + wrapper docstrings.
- **Decision (drop vs throttle)**: the Inngest SDK does expose `throttle=` / `rate_limit=` (rate-over-time, which does NOT count against the 2-constraint concurrency limit), so a global GHL-politeness cap could be re-expressed there. Judged unnecessary: throttle is a different (rate, not in-flight) semantic, needs an invented period, and the cap almost never engaged. Dropped per the card's recommendation; noted `throttle=` as the path if GHL rate-limiting is ever wanted.
- **Added (test)**: `test_worker_registry.py` - a FUNCTIONS-wide guard asserting EVERY `worker.client.FUNCTIONS` entry declares <=2 concurrency constraints (via `fn.get_config("").main.concurrency`, matching the sibling config tests' convention), plus a NEGATIVE self-test proving the guard actually fires on a synthetic 3-constraint function (so the pass is not vacuous). Its docstring is scoped to what it proves - it catches the specific >2-constraint regression, a proxy, NOT a claim that registration as a whole can never fail. Updated `test_ghl_subaccount.py`'s concurrency-declaration test (now asserts exactly the two dedup guards, no global cap) + its module docstring.
- **Changed (docs)**: corrected the S1-25 acceptance-test line in `development-sprints.md` (the "Concurrency cap of 3 enforced" criterion, now superseded by S1-26a); softened the module-docstring per-email-cap wording to acknowledge the citext-casing residual (the raw-string key does not serialise `X@y.com` vs `x@Y.COM`; the citext DB sibling SELECT + GHL lookup are the backstop).
- **Verified (live - resolves the deferred check)**: booted the app (`uvicorn`, `INNGEST_DEV=1`) against the running Inngest dev server with the fix: `POST http://127.0.0.1:8288/fn/register` returns **200 OK** on every 5s sync cycle and there are **zero `registration_failed`** logs. The BEFORE state (on `main`, 3 constraints) was captured in the same setup during the S1-27b/c e2e runs: `registration_failed: At least one function is invalid` every 5s. So the root cause + fix are confirmed, not hypothesised.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` green (incl. the registry guard + its negative self-test + the updated GHL config test); `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` clean; `make codegen` no drift. Branch `feat/s1-26a-ghl-concurrency-limit` cut off updated `main` once S1-30 merged (PR #11).

### 02/07/2026 - S1-30: write sales summary to client_knowledge with embeddings (backend) - code-complete

Built the S1-30 worker on `feat/s1-30-client-knowledge-embeddings` (off `main` at `3f42203`). Consumes S1-29's `sales_summary.ready` and persists the §7.1 summary into the client's knowledge profile with embeddings - the step that actually fills S1-32's summary panel. Built against a Fake embedding seam (no live OpenAI key exists yet). Plan: `~/.claude/plans/s1-30-client-knowledge-embeddings.md`.

- **Added**: `store_sales_knowledge` Inngest worker (`worker/sales_knowledge.py`), triggered by `sales_summary.ready`. For each of the seven §7.1 fields it writes one `client_knowledge` row (`source='sales_call'`; key = field, `value` = the field's JSONB shape, `value_text` = prose), stores an embedding of each non-empty `value_text`, records the attempt in `platform_actions` (`platform='openai'`, `action='store_sales_knowledge'`). All seven rows are inserted in ONE transaction with ONE shared `captured_at` - honouring S1-32's `_GET_SUMMARY_SQL` latest-batch contract (a per-row-committing writer could expose a partial batch). Mirrors S1-29's hardening: commit `in_progress` before the embedding call, broad-except around ONLY the external work (validation + embed) so a failure is recorded `failed` not left zombie `in_progress`, and a concurrency back-off guard.
- **Added**: the OpenAI embeddings seam (`worker/embedding_client.py`) - `EmbeddingClient` Protocol (`embed(list[str]) -> list[list[float]]`) + `HttpOpenAIEmbeddingClient` (async SDK batch embed, index-ordered, pooled client, fail-loud typed `EmbeddingConfigError` on empty key) + `FakeEmbeddingClient`. `get_embedding_client()` process-cached factory. `openai` dep; `openai_api_key` (empty default, fail-loud) + `openai_embedding_model` (`text-embedding-3-small`) config.
- **Added**: `CLIENT_KNOWLEDGE_SOURCE_*` constants + `CLIENT_KNOWLEDGE_SOURCE_VALUES` to `db/enums.py` (mirroring the 0003 tuple). Migration `0012_platform_openai` (`ALTER TYPE platform_action_platform ADD VALUE 'openai'`, autocommit block, no-op downgrade - mirrors 0011). NO migration for `client_knowledge_source` - `sales_call` already exists from 0003.
- **Decision (embedding provider, approved by the lead 02/07/2026)**: **OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small`**. Its native 1536 dimension is an exact match for the `client_knowledge.embedding vector(1536)` column, and OpenAI is already a provisioned vendor (infrastructure.md A8 Whisper) so no new vendor relationship. Built against a Fake seam; the live key is a pre-prod follow-up (same posture as S1-29's Anthropic key). Voyage was considered (Anthropic's recommended partner) but would add a net-new vendor.
- **Decision (idempotency)**: keyed on `transcript_id` + a sha256 of the summary payload (reusing `build_idempotency_key`'s `*extra` from S1-29), so an at-least-once re-emit of the SAME summary dedupes to one batch, but a corrected re-link that produces a DIFFERENT summary writes a fresh batch. Absorbs the at-least-once double-emit S1-29 relies on S1-30 to handle. No downstream emit (nothing consumes this yet).
- **Discovery**: the embedding provider was never actually pinned. PRD §4.2 sizes the column `vector(1536)` "OpenAI/Anthropic-compatible" (dimension only), and **infrastructure.md A7 wrongly attributed "semantic search embeddings" to Anthropic - which has no embeddings API**. Corrected A7 to name OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small` for embeddings. The card/dev-sprints entry names no provider.
- **Discovery (contract nuance)**: the card's test wording "each row has a non-null embedding" and PRD §4.2 "populated when `value_text` is non-empty" collide for an empty field. Resolved: all 7 rows always written; a field with empty `value_text` (e.g. `red_flags=[]`, `budget_range_usd=None`) gets a NULL embedding + NULL `value_text` per the PRD; the "non-null" assertion holds for a fully-populated summary. Also the card's "idempotent on (client_id, source, key, captured_at)" is imprecise (`captured_at` changes per run so it can never dedupe) - the real "re-run doesn't duplicate" is met by the `platform_actions` guard.
- **Fixed (pre-PR review - 2 independent review agents + the /pre-pr-review gate)**: (P0/P1) the `client_knowledge` INSERT + `complete_action` + `commit` originally sat OUTSIDE the fail-recording try, so a wrong-dimension embedding (confirmed: pgvector rejects a non-1536 literal) or any post-embed DB error propagated UNCLASSIFIED -> retriable -> infinite retry AND left a zombie `in_progress` row - the exact S1-29 class, one step later. Now the persist is wrapped (rollback -> `fail_action` in a clean txn -> re-raise), and a **dimension check** right after `embed()` raises `EmbeddingConfigError` (dead-letter) for a misconfigured model (uses the previously-unused `KNOWLEDGE_DIM`). (P2) `HttpOpenAIEmbeddingClient.embed` now asserts one vector per input (a partial API response raises instead of `IndexError`-ing downstream). (P3) the idempotency hash is now taken over the VALIDATED/canonical summary (`model_validate(...).model_dump()`), not the raw event dict, so dedupe is a function of semantic content, not the emitter's wire representation - validation moved before `begin_action` (a schema-invalid summary now dead-letters with no row, like a malformed event). (P3) `document_id` is now recorded in the `begin_action` payload (was threaded but unused). (P3/F1) closed the router's dangling "captured_at-tie -> S1-30 follow-up": a monotonic batch key was judged unnecessary (a microsecond tie across two distinct batches is unreachable given the idempotency guard + human-paced volume) and the router comment updated to record the dismissal.
- **Added (tests)**: `test_sales_knowledge.py` (happy 7-rows/shared-captured_at/embeddings + value shapes; empty-field NULL embedding; replay dedupe; corrected re-link fresh batch; schema-invalid summary dead-letters pre-row; **wrong-dimension embedding**; **persist-failure records failed not zombie**; embedding-config + transport errors; concurrency back-off; wrapper malformed-event x2 + ValidationError/EmbeddingConfigError NonRetriable + OpenAI 4xx-vs-429/5xx taxonomy + back-off propagate; Inngest config) and `test_embedding_client.py` (batch request shape, index re-order, empty-input short-circuit, **partial-response raises**, empty-key fail-loud, Fake contract).
- **Fixed (PR #11 review - external reviewer)**: (P1) the STALE in_progress reclaim had no atomic claim - two overlapping runs (e.g. a manual replay of a run that looks stuck but is alive) could BOTH pass the 15-min staleness check and BOTH write a full duplicate 7-row batch + double-spend the OpenAI call. Added `reclaim_stale_action` to the shared `platform_actions` recorder: a compare-and-swap on `started_at` (`UPDATE ... WHERE id = ? AND status='in_progress' AND started_at = <seen> RETURNING id`) so only one reclaimer wins; the loser gets False and backs off (retriable `ConcurrentKnowledgeInProgress`). This also resolves the reviewer's (P2) "losing racer silently overwrites the winner's `complete_action`" - the loser never reaches `complete_action`. **Applied the identical fix to S1-29's `summarise_sales_call`** (same latent bug - the reclaim pattern S1-30 mirrored), so both workers now route through the one shared atomic primitive (also closes the reviewer's P3 "reclaim logic duplicated").
- **Fixed (PR #11 review, P2/P3 cleanup)**: (P2) added the previously-missing stale-reclaim tests - a CAS unit test (`test_platform_actions.py`: wins once, a second reclaim with the same stale value loses; a non-in_progress row is never resurrected) + worker-level stale-reclaim-success + lost-race-backs-off for BOTH S1-30 and S1-29. (P3) consolidated the duplicated 1536 dimension constant - `sales_knowledge.py` now imports `EMBEDDING_DIM` from `embedding_client.py` (dropped `KNOWLEDGE_DIM`). (P3) added an explicit 30s request timeout to the OpenAI client (`EMBEDDING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; the SDK default 600s is far too long for a worker holding the run open).
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **424 passed** against local Postgres (`bullet_dev`, migration `0012` at head); `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard) clean; `make codegen` no drift (no FastAPI response-model change); `alembic heads` single (`0012`). Deep review: the /pre-pr-review 9 lenses PLUS two independent review agents (correctness/hardening + contract/business-flow) cross-checked against every S1-29-caught class and the S1-32/PRD/§7.1 contracts; the S1-29 hardening lessons were applied up front (hostile-input event parse, full 4xx taxonomy, concurrency back-off, broad-except, atomic batch). Confirmed pgvector accepts the `[...]` literal incl. scientific notation.
- **Deferred (pre-prod follow-up)**: real `openai_api_key` on Render + a live-embedding smoke (real 1536 vectors written + an ANN query). Sprint 4 semantic search consumes these embeddings.
- **Deferred (S1-32 dashboard follow-up, F2)**: `budget_range_usd = null` (no budget stated) falls through the dashboard's `SummaryValue` branches to `<pre>{JSON.stringify(null)}</pre>` and renders the literal "null" instead of an empty-state affordance. Backend writes JSON null intentionally (row always present); the fix is a `value == null -> <EmptyValue/>` guard in `client-detail.tsx`, out of this backend PR's scope.
- **Note (P3, near-unreachable)**: if a client were deleted between `sales_summary.ready` and the INSERT, the FK insert now records the action `failed` via the persist-guard (no zombie) but still retries (a deterministic FK error); no delete-client flow exists in Phase 1, so this is unreachable. Revisit (classify FK violations NonRetriable) if a client-delete path is added.

### 02/07/2026 - S1-29: pre-PR review hardening (8-agent CE review)

An 8-agent CE review of the S1-29 diff (before raising the PR) surfaced one P1 and four P2s that the happy-path + modelled-failure tests passed over. All fixed on `feat/s1-29-ai-sales-summary` (folded into the S1-29 commit so the branch stays one clean commit), each with a regression test.

- **Fixed (P1)**: a malformed `transcript.linked` event (missing / non-UUID / wrong-type `client_id`/`transcript_id`/`r2_key`) crashed the wrapper BEFORE any `platform_actions` row was written, so Inngest retried forever with zero dashboard visibility - a direct violation of "every job writes its outcome / partial failures must be visible". The wrapper now parses the event in a try/except and dead-letters a bad payload as `NonRetriableError` + an error log (we cannot record a row without a valid `client_id`, but the infinite invisible retry is gone).
- **Fixed (P2)**: `UnicodeDecodeError` on a non-UTF-8 / corrupted transcript object was not in the wrapper's NonRetriable tuple, so a deterministic failure was retried as if transient. Added to the NonRetriable set.
- **Fixed (P2)**: the error taxonomy dead-lettered only `anthropic.BadRequestError` (400); `AuthenticationError` (401), `PermissionDeniedError` (403), `NotFoundError` (404, e.g. a wrong `anthropic_model` id), `RequestTooLargeError` (413) etc. fell through as retriable despite being structural config failures. Replaced the single-class catch with an `anthropic.APIStatusError` branch: `400 <= status_code < 500 and != 429` -> NonRetriable; 429 + 5xx / overloaded stay retriable.
- **Fixed (P2)**: the idempotency key omitted `r2_key`, so a re-link to a *corrected* transcript (same `transcript_id`, new object - a scenario the S1-27 CHANGELOG documents) would replay-short-circuit and re-emit the stale original summary. `build_idempotency_key` now takes optional trailing `*extra` parts (existing callers unchanged); S1-29 appends `r2_key` so a corrected re-link resummarises.
- **Fixed (P2)**: two concurrent runs of one `transcript_id` could both observe `in_progress` and both call the LLM + emit (the Inngest per-transcript cap is best-effort across a redeploy). `begin_action` now also returns `inserted` (via `xmax = 0`) + `started_at`; the worker backs off (retriable `ConcurrentSummaryInProgress`) when it finds a FRESH in_progress row it did not insert, and reclaims a STALE one (older than `STALE_IN_PROGRESS` = 15 min, above the SDK timeout) so a crashed run does not block forever. Double-emit remains an at-least-once property handled by S1-30's per-transcript idempotency; this closes the double-SPEND.
- **Fixed (P3)**: the production `HttpAnthropicClient` built (and never closed) a new `AsyncAnthropic` per call - now built once and cached on the instance, with `get_summary_client()` process-cached (one client per process). Added direct unit tests for the real client's `stop_reason` branches (refusal / max_tokens / missing parsed_output).
- **Changed**: `build_idempotency_key` (shared recorder) gained optional `*extra` parts and `BeginActionResult` gained `inserted` + `started_at`. Both are additive - the other fan-out callers (S1-25 GHL, S1-25b signed-PDF) are unchanged and their full suites stay green.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **386 passed** (+20 review-regression tests: malformed-event x2, 4xx taxonomy x6, transient x4, UnicodeDecodeError, back-off, r2_key re-link, concurrent back-off, stale reclaim, three stop_reason branches). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` clean; `make codegen` no drift.
- **Process**: root cause was the completeness pass verifying the paths the code already modelled rather than adversarially generating hostile inputs at the two boundaries (the trigger event + the fetched bytes) and enumerating the full SDK exception taxonomy. A backend "hostile trigger input + exhaustive error taxonomy + replay-with-changed-input" lens should be added to `/pre-pr-review` (follow-up).

### 01/07/2026 - S1-27a: PR #9 review fixes + pre-pr-review gains a frontend lens

Addressed the three suggested-on-branch findings from the PR #9 review (no P0/P1 blockers; the failure-path hardening held up). The other three review items are logged as follow-ups below.

- **Fixed**: telemetry hole on the attach mutation (the app's only mutation). The `catch {}` in `unlinked-transcripts.tsx` bound nothing and normalised every failure to a generic `AttachError(0)`, so a real transport failure (openapi-fetch re-throws timeouts/resets) or a genuine JS bug was invisible to Sentry. Now `catch (err)` -> `Sentry.captureException(err)` + preserved as `AttachError.cause` (added an `ErrorOptions` param to the class) before normalising to the friendly status-0 copy.
- **Fixed**: dropped the redundant `(attach.error as Error).message` cast. TanStack Query v5 already types `mutation.error` as `Error` and narrows it under `isError`, so the cast only masked a would-be wrong-type error; now `attach.error.message`.
- **Fixed**: combobox/polled-state desync. `clientId` lived in `useState` and the Attach button gated on the raw id, so when the 10s clients poll dropped the picked client the button stayed enabled and fired a doomed 400. The button now gates on the resolved entity (`clients.find(id) ?? null`); if the pick leaves the current list the control disables itself.
- **Changed**: hardened the `/pre-pr-review` skill with a new **lens #9 (Dashboard / frontend - React + TanStack Query + openapi-fetch)** plus an S1-27a lesson block, since the existing lenses are Python/Inngest-shaped and could not reach the client. The lens encodes: no silently-swallowed client error (Sentry-capture + `cause` before normalising - the frontend twin of "every call records its outcome"), no type assertion that masks a real mismatch, polled/refetched state desync (gate on the resolved entity), query-key + queryFn drift (extract a typed hook), and frontend sad-path test expectations. Process step 6 now also requires `pnpm --filter dashboard build` for any `apps/dashboard/` diff.
- **Decision (follow-ups, not this PR)**: (1) wrong-client concurrent-attach race - the S1-27 lost-race branch returns 200 echoing the requested client instead of 409, so the loser sees a silent success while the row is linked elsewhere; needs a back-end fix ticket. (2) the shared untyped `['clients']` queryKey is re-spelled in both the S1-31 board and the S1-27a picker; extract one typed `useClients()`. (3) frontend sad-path coverage (status->message mapping, transport normaliser, `formatDateTime`/`formatCharCount` null/NaN, and an e2e that asserts the *specific* seeded row vanished rather than a global count) tracked under S1-36 (vitest).
- **Verified**: `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard) clean; `pnpm --filter dashboard build` green (all 9 routes compiled); Playwright e2e **5/5 green** (`make e2e` against docker Postgres at migration head 0010), including `unlinked-transcripts.spec.ts` which drives the real attach flow (`POST /transcripts/{id}/link` 200 -> row drops -> clients refetch) that these three fixes touch. No API change -> no codegen drift. No project prettier/formatter is configured for the dashboard, so the fresh-install prettier warning is default-style noise, not a repo gate; additions follow the file's single-quote/no-semicolon convention.
- **Discovery**: `client-detail.spec.ts` (S1-32) is flake-prone on a cold Next compile - its post-login `expect(page).toHaveURL(/clients$/)` uses the default 5s timeout, which the first-hit route compile can exceed (14x redirect-to-/login, then green on retry / warm run). Unrelated to S1-27a. Follow-up: widen that assertion's timeout to match the transcript spec's 30s navigation timeouts.

### 18/06/2026 - S1-27: PR #8 review fixes (9-lens self-review)

Addressing a 9-agent CE review pass on PR #8. No P0/P1 correctness or security blockers (the idempotency story was empirically validated against a live Postgres - concurrent double-link serialised, partial-index `ON CONFLICT` inference confirmed, webhook OIDC verification sound and fail-closed). The only P1s were two reliability issues in the production-only Google credentials path, both fixed here. Full technical breakdown saved to `docs/pull-requests/PR-8-s1-27-meet-transcript-capture-18-06-2026.md`. On branch `feat/s1-27-meet-transcript-capture`.

- **Fixed (reliability, P1)**: the Google bearer-token mint (`credentials.google_bearer_token`, called from `HttpMeetClient`/`HttpCalendarClient`) was a blocking synchronous `requests` refresh executed directly on the asyncio event loop - a slow Google token endpoint would have stalled the whole worker loop (every concurrent capture/webhook/request), the same trap `R2StorageClient` avoids via `asyncio.to_thread`. The Http clients now mint the token off-loop via `asyncio.to_thread` (new `_bearer_token` coroutine replacing the sync `_headers`).
- **Fixed (reliability, P1)**: that same refresh had no timeout, so a hung Google OAuth endpoint would have blocked indefinitely (and, blocking the loop, frozen the process). The off-loop mint is now bounded by `asyncio.wait_for(..., token_timeout=15s default)`; a timeout raises a clear `RuntimeError` rather than hanging. The Meet/Calendar HTTP calls already carried their own 10s timeout; this closes the gap on the token mint that precedes them.
- **Fixed (performance, P2)**: `_service_account_credentials()` rebuilt a fresh `Credentials` on every HTTP call (4-6+ per capture), so google-auth's own token cache never survived and a token was re-minted each call. Credentials are now process-cached (`_delegated_credentials()` builds once) and refreshed only when expired (`creds.valid`), with a lock that coalesces concurrent refreshes onto a single network round-trip.
- **Added (test)**: `test_meet_token_minted_off_the_event_loop` (asserts the token_provider runs in a worker thread, not the loop thread), plus `test_meet_token_mint_times_out_raises_runtimeerror` and `test_calendar_token_mint_times_out_raises_runtimeerror`. The empty-token `RuntimeError` contract is preserved (message unchanged), so the two existing empty-token tests stay green.
- **Deferred (logged in the review doc, not blockers)**: the remaining items are P2/P3 - the manual-link stale-`r2_key` emit-drop on a capture race, the non-concurrent `documents` index build (vs the migration 0007 `CONCURRENTLY` convention), the documents-lookup SELECT dedup helper, the `assemble_transcript_text(entries: list)` typing, the signing-time multi-transcript + `already_linked`-on-entry test gaps, the client-delete FK-divergence and signing-side `.strip()` email advisories, and the `enums.py` migration-filename typo (`0010_create_...` -> `0010_...`). The unverified live-Google JSON field paths remain a deliberate pre-prod-wiring smoke-test item.
- **Verified**: `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on the changed files; `uv run pytest apps/api/tests -m "not db"` -> **188 passed** (incl. the 3 new token-mint tests + the 2 preserved empty-token tests). The 160 `@pytest.mark.db` tests were not run in the review worktree (the pgvector :5433 DB was not up); they exercise the `Fake*` clients, not the changed credentials/Http paths, so they are unaffected. Full `pytest apps/api` re-runs on CI against the DB.

### 17/06/2026 - S1-29: AI sales-call summary generator (backend) - code-complete

Built the S1-29 summariser on `feat/s1-29-ai-sales-summary`, stacked on `feat/s1-27` (`c728c0d`) because it consumes S1-27's `transcript.linked` event + the R2 transcript-text format (rebase onto `main` after S1-27 merges; no PR until then). Built against a Fake LLM seam - no live Anthropic key exists yet. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/iterative-gliding-pebble.md`.

- **Added**: `summarise_sales_call` Inngest worker (`worker/sales_summary.py`), triggered by `transcript.linked`. Reads the transcript from R2, asks Claude for the PRD §7.1 structured summary, validates it against `SalesCallSummary`, records the attempt in `platform_actions` (`platform='anthropic'`, `action='summarise_sales_call'`), and emits `sales_summary.ready` carrying the validated summary for S1-30 to write into `client_knowledge`. Mirrors the S1-25b/S1-27 hardening: commit `in_progress` before the LLM call, broad-except around ONLY the external work (R2 read + LLM) so a failure is recorded `failed` not left zombie `in_progress`, emit-after-commit, and re-emit on replay (the validated summary is stored on `platform_actions.response`, so an `already_succeeded` short-circuit re-derives + re-emits rather than silently stranding S1-30).
- **Added**: the Anthropic summariser seam (`worker/summary_client.py`) - `SummaryClient` Protocol + `HttpAnthropicClient` (async SDK `messages.parse` structured output, prompt-cached system + few-shot prefix, `stop_reason` checked before `parsed_output`) + `FakeSummaryClient` (canned summary / seeded error, call counter). `SalesCallSummary` + nested `BudgetRange{min,max,currency}` / `NotableQuote{speaker,quote,timestamp_seconds}` (`summary/models.py`, `extra="forbid"`, field names byte-identical to the §7.1 / `KnowledgeEntry` / dashboard contract). `summary/prompts.py` holds the byte-stable cached prefix.
- **Added**: `storage.get_object(key) -> bytes` on the `StorageClient` Protocol + `R2StorageClient` (boto3 `get_object` via `asyncio.to_thread`; a `NoSuchKey`/`NotFound`/`404` `ClientError` becomes the typed `ObjectNotFound`, 5xx/throttle propagate as retriable) + `FakeStorageClient` (`gets` dict + `get_error`). `SALES_SUMMARY_READY_EVENT = "sales_summary.ready"` in `worker/events.py`.
- **Added**: `anthropic>=0.69,<1.0` dep (resolved 0.109.2); `anthropic_api_key` (empty default, fail-loud) + `anthropic_model` config settings.
- **Decision (model)**: `claude-opus-4-7` as carded, exposed as the `anthropic_model` setting (default `claude-opus-4-7`) so the bump to `claude-opus-4-8` is one line. Same `messages.parse` / `output_format` API surface on both.
- **Decision (boundary)**: S1-29 produces + validates + records + emits; it writes NO `client_knowledge` row and owns NO summary table. S1-30 consumes `sales_summary.ready` and does the write (one transaction, shared `captured_at`, per S1-32's read contract).
- **Discovery (correction to plan)**: the plan said "no migration." Wrong - `platform_actions.platform` is the `platform_action_platform` enum (migration 0005) and `anthropic` is not a value, so writing it raised `InvalidTextRepresentationError`. Added migration `0011_platform_anthropic` (`ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'anthropic'` in an `autocommit_block`; downgrade no-op - PG cannot drop an enum value).
- **Discovery (schema constraint)**: `platform_actions.event_id` FK-references `onboarding_events`, but `transcript.linked` carries a `sales_call_transcripts` id, not an `onboarding_events` id. Passing it violated the FK - so `event_id` stays NULL and the `transcript_id` lives only in the idempotency key string (`{client_id}:anthropic:summarise_sales_call:{transcript_id}`), which is the real dedupe guard.
- **Fixed (pre-pr-review hardening)**: ran the mandatory gate (adversarial review agent + project lenses). Findings fixed: (1) the wrapper caught bare `RuntimeError` as NonRetriable, risking silently dead-lettering a genuinely transient `RuntimeError` (a closed event loop, a mid-flight drop surfaced as RuntimeError) - introduced the typed `SummaryConfigError(RuntimeError)` for the empty-key guard and narrowed the catch to it, so an unexpected RuntimeError now propagates as retriable; (2) an `already_succeeded` replay with a stored `response` that has no `summary` (an invariant violation) silently returned a clean skip with no emit - now logs a warning so it is visible rather than a quiet no-emit; (3) noted that per-`transcript_id` dedupe leans on the best-effort Inngest concurrency cap (not a DB unique-on-success constraint), so a redeploy race could double-spend one LLM call + double-emit - acceptable because **S1-30 idempotency per `transcript_id` is load-bearing** and absorbs the duplicate (recorded here so S1-30 honours it).
- **Added (tests)**: `test_sales_summary.py` (10 cases - happy + emit, schema-invalid, refusal, transport error, empty transcript / missing object both with `calls==0`, replay re-emit without a 2nd LLM call, the new replay-with-missing-stored-summary no-emit guard, `HttpAnthropicClient` request shape with `cache_control` on system + uncached transcript, typed empty-key `SummaryConfigError`, Inngest config). `test_storage_client.py` extended for `get_object` (bytes, `NoSuchKey`->`ObjectNotFound`, transient `ClientError` propagates, empty-bucket guard, Fake get/get_error).
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **362 passed** against local Postgres (`bullet_dev`, migration `0011` at head); `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard) green; `make codegen` no drift (the new Pydantic models are worker-internal, not FastAPI response models). Migration `0011` applies + round-trips at head.
- **Deferred (pre-prod follow-up)**: real `anthropic_api_key` on Render; the prompt-cache write/hit live smoke (`cache_creation_input_tokens` on call 1, `cache_read_input_tokens` on call 2 within the 5-min TTL) - only verifiable against the live API, and the §7.1 prefix may sit under Opus's 4096-token cache floor (a silent no-op, not an error). S1-30 consumes `sales_summary.ready`.

### 17/06/2026 - S1-32: PR #7 review fixes (10-lens self-review)

Addressing a 10-agent CE review pass on PR #7 (no P0/P1 blockers; the action items were P2/P3). Full technical breakdown saved to `docs/pull-requests/PR-7-s1-32-client-detail-17-06-2026.md`. On branch `feat/s1-32-client-detail`.

- **Fixed (reliability)**: the 5s detail poll (`client-detail.tsx`) gained `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(8_000)`, matching the sibling S1-31 list query. The detail view had silently dropped the fail-fast ceiling the list view added one PR earlier, so a black-holed response (LB drain mid-deploy) could hang with no client-side bound.
- **Changed (correctness hardening)**: `_GET_SUMMARY_SQL` is now `SELECT DISTINCT ON (key) ... ORDER BY key, id DESC` instead of the bare `= max(captured_at)`. If two sales-call batches ever share the EXACT same `captured_at`, the old read returned the whole tie-set: duplicate `key`s rendered twice (colliding React keys) and the "which row wins" was non-deterministic across polls. DISTINCT ON guarantees exactly one deterministic row per key. For a contract-compliant single batch (every key already unique) it is a no-op.
- **Discovery**: `client_knowledge` has NO monotonic insertion column - `id` is a random `gen_random_uuid()` and there is no `created_at` (only `captured_at`, the value that ties). So a TRUE "newest-batch-wins on a `captured_at` tie" is impossible from the read side alone; the DISTINCT ON fix makes the read deterministic and duplicate-free but cannot fully isolate two equal-`captured_at` batches that carry disjoint keys. Complete cross-batch isolation needs a monotonic batch key the S1-30 writer owns - folded into the S1-30 writer contract (the read already pins "one transaction, one shared `captured_at`").
- **Fixed**: `inngest.ts` now trims `NEXT_PUBLIC_INNGEST_URL` (`(... ?? '').trim()`) so a whitespace-only value (an operator paste typo) collapses to empty and fails closed (raw run id, no link), instead of emitting a broken relative link like `"   /run?runID=..."` against the dashboard origin.
- **Added (test)**: `test_equal_captured_at_batches_dedupe_to_one_row_per_key` - seeds two batches at an identical `captured_at` with a shared key + disjoint keys, asserts exactly one deterministic row per key across two successive reads. Fails against the old `= max()` query (duplicate `business_type` key), passes with DISTINCT ON.
- **Deferred (logged, owned as a new follow-up ticket)**: a non-latest FAILED `platform_action` can fall off the detail view's recency-ordered top-20 while the list health-badge (latest-started action) shows green - so an unresolved failure can be surfaced nowhere in the UI, in tension with the "partial failures must be visible, never silent" rule. Not reachable at Sprint-1 volumes (clients rarely have 20+ actions) and the list-badge half is pre-existing (S1-31); fix is a separate recency-independent "unresolved failures" query, ticketed before the Sprint-2 fan-outs multiply rows per client. The deferred vitest unit suite (S1-36) remains the home for the front-end pure-helper coverage (`inngestRunUrl` Cloud/unset branches, `humanizeToken`, `startedAgo`, malformed-JSONB degradation).
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api/tests/test_client_detail.py -q` -> **11 passed** against local pgvector Postgres on :5433 (the new tie test + the existing latest-capture / source-isolation / cross-client cases that exercise the modified query). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check apps/api` clean. `make codegen` unaffected (DISTINCT ON does not change the response shape - no OpenAPI/TS drift). Full `pytest apps/api` + dashboard `tsc` re-run on CI.

### 17/06/2026 - S1-27: Google Meet transcript capture + three-layer linking (backend) - code-complete

Built the S1-27 backend on `feat/s1-27-meet-transcript-capture`. Google Meet only; built against faked Google seams (live wiring deferred to pre-prod, env-only). Dashboard UI split to S1-27a.

- **Added**: migration `0010_sales_call_transcripts` - the "always capture" parking table (nullable `client_id`, `source` enum, `(source, external_id)` unique idempotency key, lowercased `participant_emails` jsonb, partial index on the unlinked backlog, GIN on emails). Also adds the partial UNIQUE index `uq_documents_transcript_text` on `documents(client_id, kind, r2_key) WHERE kind='transcript_text'` so the one-transcript-document-per-client guarantee is structural, not lock-order-incidental (hardening, see below).
- **Added**: `bullet_api/google/` seams - `credentials.py` (service-account domain-wide-delegation token), `meet_client.py` (conferenceRecord + transcript entries + participants), `calendar_client.py` (invite attendee emails - the auto-match key), `pubsub.py` (verify the Google OIDC push + parse the Workspace Event). Each has a `Fake*` double and an `Http*` impl; `google-auth[requests]` added to deps.
- **Added**: `POST /webhooks/google-meet` Pub/Sub push receiver - verify-before-write (401 fail-closed on an unconfigured/forged push), idempotent `onboarding_events` upsert, emit-then-commit (Pub/Sub is the at-least-once retry source). `include_in_schema=False`.
- **Added**: `capture_meet_transcript` Inngest worker (`google_meet.transcript_ready`) - fetch transcript -> assemble text -> store in R2 -> upsert the parking row -> attempt the immediate email auto-link. Mirrors S1-25b hardening (deterministic R2 key, broad-except transport handling via the wrapper's NonRetriable classification, per-transcript concurrency cap, `processed_at` stamped only on full success).
- **Added**: signing-time backfill (layer 2b) in `create_client_record_core` - claims every unlinked transcript matching the new client's email and emits `transcript.linked`; the emit is re-derived from committed rows so an Inngest retry re-delivers the S1-29 trigger.
- **Added**: `GET /transcripts/unlinked` + `POST /transcripts/{id}/link` (manual fallback API, layer 3); the shared `link_transcript_to_client` helper used by all three link paths; `transcript.linked` event for S1-29 to consume later. OpenAPI + TS client regenerated.
- **Decision (architecture)**: the auto-match key is the calendar-invite attendee email (the Meet participant list exposes no clean email); `participant_emails` is stored lowercased because jsonb containment is byte-exact (cannot use the citext semantics `clients.email` has).
- **Fixed (pre-pr-review hardening)**: ran the mandatory gate (independent `/code-review` + project lenses via three parallel review agents, then a confirmatory re-scan). Findings fixed: (1) the no-duplicate-`documents` guarantee was incidental (depended on link-row lock ordering) -> made structural via the partial UNIQUE index + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; (2) the signing-path `transcript.linked` emit was lost on a post-commit-pre-emit crash -> re-derived from committed rows so retries re-emit (now symmetric with the capture path); (3) the immediate-link client match was non-deterministic on equal `created_at` -> added `id DESC` tiebreak; (4) **BLOCKER** the production `HttpMeetClient`/`HttpCalendarClient`/credentials path was untested -> added `test_google_clients.py` (13 cases via `httpx.MockTransport`); (5) added end-to-end receiver-fails-closed-when-unconfigured, emit-failure-rollback, r2_key-NULL-no-document, and documents-unique-index tests; (6) the manual-link endpoint dropped `transcript.linked` on a post-commit emit failure (a same-client re-POST hit the 409 guard) -> made it idempotent: a re-POST of the SAME client returns 200 and re-emits from committed state (a different client still 409s).
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **334 passed** (was 280; +54 across `test_google_pubsub`, `test_google_clients`, `test_google_meet_webhook`, `test_meet_transcript`, `test_signing_time_transcript_link`, `test_transcripts_endpoints`; incl. a multi-invitee test proving an invite with the client + an internal moderator links only the client). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (shared + dashboard) green; `make codegen` no drift; migration `0010` applies + round-trips (downgrade/upgrade) at head.
- **Deferred (ticketed)**: dashboard UI = **S1-27a** (`353ce408`); live Google wiring (GCP project, Pub/Sub topic + Workspace Events subscription, service-account domain-wide delegation + Workspace admin consent, live smoke) = pre-prod follow-up; S1-29 consumes `transcript.linked`.

### 17/06/2026 - S1-27: UNBLOCKED + resumed (client answered platform + booking-flow); scope split to Google Meet only

S1-27 (sales-call transcript capture) is unblocked and moved back to In Progress. John/Stephen answered the two client questions the card was parked on (10/06/2026). Branch `feat/s1-27-meet-transcript-capture` off `main` (`cbd7684`).

- **Discovery (client)**: **platform is Google Meet** - "we have now moved to Google Meet for all calls." Zoom is no longer used.
- **Discovery (client)**: **booking flow reliably carries the prospect email**. Sales calls are booked through HubSpot or directly in Google Calendar, and an invite is "always via email" - "we never send Google invite links." The prospect uses the same email for the agreement as for the call "9 times out of 10," with occasional exceptions Bullet could standardise away if needed.
- **Decision (scope)**: **drop Zoom from S1-27** - build the Google Meet receiver only (the card title's "Zoom + Google Meet" is superseded). Saves building a second provider against a platform Bullet has left.
- **Decision (architecture)**: confirmed the email auto-match key is the **Google Calendar invite attendee email**, not the Meet participant list (Meet participant objects expose only an opaque signed-in user id, not a clean email; the calendar invite is the "always sent by email" path the client described). The ~90% same-email figure makes the auto-link path realistic, backed by the mandatory manual fallback for the ~10%.
- **Decision (architecture)**: the "always capture" layer needs a **parking table**, not a `documents` row - `documents.client_id` is NOT NULL (migration 0006) and a transcript arrives before the client exists. New migration `0010_sales_call_transcripts` (nullable `client_id`, backfilled at link time, mirroring how `onboarding_events.client_id` is nullable). The `documents` row (kind `transcript_text`) is created only once a transcript is linked, preserving the "every document belongs to a client" contract S1-32's detail view relies on.
- **Decision (scope)**: the manual-fallback **dashboard UI is split into a new card S1-27a** (lead's call, 17/06/2026). S1-27 is backend only: capture + auto-link (immediate + signing-time) + the unlinked-list / manual-attach API. S1-27a consumes those endpoints.
- **Decision (credentials)**: S1-27 is built entirely against **faked Google client seams** (Meet / Calendar / Pub-Sub), the same posture used for GHL (S1-25) and R2 (S1-25b). When Bullet's real Google access lands it is **env-vars only - zero code changes required**. The live wiring (Google Cloud project, Pub/Sub topic + Workspace Events subscription, service-account domain-wide delegation + Workspace admin consent, live smoke) is a tracked **pre-prod follow-up**, not a code blocker. This entry records how it was treated now so a future reader knows the faked-seam build was deliberate.
- **Plan**: `~/.claude/plans/s1-27-meet-transcript-capture.md`. Build order: migration 0010 -> `bullet_api/google/` seams -> `/webhooks/google-meet` receiver -> `capture_meet_transcript` worker -> signing-time backfill in `create_client_record_core` -> `/transcripts` endpoints -> tests -> `/pre-pr-review`.

### 17/06/2026 - S1-31: second review pass (re-review fixes)

Addressing a second PR-review pass on PR #6 (no blockers; P2/P3 hardening + test-coverage gaps), on branch `feat/s1-31-dashboard-clients-list`.

- **Fixed (security)**: `seed_safety.assert_local_seed_db()` now splits a multi-host `DATABASE_URL` and refuses unless EVERY host is local, closing a narrow bypass where `host=localhost,prod.neon.tech` passed the guard (SQLAlchemy's `make_url` captures only the first host while asyncpg could fail over to the remote second). It also now exits with code 2 (message to stderr) to match its docstring, instead of the code 1 a bare `sys.exit(<str>)` produced.
- **Added (test)**: `apps/api/tests/test_seed_safety.py` - the fail-closed guard now has direct coverage (accepts every allowlisted local host, refuses a Neon-style remote host with exit 2, refuses the multi-host-with-remote-second bypass, accepts an all-local multi-host DSN, treats a host-less unix-socket DSN as local, and `dev_seed_password` default + `DEV_SEED_PASSWORD` override). No live DB needed - the guard only parses the URL string.
- **Added (test)**: two LATERAL ordering cases in `test_clients_list.py` - the `pa.id DESC` tiebreak on an equal `started_at` (the same-`now()` fan-out flicker case) and `NULLS LAST` beating the id tiebreak when a timestamped action races a never-started (NULL `started_at`) one. The earlier suite only used distinct timestamps, so the anti-flicker ordering fix from 12/06 was itself untested. `_seed_action` gains an optional explicit `action_id` (the column defaults to a random `gen_random_uuid()`, so a test must control id order; Postgres uuid ordering matches Python's, so `sorted([uuid4, uuid4])` picks the deterministic winner).
- **Changed (docs)**: corrected the CHANGELOG date-ordering - the 10/06/2026 S1-27 entry had slipped below a 09/06/2026 entry, breaking newest-on-top; moved it up with the other 10/06 entries. Retagged the 12/06 "Scope note" bullet to the sanctioned **Decision (scope)**.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **280 passed** (was 272; +8 = 6 seed-guard cases + 2 LATERAL ordering cases) against local pgvector Postgres on :5433; migration `0009` applied cleanly (0008 -> 0009). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on the changed files. The new `test_seed_safety.py` proves the multi-host bypass is refused (exit code 2) and every allowlisted local host is accepted; the two new `test_clients_list.py` cases prove the `pa.id DESC` tiebreak (equal `started_at`) and `NULLS LAST` ordering. Python-only change - no TS touched, so typecheck/codegen are unaffected.

### 12/06/2026 - S1-31: PR review fixes (human reviewer)

- **Fixed (security blocker)**: the seed scripts could plant a known-credential `founder` login on whatever `DATABASE_URL` resolved to (incl. Neon staging/prod). New `apps/api/src/bullet_api/seed_safety.py` `assert_local_seed_db()` aborts unless the resolved DB host is local (`localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1`/`db`/`postgres`/`host.docker.internal`) - unconditional, no override flag, so a mis-pointed URL can never be seeded - and is called at the top of every seed `main()` (`seed_dev_clients`, `seed_e2e_clients`, `seed_one_client`, retrofitted `seed_e2e_users`). The dev/test password now flows through `dev_seed_password()` (overridable via `DEV_SEED_PASSWORD`; the local default is retained for DX and is unreachable off localhost thanks to the host guard). Local dev + the e2e global-setup are unaffected (their host is local). NOTE for the reviewer: we used the host-allowlist as the single load-bearing guard rather than also requiring `SEED_ALLOW=1`, because the allowlist alone closes the remote-write vector unconditionally and keeps the one-command local DX; happy to add the explicit opt-in or drop the committed default entirely if preferred.
- **Fixed (reliability)**: a 401 mid-session (the cookie expires while a tab is open and polling) no longer silently freezes the board. `createApiClient` (`packages/shared`) gains an `onUnauthorized` hook fired by an openapi-fetch response middleware on any 401 except `/me` and `/auth/*` (which own their 401s); the dashboard (`lib/api.ts`) wires it to redirect to `/login?next=...`, guarded against looping when already on `/login`.
- **Fixed (reliability)**: added timeouts on the polled path - a 5s server-side `statement_timeout` on the shared async engine (`db/session.py`, raise per-tx with `SET LOCAL` if a future op needs longer) and an `AbortSignal.timeout(8_000)` on the `/clients` fetch - so a slow/stuck query fails fast instead of piling up held connections under polling load.
- **Added**: migration `0009_ix_clients_step_entered_at` - composite index `clients(step_entered_at DESC, id)` matching the list's `ORDER BY`, so the per-poll sort stays index-only as the client count grows. (Supersedes the original entry's "no migration" line; that line is corrected above.)
- **Fixed**: the LATERAL "latest action" now orders `started_at DESC NULLS LAST, pa.id DESC` so a same-`now()` fan-out batch (or all-NULL `started_at`) cannot flicker the health badge between polls.
- **Changed (test)**: `lib/format.ts`'s `dead_lettered -> danger` mapping now has live coverage - `seed_e2e_clients.py` gives E2E Charlie a `dead_lettered` last action and `clients-list.spec.ts` asserts the rendered badge carries the danger (red) styling. Addresses the review point that this branching helper was the one untested unit in the diff. (Full `format.ts` unit tests remain proposed S1-36 / vitest.)
- **Fixed**: `seed_one_client.py --step` is validated against `CURRENT_STEP_VALUES` (argparse `choices`), so a typo aborts with a clear message instead of a Postgres `22P02` that `global-setup.ts` mislabels as a DATABASE_URL/migrations failure.
- **Changed**: the four seed scripts reuse the shared `AsyncSessionLocal`/`engine` instead of each hand-rolling `create_async_engine` + `NullPool`, so SSL + the new timeout config live in one place.
- **Fixed (a11y / nits)**: `scope="col"` on the list table headers; the last-action sub-label now guards on `last_action_platform` (the value it leads with) rather than `last_action`; corrected the inaccurate "time-in-step ticks without a refetch" wording in the `ClientListItem` docstring + `format.ts` (it recomputes per poll, there is no timer).
- **Decision (deferred, owned)**: typing `current_step` / `last_action_status` as a shared `Literal`/`StrEnum` sourced from `db/enums.py` (instead of `str` triplicated across the DB enum, Pydantic, and `format.ts`) is **proposed S1-37** - it spans the Python enum module + the generated TS, so it is a focused fast-follow, not this fix-up. The `/clients/{id}` row link stays (it 404s only in the gap before S1-32, which is built and lands immediately after).
- **Decision (scope)**: the `pre-pr-review` SKILL.md hardening is its own commit (`0759360`) and the S1-27 "parked" CHANGELOG note is unrelated to this feature - both will be called out in the PR description (or the skill commit lifted to a separate PR) so the feature diff reverts cleanly.
- **Verified**: re-ran after the fixes - `uv run pytest apps/api -q` green, `ruff check`/`format` clean, `make typecheck` (dashboard + shared) green, `make codegen` no drift, migration `0009` applies + round-trips, Playwright e2e green (incl. the new dead_lettered-badge assertion), seed host-guard refuses a non-local host and passes localhost.

### 10/06/2026 - S1-32: dashboard /clients/[id] detail view (Sprint 1 slice)

- **Added**: `GET /clients/{client_id}` (extends `clients/router.py`) - the whole detail page in one payload so the page is a single 5s poll: client metadata + all 15 platform id columns, `current_step`/`step_entered_at`, the latest sales-call summary batch from `client_knowledge`, and the last 20 `platform_actions` (newest first by `started_at DESC NULLS LAST`). 404 for unknown AND malformed (non-UUID) ids - a bad path segment is an id we will never have, not a 500. 401 unauthenticated. New schemas `ClientDetailResponse` / `KnowledgeEntry` / `PlatformActionItem`; OpenAPI + TS client regenerated.
- **Added**: dashboard `/clients/[id]` page (`app/clients/[id]/page.tsx` via `useParams`, per the `confirm/[token]` precedent) + `components/client-detail.tsx`: header card (name, contact, step badge, time-in-step), AI sales summary panel, platform links grid, recent-actions table with per-action Inngest "View run" links. 5s `refetchInterval`. Theme-matched to S1-31 (tokens + `Badge`/`Alert` reuse); loading skeleton, "no sales summary yet", "client not found", and error states are first-class. All three states screenshot-verified locally.
- **Decision (the S1-30 no-rework contract, PINNED)**: the summary panel reads `client_knowledge` rows exactly as S1-30 must write them per its card + PRD §7.1 - `source='sales_call'`, ONE row per top-level field, `key` = the §7.1 field name (`business_type`, `business_goals`, `budget_range_usd`, `pain_points`, `red_flags`, `next_steps`, `notable_quotes`), `value` = the §7.1 JSONB shape, `value_text` prose fallback, batches stamped by `captured_at`. **Latest-capture-wins**: only the most recent `captured_at` batch is returned (re-summarising never mixes two captures). Unknown keys render generically so a §7.1 addition is not a dashboard hotfix. S1-30's implementation MUST follow this key/value contract - tests seed it verbatim.
- **Decision**: Inngest run deep-links are dashboard-side URL policy, not API data - the API returns the raw `platform_actions.inngest_run_id` and the dashboard builds `{NEXT_PUBLIC_INNGEST_URL}/run?runID={id}` (new env var; local default `http://localhost:8288` = the docker Inngest dev server; render.yaml gains a `sync:false` slot per environment - paste the Inngest Cloud app URL before staging acceptance).
- **Decision**: platform deep-links are id-driven with placeholders by design (per the card): PandaDoc / Asana / Stripe / Drive get real hrefs (stable public URL patterns); GHL / HubSpot / Xero / Timely / Meta / Sheets / Slack render as id-chips ("connected") or muted "not connected" chips - all in one `lib/platform-links.ts` map to upgrade as integrations land.
- **Discovery**: the PRD §8 detail page also lists the follow-up email draft and the asset checklist - those belong to the Sprint 2 email generator and S3-09 (`client_assets`) respectively, NOT this card's Sprint 1 slice. Recorded as explicit non-goals.
- **Added**: tests - `tests/test_client_detail.py` (7: full-shape incl. §7.1 JSONB round-trip, latest-batch-wins, empty summary, action ordering + 20-cap, unknown-UUID 404, malformed-id 404, 401) and `e2e/client-detail.spec.ts` (list->detail navigation, structured summary fields render, "no summary yet" state, exact Inngest href). Seeds extended: `seed_dev_clients.py` gives PeakFit Studio a full §7.1 batch + an `inngest_run_id`; `seed_e2e_clients.py` gives Bravo the same (Alpha deliberately summary-less). `humanizeToken` gains brand-correct labels (GHL, HubSpot, PandaDoc, Google *).
- **Fixed (accessibility audit, user-requested)**: the "not connected" platform chips dropped to ~3.5:1 contrast (`opacity-60` over already-muted text, below the WCAG AA 4.5:1 floor for small text) - opacity removed, a dashed border now carries the "inactive" signal at ~8:1 contrast. External links (platform deep-links, "View run") gained `sr-only` "(opens in a new tab)" text with the arrow glyph `aria-hidden`; both tables' headers gained `scope="col"`; both loading skeletons announce via `role="status"` + sr-only text while the shimmer is `aria-hidden`. Already correct and left as-is: status badges pair colour with a text label (never colour-only), heading hierarchy (h1 -> h2), `role="alert"` on error alerts.
- **Fixed (/pre-pr-review gate, 3 adversarial lens agents - 0 P1, 6 P2, ~12 P3, ALL addressed)**:
  - *Perpetual 404 polling*: a not-found client kept being re-fetched every 5s forever (TanStack v5 `refetchInterval` fires independently of `retry`); now the function form stops the interval when the error is `not_found`.
  - *Transient blip blanked the page*: `isError` was checked before cached data, so one failed background poll replaced a fully rendered detail page with the generic error; now the generic error renders only when there is no cached payload (matches the clients-list posture), while not-found stays terminal.
  - *Inngest Cloud URL shape*: `/run?runID=` is the DEV-SERVER route; Inngest Cloud uses path-shaped `/runs/<id>`. `inngestRunUrl` now branches on an `app.inngest.com` base, and **fails closed**: a production build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_INNGEST_URL` unset/empty renders the raw run id instead of linking to an operator's localhost (`||` not `??`, so an empty Render slot behaves too). render.yaml/.env.example comments corrected; the e2e webServer pins the var so the href assertion is self-contained.
  - *Untested WHERE clauses*: added API tests proving non-sales_call sources (e.g. portal) never leak into the summary even when newer, cross-client isolation (client A's payload contains none of client B's rows), and the `NULLS LAST` branch (never-started action appears last, `last_error` passes through). 282 tests total.
  - *S1-30 batch-atomicity clause added to the pinned contract*: a summary batch MUST be written in ONE transaction with ONE shared `captured_at` - the single-statement read sees an atomic batch whole, but a per-row-committing writer could expose a partial latest batch mid-write. Recorded in the router comment + here; both seeds comply.
  - *Dev-seed prod guard*: the seed scripts refuse any non-local database host (they plant a founder login). NOTE: S1-32's original guard here was superseded by the shared `bullet_api.seed_safety.assert_local_seed_db()` that landed with the S1-31 review fixes (now in `main`); this branch's seeds just call it.
  - *Rendering hardening*: a `[null]`/string element inside `notable_quotes` no longer white-screens the page; `NaN`/negative quote timestamps render nothing; "just now ago" grammar fixed; duplicate unknown summary keys deduped (React key collision); `text-red-200/80` opacity dimming removed (same WCAG rationale as the chips); budget renders with a pinned `en-US` locale so the e2e assertion is deterministic; `platform-links.key` narrowed to nullable-string columns only; the list skeleton's live region moved outside the `<table>` (a `tr[role=status]` breaks required ARIA table structure).
- **Deferred (logged, owned)**: "showing latest 20 of N" actions affordance -> Sprint 2 when fan-outs multiply rows per client; Stripe deep-links assume live-mode objects (test-mode needs `/test/`) -> the Stripe integration ticket; the vitest unit-test follow-up now ALSO covers the new pure helpers (`formatTimestamp`, `startedAgo`, `inngestRunUrl`, `PLATFORM_LINKS` builders, the `TOKEN_LABELS` branch) alongside S1-31's `format.ts` deferral.
- **Verified**: after all review fixes - `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **282 passed** (+10 total this ticket); Playwright e2e **4/4** green; `ruff check`/`format` clean; `tsc` green (dashboard + shared); `make codegen` no drift; both seeds re-run twice with stable row counts (7 knowledge rows, no duplicate actions); the seed prod-guard verified to refuse a non-local host and pass localhost. NOTE: work is held as UNCOMMITTED changes on the S1-31 branch per the agreed branch posture (no stacked PR); `feat/s1-32-client-detail` is cut off `main` after the S1-31 merge, then the card moves to To Review.

### 10/06/2026 - S1-31: dashboard /clients list view

- **Added**: `GET /clients` read endpoint - new `clients/` package (`apps/api/src/bullet_api/clients/router.py`, registered in `main.py`) returning every client with identity (business / contact + email), `current_step`, `step_entered_at`, and the status + platform + action of its most-recently-started `platform_actions` row. Auth = `get_current_user` (any authenticated internal user, same posture as `/me`; the dashboard is single-tenant). One statement, no N+1: a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` picks each client's latest action (`LEFT` so a client with no actions still appears, null `last_action_*`). New `ClientListItem` / `ClientsListResponse` Pydantic models in `schemas.py`; OpenAPI + TS client regenerated (`make codegen`, committed).
- **Added**: dashboard `/clients` board (`apps/dashboard`) - `clients-table.tsx` (TanStack Query, `refetchInterval` 10s, mirroring `health-status.tsx`) replaces the placeholder on the already-login-gated page; new CVA `ui/badge.tsx` (same `cva()`+`cn()` style as `button`/`alert`, palette shared with `alert` - no new colours) for step + action-status pills; `lib/format.ts` (step labels, browser-side time-in-step, status->variant). Polished loading (skeleton), empty ("No clients yet"), and error (`Alert`) states. Visually verified on-theme against the dev seed.
- **Added**: seed scripts - `scripts/seed_dev_clients.py` (5 sample gym clients across steps + mixed `platform_actions`, plus a confirmed dev founder login, so the board is viewable locally with data; idempotent), `scripts/seed_e2e_clients.py` (3 deterministic clients, run by Playwright global-setup), `scripts/seed_one_client.py` (inserts one client mid-test). All upsert on the unique `clients.pandadoc_document_id` since `clients.email` is intentionally not unique.
