- **Added**: tests - `tests/test_clients_list.py` (5 cases: steps + DESC ordering, most-recently-started action wins, no-actions->null, envelope shape, 401 unauthenticated; each tags rows with a per-test run id so it is robust to pre-existing data) and `e2e/clients-list.spec.ts` (3 seeded clients render; a 4th inserted server-side appears via the 10s poll within the window). Updated `e2e/login.spec.ts` assertion (the old "Client list" placeholder text it checked was removed) to assert the Clients heading.
- **Decision**: no NEW columns needed - `clients.step_entered_at` already backs time-in-step and `platform_actions.status` backs last-action health. (A supporting index on `clients(step_entered_at DESC, id)` was later added in migration 0009 per PR review - see the 12/06/2026 fixes entry.) Time-in-step is returned as the raw `step_entered_at` timestamp and formatted in the browser, recomputed on each poll.
- **Decision**: the row links to `/clients/{id}` now, ahead of S1-32 building that page (it 404s until then) - the link is the contract S1-32 fulfils. Non-goals this card: the detail page, pagination/search/sort, SSE.
- **Decision (for the reviewer)**: built **client-rendered** (TanStack Query + 10s poll) rather than strictly server-rendered as the card wording says. The card's own "10s polling" requirement needs the client to keep re-fetching (SSR gives a single frozen snapshot), and the whole dashboard already renders client-side behind the `AuthenticatedShell` cookie guard (S1-17/S1-18 pattern; S1-32 also specifies polling). The only practical difference is a brief loading skeleton on first paint; everything after is identical. Recorded so the literal-wording deviation is a conscious call, not an oversight.
- **Changed**: client name + email cells truncate with an ellipsis (full value on hover) so a long business name does not stretch the column.
- **Discovery**: `platform_actions` has no `created_at` column (only nullable `started_at` / `completed_at`), so "most recent action" orders by `started_at DESC NULLS LAST` (the action's chronological anchor; there is an `ix_platform_actions_started_at_desc` index).
- **Fixed (pre-PR review, failure-path lens)**: `clients-table.tsx` only blanks the board to the error state on the INITIAL load (`isError && !data`); a transient background-poll failure now keeps the last good rows on screen and recovers on the next poll, instead of flashing a full-table error every time a 10s poll hiccups (the header API-status dot already signals connectivity).
- **Decision (deferral, pre-PR review pure-helper lens)**: direct unit tests for `lib/format.ts` (time-in-step boundaries, NaN/unknown-step/null-status edges) are owned by **proposed S1-36** (introduce a vitest JS unit runner for the dashboard, alongside the already-parked `next lint` flat-config follow-up). The dashboard has no JS unit runner yet (only Playwright e2e + `tsc`); the happy paths are covered by the e2e + visual check and the edges are simple defensive guards (NaN -> `-`, unknown step passthrough, null -> neutral badge). (Per PR review the `dead_lettered -> danger` branch now also has live e2e coverage; see the 12/06/2026 fixes entry.)
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> **272 passed** (was 267; +5). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean. `make typecheck` (dashboard + shared `tsc --noEmit`) green. `make codegen` produces no drift. Playwright e2e **3/3 green** (incl. the new clients-list poll spec). Dev seed run twice -> idempotent (5 clients, 8 actions, no dupes).

### 10/06/2026 - pre-pr-review skill hardened from the S1-25c (PR #5) review gap

- **Changed**: `.claude/skills/pre-pr-review/SKILL.md` gains the lenses the gate missed on S1-25c even though it was run. New **section 6 "Rename / redefinition completeness"**: a rename is not done until every *string* is updated - error/exception messages, log lines, docstrings, comments, `render.yaml` NOTE blocks, `config.py` headers, `.env.example` - with an operator-facing rule that any failure message must name a thing that currently exists and is actionable; plus "alias a repeated `Literal` union once, don't re-spell it per call site". New test-matrix rows: **degenerate-config / fail-closed** (multi-config changes must test the one-configured + none-configured permutations and assert fail-closed) and **sequential fan-out loop isolation** (a later item's failure must not undo an earlier item's committed work). The **replay** row now requires a new-non-key-column case (prove a descriptive column is metadata, not identity), and the **deferral bar** is tightened - a test may not be deferred because the harness is unfamiliar (branching inside an Inngest wrapper is testable via `fn._handler`).
- **Discovery**: PR #5's findings were all P2/P3 but all of a kind the gate *should* catch: stale `PANDADOC_API_KEY` env-var name left in three live `RuntimeError` strings + docs after the `_UK`/`_INT` rename (operator pointed at a deleted variable), four missing tests (cross-account replay idempotency, the `store_signed_pdf` wrapper account->key selection that had been deferred too cheaply, only-one-account-configured webhook 401, reconcile `_run` per-account isolation), and a re-spelled `Literal["uk","int"]`. Root cause: the lenses were applied to logic but not to operator-facing strings, degenerate-config permutations, or a deferral whose precedent (`._handler`) already existed. Source fixes landed on the branch as `4bc03e3` before merge; this entry hardens the gate so the next rename/multi-config diff catches them pre-review.

### 10/06/2026 - S1-27: sales-call transcript capture - design agreed, BLOCKED pending client decision

- **Decision**: S1-27 is **parked** (Flow card moved out of In Progress back to Sprint 1, tagged `Blocked`) pending two client answers from John/Stephen, so we do not build against an assumption. (a) **Platform** - settling on Google Meet, staying on Zoom, or still deciding (+ timeline); decides which provider receiver we build first. (b) **Booking flow** - does the prospect's email reliably reach the recorded meeting (HubSpot/Calendly/calendar invite vs an ad-hoc Zoom link); decides how often the manual fallback fires and whether a booking-side change is worth proposing. The lead has emailed the client; resume on their reply.
- **Decision**: agreed the capture/linking shape (no code yet) - **three layers**: (1) always capture (store every transcript in R2 + the `documents` row the moment a recording-ready webhook fires, matched or not, so no call is ever lost); (2) auto-link by email (match a call participant email to `clients.email` at signing time and back-attach parked transcripts); (3) manual fallback (a dashboard "unlinked transcripts" list a human assigns to the right client). Email is the only viable auto-match key; the manual fallback is mandatory, not optional, because email matching is not bulletproof.
- **Discovery**: the linkage asymmetry that drives the whole design. The sales call happens BEFORE the client record exists (call -> agreement -> sign -> `pandadoc.signed` creates the row in S1-25a), and `documents.client_id` is NOT NULL, so a fresh transcript usually has no client to attach to - linking must be deferred to signing time. The **signing side is the reliable end**: at signing we already capture both the client email and their HubSpot contact id for free from the PandaDoc document metadata (`hubspot.contact_id`, stored on `clients.hubspot_contact_id` per S1-25a). The **call side is the weak end**: a Zoom/Meet recording-ready webhook only ever carries an email, and only if the prospect joined signed-in; it never carries a HubSpot id. So a HubSpot-id auto-match is impossible from the call payload, and the booking-flow question (b) is what determines email reliability.
- **Discovery**: `document_kind` already includes `transcript_text` (`apps/api/src/bullet_api/db/enums.py`, declared at S1-10), so the eventual build needs no enum migration. The "no transcript -> S1-28 path" in the card text is the Whisper audio-only fallback (plan S5); there is no S1-28 card, so that branch is a future ticket, not part of S1-27.

### 09/06/2026 - S1-25c: PR #5 review hardening

Addressing the PR #5 self-review findings (all P2/P3; no blockers) before merge, on branch `feat/s1-25c-pandadoc-dual-account`.

- **Fixed**: the three live `RuntimeError` strings that still named the removed `PANDADOC_API_KEY` env var (`pandadoc/client.py` fetch + download, `integrations/pandadoc_client.py` list) now read "PandaDoc API key is empty ... Set PANDADOC_API_KEY_UK / PANDADOC_API_KEY_INT on the Render env group" - so an operator hitting an empty-key failure during the env-var rename this PR requires is pointed at a variable that exists. Synced the pinned `match=` / substring assertions in `test_pandadoc_client.py` + `test_create_client_record.py`.
- **Changed**: extracted the account-label union into a single `PandaDocAccount = Literal["uk", "int"]` alias in `pandadoc/accounts.py` (exported via `__all__` + the package `__init__`); the replay endpoint param (`admin/pandadoc.py`) and `get_pandadoc_client` (`pandadoc/client.py`) import it instead of each re-spelling the literal. Internal `account: str` params deliberately left as `str` - they take untrusted runtime event data and rely on `_creds_for` raising on an unknown label.
- **Added**: the four test gaps the review flagged. (1) cross-account replay idempotency - replaying one document as `uk` then `int` persists exactly one row, keeps the first writer's account, emits once (proves account is metadata, not identity); (2) the `store_signed_pdf` Inngest wrapper's `account -> api_key_for -> HttpPandaDocClient` selection, asserting an `int` event builds the INT-keyed client and a missing account defaults to UK (closes the deferral logged in the entry below, invoking the wrapper via its `._handler`); (3) only-one-account-configured at the webhook endpoint - an INT-signed delivery is rejected 401 when only the UK secret is set, plus the inverse, so a single-account deploy fails closed; (4) reconcile `_run` per-account isolation - a later account raising does not undo an earlier account's committed pass and the failure propagates.
- **Changed**: doc/comment cleanup of the remaining stale `PANDADOC_API_KEY` references - the reconcile module docstring + `render.yaml` cron NOTE now say "no PandaDoc account has an API key", the `config.py` section header reads "S1-23/S1-24 PandaDoc API base URL + reconciliation cron", and the `worker/client_record.py` + `worker/signed_pdf.py` docstrings/comments say "PandaDoc API key".
- **Decision**: the reconcile shared-session finding needed NO code change - each per-account `reconcile_pandadoc` already commits at its end and the docstring already documents that accounts are not all-or-nothing; that guarantee is now locked in by the new `_run` isolation test rather than refactoring to a per-account session.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest apps/api -q` (local docker Postgres on :5433) -> **267 passed**, 0 failed (was 260; +7). `ruff check apps/api` + `ruff format --check apps/api` clean (91 files). `make typecheck` is TS-only and unaffected (no TS changes; Python mypy still gated behind the S1-33 TODO). The `/pre-pr-review` hardening lenses were applied to the diff directly in the worktree: the shared seams (the error-string reword + the `PandaDocAccount` alias) were audited against every caller via grep + the full green suite, and the new tests cover the success + failure (401 fail-closed, isolation-on-failure) + replay (cross-account dedupe) matrix.
- **Verified**: CI run 27204092208 on the PR's HEAD commit (4bc03e3) is 5/5 GREEN - Python tests (9m10s), TypeScript lint + typecheck, OpenAPI codegen drift check, Pre-commit hooks, Python lint + typecheck.

### 09/06/2026 - S1-25c: PandaDoc dual-account routing (UK + International)

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/pandadoc/accounts.py` - the single source of truth for the two PandaDoc accounts. Constants `PANDADOC_ACCOUNT_UK="uk"` / `PANDADOC_ACCOUNT_INT="int"` + `PANDADOC_ACCOUNTS`; `api_key_for(account, settings)` (raises on unknown account), `webhook_secrets(settings)` (returns `{account: secret}` for accounts with a non-empty secret, UK first), `api_accounts(settings)` (accounts with a non-empty API key, for the reconcile loop). Every part of the PandaDoc path resolves credentials through this one helper.
- **Added**: `resolve_pandadoc_account(raw_body, signature, secrets)` in `webhooks/pandadoc_core.py` - the try-both-secrets router: verifies the incoming HMAC against each configured account's shared key and returns whichever verifies (else None). Pure in-memory hashing (microseconds, no extra API calls); HMAC-SHA256 uniquely identifies the signing account.
- **Added**: migration `0008_oe_pandadoc_account` - `onboarding_events.pandadoc_account TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'uk'` (existing single-account rows are UK by definition; `ADD COLUMN ... DEFAULT <const>` is metadata-only on PG 11+). Revision id kept <=32 chars to fit `alembic_version.version_num VARCHAR(32)`. Round-trip (downgrade/upgrade) verified.
- **Changed**: the whole PandaDoc path is now per-account. Webhook (`webhooks/pandadoc.py`) tries both shared keys, routes by which verifies (401 if none), and stamps the account on the `onboarding_events` row + the emitted `pandadoc.signed` event. `account` is threaded `pandadoc.signed` -> `client.created` (defaulting to `uk` when absent, so in-flight pre-S1-25c events keep working) so the async workers (`worker/client_record.py`, `worker/signed_pdf.py`) build their `HttpPandaDocClient` with `api_key_for(account)`. The reconcile cron (`crons/reconcile_pandadoc.py`) loops every account that has a key, stamping each healed row/event/Slack-alert with its account; `main()` disables only when NO account has a key. The replay endpoint (`admin/pandadoc.py`) takes a validated `?account=uk|int` query param (default uk; 422 otherwise) and `get_pandadoc_client(account)` selects the matching key.
- **Changed**: config split into explicit per-account settings - `pandadoc_api_key` -> `pandadoc_api_key_uk`, `pandadoc_webhook_secret` -> `pandadoc_webhook_secret_uk`, plus new `pandadoc_api_key_int` / `pandadoc_webhook_secret_int` (`config.py`, `.env`, `.env.example`, `render.yaml` env-group slots). **Deploy note**: the Render staging/prod env groups must rename `PANDADOC_API_KEY` -> `PANDADOC_API_KEY_UK` (+ `PANDADOC_WEBHOOK_SECRET` -> `_UK`) and add the two `_INT` vars before the deploy that ships this.
- **Decision**: routing is **try-both-secrets on the single existing endpoint** (Option A), NOT per-account webhook URLs. It needs no change to either PandaDoc account's webhook subscription (zero coordination on a live account) and is equally fast. Per-account URLs were rejected because they would require editing the International account's live webhook URL.
- **Decision**: the `onboarding_events` idempotency key stays `(event_type, external_id)` - `pandadoc_account` is deliberately NOT in it. PandaDoc document ids are globally unique opaque ids, so a UK doc and an INT doc can never collide; account is descriptive metadata, not identity. Putting it in the key would be actively wrong: a replay of one document with the wrong `?account=` would then create a second row for one signing.
- **Discovery**: confirmed the UK vs International agreement templates via a read-only API diff (both keys in hand). UK template `Kn7vBp56MLSxreXPXwpNWk` ("NEW GYM CLIENT ... Partnership Agreement"), INT template `rQ9jQ6f4dcP2jjmCfF3H6Y` ("NEW FITNESS FACILITY CLIENT ... Non-UK"). EVERY field `extract_client_fields` reads is present and identically named in both: tokens `Client.Email/FirstName/LastName/Phone`, `Company.Name/Address/State/Zip/Country`, `Deal.Currency`, `Deal.MonthlyServiceFee`, and the form-field title `Enter Your Company's Legal Trading Name`. The only differences are fields we do NOT read (INT adds a `Company.City` token; UK adds an `Enter VAT Number` field). Conclusion: **one shared `clients_payload.extract_client_fields` covers both accounts - no per-template branch needed.** `Deal.Currency` is read as-is, so INT's non-GBP currency flows through correctly.
- **Fixed (self-review, pre-PR)**: `apps/api/scripts/inspect_pandadoc_document.py` referenced the removed `settings.pandadoc_api_key` (would `AttributeError`). Made account-aware (`--account=uk|int`, default uk; uses `api_key_for`).
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest .` (apps/api, local docker Postgres) -> **260 passed**, 0 failed (was 247). New: `test_pandadoc_accounts.py` (accounts helper), `resolve_pandadoc_account` cases in `test_pandadoc_core.py`, webhook UK/INT routing + wrong-key-401 + account-stamp in `test_pandadoc_webhook.py`, account propagation in `test_create_client_record.py`, account stamping + the `_run` per-account loop in `test_reconcile_pandadoc.py`, and `?account=int`/invalid-account in `test_pandadoc_replay.py`. `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean; `make typecheck` (TS) green; `alembic upgrade head` applies 0008 then round-trips. **/pre-pr-review** hardening gate run (3 finder angles): fixed the stale inspect script; documented the globally-unique-doc-id rationale for the idempotency key (refuting a "cross-account collision" finding); documented the reconcile per-account partial-failure posture (healing cron, idempotent next run); added the `_run` loop test. **Consciously deferred**: a direct invocation test of the `store_signed_pdf` Inngest wrapper's account->key selection - the project has no precedent for invoking decorated Inngest wrappers in tests (they are asserted via `get_config`), and the new logic is composed of unit-tested pieces (`api_key_for` unit-tested; `client_record`'s identical account-threading tested end-to-end through its core; `get_pandadoc_client` account selection exercised via the replay tests). Residual = 2 lines of `ctx.event.data.get("account", "uk")` boilerplate symmetric to `client_record`.
- **NOT verified live**: no full live INT signing smoke (webhook -> fetch -> fan-out) was run; only the read-only template diff. Both `_INT` env vars + the `_UK` renames must be set on the Render env groups before rollout. The local `.env` now holds both real API keys (gitignored, never committed).

### 09/06/2026 - S1-26: returning-client check + `parent_client_id` linking

- **Added**: returning-client resolution in `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/ghl_subaccount.py` `create_ghl_subaccount_core`, run BEFORE provisioning a GHL sub-account, so a returning client (same email signs again for a second gym/site) reuses the existing sub-account instead of getting a duplicate. Two checks: (1) **local DB sibling** - a prior `clients` row with the same email (citext, case-insensitive) that already holds a `ghl_subaccount_id`; the new row is linked via `parent_client_id` to the original ROOT (`COALESCE(parent_client_id, id)`, keeping a flat two-level tree, root stable across multiple returning signings) and the id is reused, with no GHL call; (2) no DB sibling, so a **live GHL lookup-by-email** runs on EVERY create attempt before the POST. `CreateSubaccountResult` gains `parent_client_id`; the Inngest wrapper return dict carries it too.
- **Added**: `find_location_by_email(email, *, company_id) -> GhlLocation | None` on the GHL client seam (`apps/api/src/bullet_api/ghl/client.py`) - Protocol + `HttpGhlClient` (`GET {base}/locations/search?companyId=&email=&limit=1`, empty list / 404 -> `None`, `429`/`5xx` -> `GhlServerError`, other `4xx` -> `GhlClientError`, empty key -> loud `RuntimeError`) + `FakeGhlClient` (`lookup_result` / `lookup_error` / records `lookup_calls`; default `None` so existing create-path tests proceed to create unchanged). `GHL_SKIP_REASON_DB_SIBLING` / `GHL_SKIP_REASON_GHL_LOOKUP` constants added.
- **Changed**: `create_ghl_subaccount` declares a THIRD Inngest concurrency cap, `Concurrency(key="event.data.email", limit=1, scope="fn")` (alongside the global `limit=3` + per-client `limit=1`). Function-level concurrency holds the slot for the whole run (no `step.run` boundary), so two DIFFERENT client rows sharing an email are serialised - the second starts only after the first commits its `ghl_subaccount_id`, then finds it as a committed DB sibling. Residual: the cap key is a raw string while `clients.email` is citext, so different casings ("X@y.com" vs "x@Y.COM") are not serialised here; the citext DB sibling SELECT + the GHL lookup are the correctness backstop for that narrow case.
- **Decision**: the `skipped_existing` outcome is recorded as `platform_actions.status = 'success'` + a `response = {"skipped_existing": true, "reason": "db_sibling"|"ghl_lookup", ...}` marker + `external_id` = the reused sub-account id - NOT a new `platform_action_status` enum value (one of the two riders S1-25 carried into this card). This matches the convention S1-25's already-provisioned skip already uses (success + `response` note), needs no migration, and keeps the dashboard's terminal-good semantics consistent across every fan-out. A reuse is genuinely a terminal success against the surviving id; the marker carries the audit detail.
- **Decision**: full scope - DB sibling check AND the live GHL lookup on every attempt (the other S1-25 rider). The lookup catches a client that exists in GHL but not our DB, and closes S1-25's at-least-once duplicate-create window: a retry after a lost create response finds the orphaned location (it carries the client's email) and reuses it instead of POSTing a second one. No migration: `clients.parent_client_id` + `ix_clients_parent_client_id` exist since 0002, and `clients.email` is citext and intentionally NOT unique for exactly this returning-client case.
- **Fixed (self-review, pre-PR)**: the SHARED `worker/platform_actions.py` `complete_action` now also sets `last_error = NULL`. The S1-26 at-least-once recovery path transitions a `failed` action (a create whose response was lost) to `success` via `complete_action`; without clearing it, the now-green row kept the stale `last_error` ("response lost"), so the dashboard would show a successful action carrying an error string. Benefits every fan-out that can recover a failed action. Locked with an assertion in the at-least-once test. Caught by the `/pre-pr-review` gate.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest .` (from `apps/api`, local docker Postgres) -> **245 passed**, 0 failed (was 223). New: `test_ghl_client.py` +12 `find_location_by_email` cases (success parse, exact URL+params, empty list / missing key / 404 -> None, empty-key RuntimeError, 4xx -> GhlClientError, 429/5xx -> GhlServerError); `test_ghl_subaccount.py` +8 (db_sibling reuse + parent link, db_sibling replay idempotency, link-to-root-not-intermediate, GHL-lookup reuse with no sibling, at-least-once window closed on retry, fresh-create-runs-lookup-first, lookup transport-error -> failed, lookup GhlServerError -> failed) + the 3-cap concurrency assertion. Branch-coverage audit (transient `pytest-cov`) of the changed modules: `platform_actions.py` 100%, `ghl/client.py` 96%, `ghl_subaccount.py` 82% - every uncovered line is either an unreachable torn-state `already_succeeded` guard (Gate 1 catches all replays first), the thin Inngest wrapper (untested by design, same posture as S1-25), the `get_ghl_client()` factory, or a pre-existing `_build_location_payload` optional-field branch; no real success/failure behaviour is left untested. `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean (88 files); `make typecheck` (TS) green. `alembic upgrade head` a no-op (no new migration). **/pre-pr-review** hardening gate run: 4 finder angles, one shared-seam audit fix applied (above); the per-email-cap "HIGH" finding refuted (Inngest fn-level concurrency holds the slot for the whole run, not just delivery); stale-sibling-id (GHL location deleted out-of-band) deferred to a future reconciliation ticket (pre-existing posture, S1-25's short-circuit reuses a stored id without a liveness check too).
- **NOT verified live**: no GHL creds / sandbox, so `find_location_by_email`'s real HTTP shape (path, params, response envelope - marked `CONFIRM PRE-PROD` in code) is unconfirmed against the live API; same deferred posture as S1-25 `create_location`. `GHL_AGENCY_API_KEY` + `GHL_COMPANY_ID` must be set on the Render env groups before rollout.

### 08/06/2026 - Pre-PR hardening self-review (skill + lifecycle gate)

- **Added**: `.claude/skills/pre-pr-review/SKILL.md` - a user-invocable deep self-review run on a ticket's diff before it goes to review. Codifies the project's hardening lenses so they are applied consistently: shared/reused seams judged against all callers + concurrent use (not just the first caller or its protective cap), failure paths including transport-level errors (timeouts / connection resets, not only typed errors), idempotency / replay / at-least-once windows, DB-correctness, project-future awareness, and a success-AND-failure-AND-replay-AND-concurrency test matrix. Every finding is fixed (or deferred with a logged reason) and the suite re-run green before the PR.
- **Changed**: `CLAUDE.md` "Ticket lifecycle" - added the `/pre-pr-review` gate as a required step of the code-complete ritual (renumbered the subsequent steps; the completion-comment verification now cites the review pass).
- **Decision**: a deep self-review pass is a standard pre-PR step on every ticket, raising the bar on concurrency, failure-path, and idempotency coverage before code reaches review.

### 08/06/2026 - S1-25b: store signed PDF in R2 + `documents` on `client.created`

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/storage/` package with `client.py` - the Cloudflare R2 (S3-compatible) storage seam, mirroring the PandaDoc / GHL client seams. `StorageClient` Protocol (`put_object(key, body, content_type) -> url`), production `R2StorageClient` (boto3 S3 client pointed at the R2 endpoint; the blocking `put_object` runs via `asyncio.to_thread` so the event loop is not stalled), and `FakeStorageClient` test double recording `puts`. `get_s3_client()` is a **`functools.lru_cache` singleton** - ONE boto3 client per process, reused across every invocation (client construction loads service models + resolves creds and owns a urllib3 pool, so per-call construction is wasteful). Verified safe for our architecture: the API is a single uvicorn process with Inngest served in-process, boto3 *clients* are thread-safe (so sharing across the `to_thread` threads is fine), and the singleton is per-process so `--workers N` / multiple instances each get exactly one (you cannot share a client across processes anyway). Empty creds -> `RuntimeError` loud-fail.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/signed_pdf.py` - the S1-25b Inngest function `store_signed_pdf`, triggered by `client.created` (sibling of S1-25's GHL creation, runs in parallel). Pure core `store_signed_pdf_core(session, pandadoc_client, storage, *, client_id, onboarding_event_id, document_id, inngest_run_id)`: records an `in_progress` `platform_actions` row (platform=`pandadoc`, action=`store_signed_pdf`) and **commits before the external calls**; downloads the signed PDF from PandaDoc; uploads the bytes to R2 at the deterministic key `signed-agreements/{client_id}/{document_id}.pdf`; then writes a `documents` row (kind `pandadoc_signed_pdf`, `r2_key`, `external_url`, `metadata={document_id,content_type,size_bytes,source}`) via a **guarded `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS`** (idempotent with no migration), marks the action `success` (external_id = r2_key), and commits. Fills the gap where the signed PDF was never persisted (the S1-22 webhook only kept the raw event JSON). Appended to `FUNCTIONS` in `worker/client.py`. Concurrency: global `Concurrency(limit=5)` + per-client `Concurrency(limit=1)`. Retriable classification in the wrapper: `PandaDocNotFound` / empty PDF / empty-creds `RuntimeError` -> `NonRetriableError`; httpx 5xx/429/timeout + botocore transient errors propagate for Inngest retry.
- **Added**: `download_document(document_id) -> bytes` on the PandaDoc client (Protocol + `HttpPandaDocClient` `GET /public/v1/documents/{id}/download` with `Authorization: API-Key`, 404 -> `PandaDocNotFound`, other non-2xx raises for retry + `FakePandaDocClient` `pdfs` map + a `download_error` hook for transport-error tests). `document_kind` constants added to `bullet_api/db/enums.py` (`DOCUMENT_KIND_PANDADOC_SIGNED_PDF` + siblings). Five `config.py` R2 settings (`r2_account_id`, `r2_access_key_id`, `r2_secret_access_key`, `r2_bucket_name` + derived `r2_endpoint_url` property) + the matching `.env.example` block (names already in `render.yaml` from S1-21; NOT yet set on the Render env groups - pre-prod follow-up). `boto3>=1.35,<2.0` added to `apps/api/pyproject.toml` (uv.lock -> boto3 1.43.24).
- **Decision**: R2 is talked to via **boto3 + `asyncio.to_thread`** over aioboto3 (fussier pinned `aiobotocore`/`botocore` chain) or hand-rolled SigV4 (security-sensitive). At Bullet's volume (one PDF per signing across ~90-100 clients) reliability + low maintenance beat async-native throughput, and `to_thread` already keeps the loop free. The storage seam is a Protocol so a future swap touches one file.
- **Decision**: idempotency is layered - `platform_actions` idempotency-key short-circuit (replay) + deterministic R2 key (overwrite, no duplicate object) + guarded `documents` insert (no duplicate row, no migration). The known at-least-once edge (crash after the R2 put, before the commit) is harmless: the deterministic key overwrites and the guarded insert dedupes on retry.
- **Fixed (self-review, pre-PR)**: the broad `except` that records `failed` was narrowed to wrap ONLY the external calls (download + upload), with the `documents` insert + `complete_action` moved AFTER it. Originally the DB writes sat inside the try; a DB error there would abort the transaction and the `except`'s own `fail_action` UPDATE would then fail on the aborted transaction and never record. Now the realistic failure surface (the external calls) records `failed` cleanly, and a (rare) DB-write failure propagates uncaught so Inngest retries and converges - matching S1-25's post-call-DB-writes shape. Caught by the `/pre-pr-review` gate.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest .` (from `apps/api`, local docker Postgres) -> 223 passed, 0 failed (was 202; +21: `test_storage_client.py` 6, `test_signed_pdf.py` 10, `test_pandadoc_client.py` +5 download cases). `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean (88 files); `make typecheck` green; `alembic upgrade head` a no-op (`documents` from 0006 + `platform_actions` from 0005 already exist). The `test_signed_pdf.py` matrix covers success, PandaDoc 404, a transport-level download error (`httpx.ReadTimeout`), R2 upload failure, empty PDF, replay idempotency, and guarded-insert dedup - plus the concurrency/trigger declaration. **NOT verified live**: no R2 creds / no GHL-style sandbox locally, so the real PandaDoc download shape and a real R2 put are unconfirmed (deferred to a pre-prod smoke, same posture as the GHL contract); R2 env vars must be set on the Render env groups before rollout.

### 08/06/2026 - S1-25: GHL sub-account creation (direct API, replaces Pabbly)

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/ghl/` package with `client.py` - the GoHighLevel (LeadConnector) REST client seam, mirroring `bullet_api.pandadoc.client`. `GhlClient` Protocol (`create_location(payload) -> GhlLocation`), production `HttpGhlClient` (`POST {base}/locations/` with headers `Authorization: Bearer <agency key>`, `Version: 2021-07-28`, `Content-Type: application/json`; empty key -> `RuntimeError` loud-fail like `HttpPandaDocClient`), and `FakeGhlClient` test double that returns a preloaded `GhlLocation` or raises a preset error and records every request payload on `.calls` for assertions. Status-code mapping splits errors so the worker can decide retriable vs not: 2xx -> parse; `429`/`5xx` -> `GhlServerError` (retriable); other `4xx` -> `GhlClientError` (non-retriable). Accepts an injectable `httpx` transport for `MockTransport` tests.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/platform_actions.py` - the SHARED fan-out audit recorder over the `platform_actions` table, established here as S1-25 is the first fan-out and reused by every later one (S1-25b, Asana, Stripe, Xero, Timely). `build_idempotency_key(client_id, platform, action, event_id)` centralises the `{client_id}:{platform}:{action}:{event_id}` contract from migration 0005; `begin_action(...)` does `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) DO NOTHING RETURNING id, status` then reads the row back on conflict (returns `BeginActionResult{action_id, status, already_succeeded}`); `complete_action(...)` sets `status='success'` + `external_id` + `response` JSONB + `completed_at`; `fail_action(...)` sets `status='failed'` + `last_error` + `retry_count = retry_count + 1` (leaves `completed_at` NULL - the action will be retried). JSONB columns written via `cast(:p AS jsonb)` + `json.dumps(...)`, the same pattern as the S1-22 webhook / S1-23 reconcile inserts. The module never commits - transaction control stays with the orchestrator, matching `create_client_record_core`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/ghl_subaccount.py` - the S1-25 Inngest function `create_ghl_subaccount`, triggered by `client.created` (NOT `pandadoc.signed`, per the 04/06/2026 rescope). Pure testable core `create_ghl_subaccount_core(session, ghl_client, *, client_id, onboarding_event_id, company_id, snapshot_id, inngest_run_id)`: loads the `clients` row (missing -> `ClientNotFoundError`), **short-circuits if `ghl_subaccount_id` is already set** (records the action as `success` against the existing id, never POSTs a second location), else records an `in_progress` action and **commits before the GHL call** (a crash mid-POST leaves a visible row, never a silent gap), calls `ghl_client.create_location(...)`, and on success records `success` + the returned id and writes `clients.ghl_subaccount_id` back (`UPDATE ... WHERE id = :id AND ghl_subaccount_id IS NULL` guards a concurrent writer) then commits; on `GhlError` records `failed` (+ bumps `retry_count`), commits, and re-raises. The thin Inngest wrapper builds `HttpGhlClient` from settings, validates `ghl_company_id` is set (empty -> NonRetriable), opens `AsyncSessionLocal`, and translates `ClientNotFoundError` / `GhlClientError` / empty-key `RuntimeError` into `inngest.NonRetriableError` (dead-letter); `GhlServerError` (5xx/429) + transient errors propagate so Inngest retries. The function is appended to `FUNCTIONS` in `worker/client.py` so the FastAPI Inngest adapter serves it.
- **Added**: five `config.py` settings under a new S1-25 block: `ghl_agency_api_key` (Bearer token, empty in dev/test), `ghl_company_id` (required `companyId` body field), `ghl_api_base_url` (default `https://services.leadconnectorhq.com`), `ghl_api_version` (default `2021-07-28`), and `ghl_snapshot_id` (optional; empty omits `snapshotId` from the create body so a bare sub-account is created). The matching `.env.example` block was added too (`GHL_AGENCY_API_KEY`, `GHL_COMPANY_ID`, `GHL_API_BASE_URL`, `GHL_API_VERSION`, `GHL_SNAPSHOT_ID`) with the same dev-empty / prod-set convention as the S1-22/S1-23 secret blocks. NOTE: unlike S1-23, the matching keys have NOT yet been added to the Render `bullet-staging-env` / `bullet-prod-env` groups - that is a pre-production step (the two required values, `GHL_AGENCY_API_KEY` + `GHL_COMPANY_ID`, plus an optional `GHL_SNAPSHOT_ID`, must be set there before rollout; tracked as a follow-up).
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_ghl_client.py` (8 no-DB tests via `httpx.MockTransport`: success parse, exact URL+headers+body shape, empty-key `RuntimeError`, `4xx -> GhlClientError` and `429/5xx -> GhlServerError` parametrized) and `apps/api/tests/test_ghl_subaccount.py` (13 tests, mostly `@pytest.mark.db` with `FakeGhlClient`): success writes `status=success` + `external_id` + `clients.ghl_subaccount_id` and persists the request payload; `snapshotId` present only when configured; `name` falls back to `legal_entity`; 4xx records `failed` + `last_error` + `retry_count=1` and raises with no id written; 5xx records `failed` and propagates; missing client -> `ClientNotFoundError` (GHL never called); replay of the same event creates no second location/row (idempotency-key dedupe); already-provisioned client short-circuits `skipped` and audits the existing id; plus declaration assertions that the function carries a global cap of 3 + a per-client cap of 1 and triggers on `client.created`.
- **Decision**: the `platform_actions` pre/post audit pattern is built as a SHARED recorder (`worker/platform_actions.py`) at the first fan-out rather than inlined, because the table's idempotency-key format + status lifecycle are already a fixed contract (migration 0005) and S1-25b is the immediate next consumer; one tested recorder prevents each fan-out from re-implementing the `ON CONFLICT` + status-transition SQL slightly differently and drifting the audit trail.
- **Decision**: S1-25 commits the `in_progress` action row BEFORE the external GHL POST, then commits the terminal `success`/`failed` state after. Satisfies the project rule that partial failures must be visible in the dashboard, never silent - a crash between the two commits leaves an `in_progress` row rather than nothing. (This differs from S1-25a's commit-then-emit ordering, which is about a different hazard - non-durable `client.created` consumers.)
- **Decision**: concurrency is declared as TWO Inngest caps: a global `Concurrency(limit=3, scope="fn")` to respect the GHL agency API rate limit (the card's "cap of 3"), plus a per-client `Concurrency(key="event.data.client_id", limit=1, scope="fn")` to eliminate a concurrent double-create for the same client. The per-client cap is safe because there is exactly one `pandadoc.signed` onboarding_events row per document (UNIQUE on `(event_type, external_id)`), so the idempotency key `{client_id}:ghl:create_subaccount:{onboarding_event_id}` is stable per client and replays dedupe.
- **Discovery**: the GHL automation IDs Bullet shared (`Post Signing OB Link Workflow & Reminders`, `OB Survey Complete`, `Kick Off Call Booked`, `Outstanding Elements Follow Up`) are **workflow** ids INSIDE an existing location (`gdyA9mQE9Q7JZxHnPNCe`, Bullet's master/template sub-account), NOT a `snapshotId`. A snapshot is an agency-level template bundle that GHL clones into new sub-accounts; those workflows would be PACKAGED into such a snapshot but are not one themselves. S1-25 therefore ships with `ghl_snapshot_id` empty (bare sub-accounts) pending confirmation of an agency snapshot id - see follow-up. Note `Outstanding Elements Follow Up` is being retired (replaced by the DB-driven dashboard checklist) so it must NOT be in any snapshot we adopt.
- **Decision**: a known at-least-once edge in S1-25 is deferred to S1-26, NOT fixed here. Creating a GHL location is a non-atomic two-step (POST to GHL, then persist the id locally); if the worker crashes in the gap after GHL creates the location but before the success commit, an Inngest retry sees no `ghl_subaccount_id` and POSTs again, producing a duplicate GHL sub-account. GHL's `/locations/` offers no idempotency-key header, so the platform cannot dedupe for us. The fix is "look up GHL before creating" - which is exactly the mechanism S1-26 (`Returning-client check + parent_client_id linking`, card `0287df44-23bf-44ec-a6e8-ce5b852bb0ad`, depends on S1-25) already introduces for the returning-client case: it looks up an existing GHL contact/location by the signed-document email and reuses the existing `ghl_subaccount_id` instead of creating. The same lookup also catches a crash-orphaned location (it carries the client's email), so S1-26 closes this window for free. Two riders to carry into S1-26: (a) run the GHL lookup on EVERY create attempt (covers the crash-retry path, not only the returning-client path); (b) S1-26's planned `skipped_existing` outcome has no matching `platform_action_status` enum value today (statuses are `pending`/`in_progress`/`success`/`failed`/`dead_lettered`) - S1-25's already-provisioned skip currently records plain `success` with a note in the `response` JSONB, so S1-26 must decide whether to add a `skipped_existing` enum value (migration) or keep the `success` + response-marker convention. Until S1-26 lands, the partial failure is at least VISIBLE (a stuck `in_progress` row in the dashboard), never silent.
- **Verified**: `uv run pytest .` (from `apps/api`, against local docker Postgres) -> 201 passed, 0 failed (was 180 before this card, +21 net new tests, no regressions); `uv run ruff check .` clean; `uv run ruff format --check .` clean (83 files); `make typecheck` (dashboard + shared TS) passes. `uv run alembic upgrade head` is a no-op (no new migration - `clients.ghl_subaccount_id` from 0002 and `platform_actions` from 0005 already exist). Function-config inspection: `create_ghl_subaccount.get_config("").main.concurrency` returns the global `limit=3` + per-client `key='event.data.client_id', limit=1` caps, and `.triggers[0].event == "client.created"`.
- **NOT verified (deferred, with reason)**: no live smoke test against the real GoHighLevel API was run for this card, so the create-location request/response contract is confirmed only against the OpenAPI spec + our unit tests, NOT against GHL's actual runtime validation (which is known to sometimes require more fields than the spec marks required). Reasons: (1) **GHL has no sandbox** - `services.leadconnectorhq.com` is the only host, so any smoke test creates a REAL sub-account in Bullet's live agency, which can count toward GHL per-location billing and fire agency-side automations; (2) the only credentials available are two private-integration tokens (`pit-...`) labelled "High Level Agency" and "High Level Bullet Sub Acc" - the agency token is the right one for `GHL_AGENCY_API_KEY`, but the required `companyId` is not among them and was not located in the auth sheet; (3) creating against the client's production agency needs explicit owner sign-off. Two throwaway helper scripts were written for performing the smoke test - `scripts/ghl_get_company_id.py` (READ-ONLY - `GET /locations/{id}` with the agency token to discover the `companyId` and validate auth, zero side effects) and `scripts/smoke_ghl_subaccount.py` (creates one clearly-labelled throwaway sub-account via the production `HttpGhlClient`, prints the real response shape, then auto-deletes it) - but they are deliberately kept LOCAL and NOT committed (excluded from this PR): they are one-off verification aids, not shipped code. The live contract should be confirmed via these against the staging/live agency before production rollout (tracked as a follow-up alongside the S1-26 returning-client lookup, which needs the same GHL read access).
- **Fixed (PR review)**: `worker/platform_actions.py` `begin_action` now uses `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) DO UPDATE SET idempotency_key = EXCLUDED.idempotency_key RETURNING id, status` instead of `DO NOTHING` + a fallback `SELECT`. The no-op SET makes RETURNING populate the surviving row on both a fresh insert and a conflict in one round-trip, and the conflict path takes a row lock that blocks until a concurrent inserter commits - closing the empty-read-back the `DO NOTHING` + separate `SELECT` could hit under READ COMMITTED when a racing duplicate is still uncommitted. This matters because this recorder is the SHARED primitive every later fan-out (S1-25b, Asana, Stripe, Xero, Timely) reuses, and not all of them will carry S1-25's per-client `Concurrency(limit=1)` cap that made the race unreachable here. Mirrors the `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... RETURNING` pattern S1-25a already chose for the `clients` upsert. Behaviour-preserving on the happy / replay / already-provisioned paths (existing idempotency tests still green).
- **Fixed (PR review)**: `worker/ghl_subaccount.py` `create_ghl_subaccount_core` now records `failed` for ANY exception raised by `ghl_client.create_location(...)`, not only `GhlClientError` / `GhlServerError`. A transport-level error (an httpx timeout / connection reset, which carries no HTTP status) previously bypassed `fail_action` and left the `platform_actions` row stuck `in_progress` with `retry_count=0`; it now flips to `failed` (+ `last_error`, `retry_count` bumped) before re-raising the exception unchanged, so the Inngest wrapper still decides retriable-vs-not from the type. This also makes the response-lost read-timeout case visible - the worst variant of the at-least-once create window still deferred to S1-26 (a hard process crash in the commit gap can still leave a visible `in_progress` row; only the exception / timeout path is now recorded `failed`). New test `test_transient_error_records_failed_and_propagates` (raises `httpx.ReadTimeout`) covers it; `GhlServerError` was dropped from the module imports as it is no longer referenced in code.
- **Verified (PR review fixes)**: `uv run pytest .` (from `apps/api`, against docker Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` on :5433, run in an isolated git worktree) -> **202 passed**, 0 failed (was 201; +1 for the transient-error test), no regressions; `uv run ruff check .` clean; `uv run ruff format --check .` clean (83 files); `alembic upgrade head` at single head `0007`.

### 04/06/2026 - S1-25a: client record creation from `pandadoc.signed` (orchestrator entry step)

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/client_record.py` - the S1-25a Inngest function `create_client_record`, triggered by `pandadoc.signed`. Pure testable core `create_client_record_core(session, *, onboarding_event_id, document_id, document, emitter)` takes the already-fetched PandaDoc document body (the wrapper does the HTTP fetch OUTSIDE the DB session so the pool is not held during PandaDoc latency), extracts client fields, upserts a `clients` row via `ON CONFLICT (pandadoc_document_id) DO UPDATE SET pandadoc_document_id = EXCLUDED.pandadoc_document_id RETURNING id, (xmax = 0) AS inserted` (no-op SET so RETURNING populates on both insert and conflict; `xmax = 0` flags inserts vs conflicts in one round-trip), backfills `onboarding_events.client_id` + `processed_at = COALESCE(processed_at, now())` (set-once across retries; `rowcount=0` raises `OnboardingEventNotFoundError`), **commits**, then emits `client.created` carrying `{client_id, onboarding_event_id, document_id, email}`. The commit-BEFORE-emit ordering means downstream consumers never see a `client.created` for a non-durable client (a failed emit triggers an Inngest retry; the UPSERT is a no-op on retry and the emit gets another shot). Function decorator declares a per-document concurrency cap: `concurrency=[Concurrency(key="event.data.document_id", limit=1, scope="fn")]` eliminates duplicate fetches/emits for the same signing. Helper `fetch_document_for_orchestrator(pandadoc_client, document_id)` translates `PandaDocNotFound` (404) and `RuntimeError` (empty API key) into `inngest.NonRetriableError` so Inngest dead-letters immediately rather than burning the retry budget on failures that cannot self-heal; httpx 5xx/429/timeout errors propagate naturally for Inngest to retry. `current_step = CURRENT_STEP_SIGNED` (imported from `bullet_api.db.enums`) is set on INSERT only - a replay must not stomp downstream-step progress. `legal_entity` is NOT NULL: orchestrator falls back to `business_name`, then to `LEGAL_ENTITY_PLACEHOLDER = "Unknown - needs review"`. `business_name` stays NULL when both are missing (the column is NULL-able; only `legal_entity` gets the placeholder so the dashboard "needs review" alert doesn't duplicate across columns).
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/clients_payload.py` - PURE helper `extract_client_fields(document) -> ExtractedClientFields`. Maps a PandaDoc document detail body (the `GET /public/v1/documents/{id}/details` response, NOT the webhook event envelope) to a dataclass of `clients` columns. Hard-required field is `tokens[Client.Email]`; missing/empty raises `PandaDocPayloadError("tokens[Client.Email]")` so the transaction rolls back and the Inngest run dead-letters with that field name in the error. Every other field optional. Each field reads from exactly ONE pinned token / form-field / metadata key, verified against a real Bullet UK signed agreement: tokens `Client.Email`, `Client.FirstName`, `Client.LastName`, `Client.Phone`, `Company.Name`, `Company.Address`, `Company.State`, `Company.Zip`, `Company.Country`, `Deal.Currency`, `Deal.MonthlyServiceFee`; form field `Enter Your Company's Legal Trading Name`; metadata `hubspot.contact_id`, `hubspot.deal_id`, `hubspot.company_id`, `document__creation_source`; template id from the `template` object. Defensive against malformed input (non-dict, non-list, non-string values) - returns None rather than raising.
- **Added**: `apps/api/alembic/versions/0007_unique_pandadoc_document_id.py` - additive migration that adds `uq_clients_pandadoc_document_id` UNIQUE INDEX on `clients.pandadoc_document_id`. Enforces upsert idempotency at the DB layer: a replayed `pandadoc.signed` event for the same document_id cannot create a second `clients` row. Postgres treats NULL as distinct in UNIQUE by default, so existing NULL rows (no application code has written to this column prior to S1-25a) remain valid. `clients.email` deliberately stays non-unique - returning-client second-site rows share an email, linked via `parent_client_id` (S1-26). Built with `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` (wrapped between explicit `COMMIT` / `BEGIN` because Alembic's default transaction wrap is incompatible with `CONCURRENTLY`); safe to run on a populated production `clients` table without acquiring an `AccessExclusiveLock`. Downgrade uses `DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS` so a partial-build cleanup is safe. Round-trip (`alembic downgrade -1 && alembic upgrade head`) verified locally.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/_inngest.py` - dep-free home for the shared `inngest_client` symbol. Every function-registering module (`worker/client.py`, `worker/client_record.py`, future GHL / Stripe handlers) imports `inngest_client` from here, breaking what would otherwise become an import cycle (`client.py` -> `client_record.py` -> `events.py` -> `client.py`). The canonical public import path is `from bullet_api.worker import inngest_client` (re-exported from `worker/__init__.py`); existing call sites (`main.py`, `crons/reconcile_pandadoc.py`) updated to use it.
- **Added**: `CLIENT_CREATED_EVENT = "client.created"` constant in `bullet_api/worker/events.py`, exported via `bullet_api.worker`. This is the new common fan-out trigger that every downstream Inngest function (S1-25 GHL sub-account creation, S1-25b signed-PDF storage, Sprint 2 Asana / Slack / Drive / Stripe / Xero / Timely) subscribes to, instead of subscribing to `pandadoc.signed` directly. `client_id` is guaranteed to exist in the event payload by the time those functions run.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/pandadoc/client.py` extended. New `fetch_document_details(document_id) -> dict` method on the Protocol + `HttpPandaDocClient` + `FakePandaDocClient` returns the raw JSON body for the same `/public/v1/documents/{id}/details` endpoint S1-24's `fetch_document` already hits. The existing `fetch_document` refactored to call `fetch_document_details` internally and project from its result, so both methods share one HTTP path. `FakePandaDocClient` gains a `details: dict[str, dict]` field for preloaded raw bodies. Backward-compatible: S1-24's call sites and tests unchanged.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_clients_payload.py` - 22 pure-Python unit tests for the extraction helper (no DB, no FastAPI, no Inngest). Cover: required-email validation (raises with `tokens[Client.Email]` field name), all token/field/metadata extraction against real Bullet UK shape, `Company.Name` vs the legal-trading-name form field independence, recipient[0] explicitly NOT used as identity source, first-matching-token-wins, defensive handling of non-list tokens / non-dict metadata / non-string values / whitespace-only values, template id with `template.uuid` fallback.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_create_client_record.py` - 11 integration + unit tests covering the three card-spec behaviours plus the full failure-mode matrix: (a) new signing creates exactly one `clients` row with mapped fields and `current_step = 'signed'`, `onboarding_events.client_id` backfilled, `processed_at` set, exactly one `client.created` emitted; (b) replay of the same `document_id` creates no second row AND `processed_at` is preserved via COALESCE; (c) missing `Client.Email` token raises `PandaDocPayloadError` with no partial row written and no event emitted. Plus: `legal_entity` fallback to `business_name`; both-missing-uses-placeholder-ONLY-for-legal-entity (`business_name` stays NULL); orphan event_id raises `OnboardingEventNotFoundError`; **regression test** that `onboarding_events.payload` JSONB is NOT read for field extraction (pins the architecture pivot in test code); emit-failure-after-commit propagates AND the client row is durable (pins the commit-then-emit ordering); 3 tests for `fetch_document_for_orchestrator` confirming NonRetriable translation of 404 and empty-key errors; concurrency-cap declaration assertion via `create_client_record.get_config("").main.concurrency`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/scripts/inspect_pandadoc_document.py` - throwaway investigation script that fetches the most recent completed PandaDoc document via the API and prints a MASKED shape summary (field names + types only, no values) safe to share. Writes a fuller masked dump to `apps/api/.tmp/`; `--with-raw` opt-in writes the raw PII body for local-only inspection. Used to lock down Bullet's UK template's exact token / field / metadata names; should be re-run against the INT account when those credentials land.
- **Added**: `.tmp/` entry in `.gitignore` so the inspection script's local dumps (which contain real client PII when `--with-raw` is passed) never end up in a commit.
- **Decision**: client-row creation is its OWN orchestrator step (S1-25a), NOT inlined into the S1-22 webhook or any downstream fan-out. Owner-confirmed: keep S1-22 thin (HMAC + `onboarding_events` insert + `pandadoc.signed` emit only - no platform writes), then make every fan-out (S1-25, S1-25b, S2-*) trigger off `client.created` rather than `pandadoc.signed`. Guarantees `client_id` exists for every consumer and prevents a thundering herd of find-or-create races. S1-25 has been re-scoped accordingly: it now reads `client_id` from `client.created` and only writes `ghl_subaccount_id` back to the existing row.
- **Decision**: the orchestrator FETCHES the document body via the PandaDoc API (`GET /public/v1/documents/{id}/details`) instead of reading `onboarding_events.payload` JSONB. The `document.completed` webhook payload does NOT carry client values - it only has `data.id` and `data.status`. PandaDoc populates `recipients` / `tokens` / `fields` / `metadata` only on the document detail endpoint. The single API call per signing is the authoritative read; the captured webhook payload stays as the audit trail.
- **Decision**: extraction reads from EXACT pinned names verified against Bullet's real UK template, NOT multi-alias fallback search. Single source of truth per field; if the International (INT) account's template uses different names, the helper will need a per-template branch.
- **Decision**: `recipients[0]` is NOT used as the source for client email / name. Bullet's agreement has 4 recipients (client signers + Bullet signers); index 0 is unreliable. The `Client.Email` TOKEN is the authoritative single source.
- **Decision**: `legal_entity` falls back to `business_name` then a `LEGAL_ENTITY_PLACEHOLDER` sentinel rather than dead-lettering. Keeps the row insertable; team corrects in the dashboard once the dashboard surfaces it. The placeholder is ONLY written to `legal_entity` (NOT NULL); `business_name` (NULL-able) stays NULL when missing, so the dashboard "needs review" alert isn't duplicated across columns.
- **Decision**: `current_step` is set to `CURRENT_STEP_SIGNED` (constant from new `bullet_api.db.enums`) ONLY on the initial INSERT path. A replay of `pandadoc.signed` for a document whose client has progressed past `signed` (e.g. into `portal` or `kickoff`) must not regress the step. New module `bullet_api/db/enums.py` is the application-side source of truth for Postgres enum string values; future enum-driven SQL should import from here.
- **Decision**: commit BEFORE emit (not the S1-22 webhook's emit-then-commit). S1-22's retry source is PandaDoc; S1-25a's is Inngest. Under commit-then-emit, a failed emit triggers an Inngest retry that converges via the idempotent UPSERT + COALESCE backfill; downstream consumers can NEVER receive a `client.created` for a non-durable client.
- **Decision**: per-document concurrency cap (`limit=1`, `key="event.data.document_id"`) on `create_client_record` eliminates duplicate PandaDoc fetches + duplicate `client.created` emits when two events fire for the same signing. Inngest enforces server-side; test asserts the declaration.
- **Decision**: structural data failures that cannot self-heal on retry (PandaDoc 404, empty API key, missing required `Client.Email` token) are wrapped in `inngest.NonRetriableError` so Inngest dead-letters immediately. Transient failures (httpx 5xx/429/timeout, DB connection drops) propagate naturally for Inngest to retry. Orphan event-row (backfill UPDATE matched 0 rows) raises `OnboardingEventNotFoundError(LookupError)` - a DISTINCT exception class so log filters / alert routing can tell it apart from PandaDoc payload errors - and **propagates** (not wrapped NonRetriable): the most common cause is the S1-22 webhook's emit-before-commit visibility race (the producer INSERTs, emits, then commits; Inngest can dispatch our orchestrator before the commit lands, so our separate transaction sees rowcount=0), which self-heals on a single retry. A genuinely-orphaned row (manual deletion) still dead-letters after Inngest's default retry budget is exhausted. Asymmetric cost: a transient race shouldn't permanently drop a valid signing, and a real orphan dead-letters either way.
- **Discovery**: Bullet runs TWO separate PandaDoc accounts - `pandadoc - UK` and `pandadoc INT` - each with its own API key + webhook subscription + shared key. Our current single-value `Settings.pandadoc_api_key` and `PANDADOC_WEBHOOK_SECRET` cannot serve both. Production needs: (a) two API key settings (`pandadoc_api_key_uk`, `pandadoc_api_key_int`), (b) two webhook subscriptions with distinct shared keys (likely served on distinct paths like `/webhooks/pandadoc/uk` + `/webhooks/pandadoc/int`), and (c) a per-document routing key on `onboarding_events` so the orchestrator knows which account to query. Surfaces Steve's open question #2 in OB-Phase-1.md ("UK vs International routing logic"). Needs its own ticket (proposed: S1-25c) before any production rollout. Today's work uses the UK key only.
- **Discovery**: real Bullet UK template shape (verified via the inspection script): PandaDoc uses dot-notation metadata keys (`hubspot.contact_id`, NOT `hubspot_contact_id` or `hubspotContactId`); legal entity is a FORM FIELD filled during signing (titled literally `"Enter Your Company's Legal Trading Name"`), not a HubSpot-pushed token; the template carries deal economics (`Deal.MonthlyServiceFee`, `Deal.Currency` likely GBP) which our `monthly_fee_usd` column name does not reflect; tokens are namespaced (`Client.*`, `Company.*`, `Deal.*`, `Bullet.*` for the salesperson - `Bullet.*` ignored here). Top-level keys include `template`, `linked_objects`, `pricing`, `tables`, `tags`, `texts` - useful for follow-up integrations.
- **Discovery (live smoke test)**: end-to-end smoke against a real signed UK PandaDoc confirmed the orchestrator works against real data AND surfaced that HubSpot's PandaDoc integration sends `metadata` values in MIXED TYPES per key, not all as strings: `hubspot.deal_id` and `hubspot.company_id` are strings, but `hubspot.contact_id` is a LIST (one entry per HubSpot contact linked to the deal). The original strict `_metadata_value` returned None for non-string values, silently dropping `hubspot_contact_id`. Fixed `_metadata_value` to coerce ints/floats to str and to take the first non-empty entry of a list; bools are explicitly NOT coerced (would yield literal `"True"`/`"False"` which is never a valid id). 5 new unit tests cover the type-coercion paths. Multi-contact deals lose the additional contact ids - a follow-up `client_hubspot_contacts` linking table is the schema fix once multi-contact matters in practice.
- **Discovery**: the import cycle `worker/client.py` -> `worker/client_record.py` -> `worker/events.py` -> `worker/client.py` was triggered the moment `worker/client.py` started importing from another `worker/*` module. Fixed by extracting `inngest_client` into a dep-free `_inngest.py` module and lazily importing it inside `get_event_emitter()`. Canonical import path for callers is `from bullet_api.worker import inngest_client` (re-exported from the package root); intra-worker modules use `from bullet_api.worker._inngest import inngest_client` directly.
- **Fixed**: `log.info(..., extra={"created": True, ...})` collided with `LogRecord.created` (the standard timestamp attribute) and raised `KeyError: "Attempt to overwrite 'created' in LogRecord"` when the structured logging handler iterated the extra dict. Renamed the extra key to `row_inserted`. Reserved LogRecord attribute names (`created`, `message`, `asctime`, `levelname`, `lineno`, `module`, ...) must not be used as `extra` keys anywhere.
- **Fixed (PR review)**: four items raised by code review on this PR. (1) **P1** - `OnboardingEventNotFoundError` was wrapped in `inngest.NonRetriableError` and dead-lettered immediately, which would permanently drop a valid signing if Inngest ever dispatched the orchestrator inside the S1-22 webhook's emit-before-commit visibility window. Removed from the wrapper's NonRetriable catch; the exception now propagates so Inngest's default retry policy absorbs the race; a persistent orphan still dead-letters after retries. The class docstring + the module's correctness-rules + the wrapper's `Raises:` section + the existing orphan-id test comment all updated to reflect the new policy. (2) **P2** - `.gitignore` `.tmp/` entry was not on the PR branch (had been excluded earlier); committed it so the inspection script's local `--with-raw` PII dumps can never accidentally be `git add -A`'d. (3) **P3a** - `address`, `state`, `postal_code`, `country` were extracted into `ExtractedClientFields` but neither persisted (no columns) nor logged. Added matching `has_*` boolean entries to the structured `log.info` `extra` dict so presence is visible in production logs until the follow-up migration adds the columns. (4) **P3c** - dropped the redundant `step_entered_at = now()` from the INSERT; the column's `server_default = now()` populates it on insert, and `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` doesn't touch it either way (same behaviour, one fewer redundant value). **P3b** (two `HttpPandaDocClient` classes in `pandadoc/client.py` and `integrations/pandadoc_client.py`) is acknowledged but NOT addressed in this PR - pre-existing per the 03/06/2026 S1-23 Discovery in this CHANGELOG, where the consolidation follow-up is already tracked.
- **Verified**: `uv run alembic upgrade head` applies 0007 cleanly against local Postgres (and `downgrade -1` then `upgrade head` round-trips); `uv run pytest apps/api -q` -> 180 passed, 0 failed (was 132 before this card, +48 net new tests, no regressions in the pre-existing suite); `uv run ruff check apps/api` clean. The integration tests use the existing transactional `async_session` fixture and feed the orchestrator core synthetic `document: dict` fixtures (no real PandaDoc HTTP call); the `fetch_document_for_orchestrator` tests use `FakePandaDocClient` to exercise the NonRetriable translation. Function-config inspection: `from bullet_api.worker.client import FUNCTIONS; FUNCTIONS[1].get_config('').main.concurrency` returns `[Concurrency(key='event.data.document_id', limit=1, scope='fn')]`. End-to-end live smoke test against a real Bullet UK signed agreement (executed via a throwaway script during dev, not committed) confirmed every stage works against real data: PandaDoc fetch -> field extraction (all expected fields populated, incl. `hubspot_contact_id` after the list-coercion fix) -> upsert -> backfill -> commit -> emit; test rows cleaned up after.

### 04/06/2026 - CI fully green: Neon python-tests secrets added + two latent main regressions fixed

- **Added**: the three repository secrets the `python-tests` CI job reads, set on `tsizzybots/Bullet-Digital-Media-Onboarding`: `NEON_API_KEY`, `NEON_PROJECT_ID` (= `old-credit-26332098`, the `bullet-staging` Neon project), and `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL` (the staging direct connection string). The repo had ZERO Actions secrets (`gh secret list` empty since creation), so `python-tests` could never reach a database. With them in place the job provisions a per-run ephemeral Neon branch, runs the Alembic up/down/up, and runs the full pytest suite. Closes Action 1 of `docs/GitHub-actions-fix.md`. The secrets were retrieved by driving the Neon console with Playwright; the two live-credential values were pasted by the user into `gh secret set` no-echo prompts so they never entered the session transcript.
- **Discovery**: `NEON_API_KEY` is a PERSONAL Neon key, not an organization key. The logged-in account (`team@izzyagents.ai`) is a MEMBER of the "Bullet Launch" org (`org-lingering-river-46736330`), not an org admin, so the org-level "Create new API key" button (and the other org-admin actions) is disabled in the Neon console. A member's personal API key inherits that member's access to the org's `bullet-staging` project, which is sufficient for the CI branch create/delete. The three Bullet Neon projects are `bullet-prod`, `bullet-staging` (CI/test), and `Bullet Agents`.
- **Discovery**: the `@pytest.mark.db` tests had never executed in CI (no secrets), only locally. Their first real CI run immediately surfaced a latent S1-23 bug that the local "Verified - 3 passed" note had missed - direct evidence that leaving `python-tests` perpetually red/skipped was hiding regressions on `main`, and that wiring the Neon secrets in was the right call.
- **Fixed** (commit f11e3ce): `KeyError: "Attempt to overwrite 'created' in LogRecord"` in `crons/reconcile_pandadoc.py` (S1-23). Two `log.info(..., extra={"created": ...})` sites (the per-pass "reconciliation pass complete" and the `main()` "reconciliation finished") used `created`, a RESERVED `logging.LogRecord` attribute, so stdlib logging raised whenever the line executed - failing all 3 reconcile db tests and, unnoticed, would have crashed the live nightly cron after any successful pass. Renamed the log key to `created_count` at both sites; the `ReconcileResult.created` dataclass field the tests assert on is unchanged.
- **Fixed** (commit f11e3ce): trailing whitespace in `qa-reports/qa-review-2026-05-22-090445.md` (markdown hard-break spaces, introduced by commit be860e0) that was failing the `pre-commit` CI job's `trailing-whitespace` hook.
- **Verified**: CI run `26931499074` on `main` (commit f11e3ce) is 5/5 GREEN - pre-commit, OpenAPI codegen drift, python lint+typecheck, TypeScript lint+typecheck, and python-tests (ephemeral Neon branch + Alembic up/down/up + full pytest) all pass. The two fixes were also checked locally pre-push: `pre-commit run --all-files` exits 0 and `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` are clean across `apps/api` (69 files).

### 03/06/2026 - S1-23: PandaDoc daily reconciliation cron

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/crons/` package (the home for recurring Render cron jobs, distinct from one-off `scripts/`) with `reconcile_pandadoc.py`. The nightly job is the safety net for missed S1-22 webhook deliveries: `reconcile_pandadoc(session, *, pandadoc_client, emitter, slack, lookback_days, now)` lists PandaDoc documents completed since `now - lookback_days`, and for each with no `onboarding_events` row inserts a synthetic event, emits `pandadoc.signed`, and posts a Slack alert. The insert REPLICATES the canonical S1-22 contract (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (event_type, external_id) DO NOTHING RETURNING id`) so a document already present (webhook delivered it, or a prior reconcile) is a pure no-op (no emit, no alert); `verified_at` is stamped `now()` and the payload tagged `source = "pandadoc_reconciliation"`. Commits once after the emits/alerts succeed, mirroring the webhook. `main()` is the `uv run python -m bullet_api.crons.reconcile_pandadoc` entry point; it `configure_logging()`s and, when `PANDADOC_API_KEY` is empty, logs "disabled" and exits 0 without calling PandaDoc.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/integrations/` package with `pandadoc_client.py` (list-documents client: `PandaDocDocument`, `PandaDocClient` Protocol, `parse_documents` pure/total parser, `HttpPandaDocClient` paging `GET /public/v1/documents?status=2&completed_from=<ISO>` with `Authorization: API-Key`, and `FakePandaDocClient`) and `slack.py` (`SlackNotifier` Protocol, disabled-by-default `HttpSlackNotifier` that no-ops + warns on an empty webhook URL, `FakeSlackNotifier`, and the pure `format_reconciliation_alert`). Both HTTP clients accept an injectable `httpx` transport for `MockTransport` testing; `format_reconciliation_alert` imports `PandaDocDocument` only under `TYPE_CHECKING` so the two slices stay decoupled.
- **Added**: four `config.py` settings reusing the existing S1-24 `pandadoc_api_key` plus new `pandadoc_api_base_url` (default `https://api.pandadoc.com`), `slack_webhook_url` (default `""`), and `reconciliation_lookback_days` (default `7`); the matching commented block in `.env.example`; and the four keys in BOTH `bullet-staging-env` and `bullet-prod-env` Render groups (`PANDADOC_API_KEY` / `SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL` `sync: false`). The `bullet-cron-staging` placeholder `startCommand` is replaced with `uv run python -m bullet_api.crons.reconcile_pandadoc` (schedule `0 2 * * *`).
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_pandadoc_integrations_client.py` (12 pure tests: parser well-formed/malformed/empty/missing-id, fake watermark recording, and `MockTransport` request shape + pagination), `apps/api/tests/test_slack_notifier.py` (5 pure tests: alert formatting with/without completion date, fake capture, empty-webhook no-HTTP + warning, non-empty `MockTransport` POST body), and `apps/api/tests/test_reconcile_pandadoc.py` (3 `@pytest.mark.db` tests: 3 docs / 1 missing -> exactly 1 row + 1 emit + 1 alert + look-back watermark assertion; second run is a no-op; no-documents is a no-op).
- **Decision**: `signed_after`/`last_check` is a rolling look-back window (default 7 days), NOT a persisted cursor + migration. Over-fetching is free because the `(event_type, external_id)` unique constraint dedupes, so the window is self-healing and can never advance past an un-delivered document. Confirmed with the user.
- **Decision**: the S1-22 webhook is left untouched - the cron REPLICATES the insert (with a comment naming `webhooks/pandadoc.py` as canonical) rather than sharing a helper, because the cron must interleave a per-document Slack alert that the webhook's `persist_and_emit_signed_documents` (count-only return) does not expose. Confirmed with the user.
- **Decision**: synthetic reconciliation rows set `verified_at = now()` (the document came from an authenticated PandaDoc API call, so it is trusted and must fan out like a verified webhook) and tag the payload `source = "pandadoc_reconciliation"` for audit. Leaving `verified_at` NULL would risk a future verified-only orchestrator skipping a healed signing.
- **Discovery**: a concurrent S1-24 session independently landed `bullet_api/pandadoc/client.py` (single-document `fetch_document` -> `/public/v1/documents/{id}/details`). S1-23's `bullet_api/integrations/pandadoc_client.py` is the list-oriented sibling (`/public/v1/documents?status=2`). The two share names (`PandaDocDocument`, `HttpPandaDocClient`, `FakePandaDocClient`) but different endpoints; kept separate now (zero collision with the live S1-24 edits) with a tracked follow-up to fold the list method into the single `bullet_api/pandadoc` package once both cards merge. Confirmed with the user.
- **Discovery**: PandaDoc's list-documents API filters completed documents with the integer enum `status=2` (not the `document.completed` string the webhook payload carries) combined with `completed_from=<ISO 8601>`; auth is `Authorization: API-Key <key>` (verified via context7 against the PandaDoc OpenAPI 7.29.1). Render cron schedules are UTC-only with no DST support, so `0 2 * * *` is 03:00 UK in BST / 02:00 in GMT - a daily safety-net reconcile tolerates the ±1h drift.
- **Verified**: `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on all S1-23 files; the full Python suite collects with no errors and 62 passed / 70 skipped without a DB; the 3 reconcile `@pytest.mark.db` tests (written to the proven `test_pandadoc_webhook.py` pattern) then ran live against the Neon staging branch - 3 passed, nothing persisted (the transactional rollback fixture); the disabled-no-op entry point (`PANDADOC_API_KEY` empty) runs end-to-end and exits 0 without touching the DB; `render.yaml` parses. The cron adds no FastAPI routes, so it contributes zero `make codegen` / `packages/shared` drift by design (no `npm run build` impact - no TS/dashboard files touched).

### 03/06/2026 - S1-24: PandaDoc manual replay endpoint

- **Added**: `POST /admin/pandadoc/replay/{document_id}` (founder + `izzyagents_engineer` only, `include_in_schema=False`). An authenticated escape hatch to re-drive the signing fan-out for a document already completed in PandaDoc whose S1-22 webhook was missed (outage, deploy window, subscription mis-config, or a document signed before the webhook existed). It FETCHES the document from the PandaDoc REST API, builds a synthetic webhook event from its current state, runs it through the same `extract_signed_documents` gate, and pushes it through the EXACT same persist + emit path as the live webhook - no HMAC (the caller is an authenticated operator, not PandaDoc) and no internal HTTP self-call. Behaviour: 401 no session / 403 wrong role (both before any fetch, so no row/emit); 404 when PandaDoc has no such document; a found-but-not-completed document is acked and ignored (`{"ignored",0}`, no row/emit); a completed document is persisted + fanned out exactly once (`{"accepted",1}`) and a second replay is an idempotent `{"duplicate",0}`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/admin/` router package (`admin_router`, registered in `main.py`) housing the replay route; future manual-ops endpoints join it. The existing `/admin/ping` smoke endpoint stays in `main.py`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/pandadoc/` outbound REST client package mirroring the outbound-email seam (`email/client.py`). `PandaDocClient` Protocol with a single `async fetch_document(document_id) -> PandaDocDocument`; `PandaDocDocument` is a frozen dataclass projecting `id`, `name`, `status`. `HttpPandaDocClient` is the production wiring (GETs `{base}/public/v1/documents/{id}/details`), `FakePandaDocClient` is the test double constructed with a `{document_id: PandaDocDocument}` map, and `get_pandadoc_client()` is the FastAPI dependency. `PandaDocNotFound` is raised on a 404 (and by the fake for unknown ids). `__init__.py` re-exports: `PandaDocClient`, `PandaDocDocument`, `PandaDocNotFound`, `HttpPandaDocClient`, `FakePandaDocClient`, `get_pandadoc_client`, `PANDADOC_API_BASE_URL`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py` `pandadoc_api_key` setting (`PANDADOC_API_KEY`, default `""`). `HttpPandaDocClient` fails loudly (`RuntimeError`) on a real call when it is empty, mirroring `ResendEmailClient`; tests use the `FakePandaDocClient` so the key stays empty locally. Needs adding to the Render staging / prod env groups before the live fetch works.
- **Changed**: extracted the persist + emit loop out of `receive_pandadoc_webhook` into a module-level `persist_and_emit_signed_documents(documents, db, emitter) -> dict` in `webhooks/pandadoc.py`, so the webhook and the replay endpoint share ONE persist/emit code path (the audit row and `pandadoc.signed` trigger are produced identically). The webhook's external behaviour is unchanged; its 5 `@pytest.mark.db` tests pass untouched.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_pandadoc_client.py` (4 unit tests, no `db`, no network - `httpx.MockTransport` for 200/404, empty-key `RuntimeError`, fake lookup) and `apps/api/tests/test_pandadoc_replay.py` (7 `@pytest.mark.db` tests: known signed -> row + emit; engineer allowed; unknown -> 404; account_manager -> 403; no session -> 401; idempotent duplicate; found-but-unsigned ignored).
- **Decision**: "emits the synthetic webhook through the same handler as S1-22" is implemented by EXTRACTING a shared function, not by an internal HMAC-signed HTTP self-call - confirmed with the user. The route is `include_in_schema=False` (consistent with the webhook and `/_debug/sentry`; the dashboard does not call it in Phase 1, so no generated-TS-client drift). A found-but-unsigned replay is ignored (no row/emit) rather than erroring, mirroring the webhook. No `PandaDocReplayResponse` Pydantic model was added: the route is hidden so a model would never reach the generated client, and the bare-`dict` return matches the sibling webhook.
- **Discovery**: PandaDoc's REST API authenticates with the header `Authorization: API-Key <key>` (NOT `Bearer <key>`, unlike Resend); the document-detail endpoint is `GET https://api.pandadoc.com/public/v1/documents/{id}/details`. `HttpPandaDocClient` takes an optional `httpx.AsyncBaseTransport` purely as a testability hook (a `MockTransport` stands in with no network; production passes `None`).
- **Verified**: `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean across `apps/api` (66 files); full Python suite 45 passed / 67 skipped without a DB (no collection errors). The 7 replay + 5 webhook + 9 auth-dependency `@pytest.mark.db` tests were then run live and ALL 21 passed against a throwaway local Postgres (pgvector is unavailable on the native PG so the full `alembic upgrade head` cannot run; a minimal faithful schema - `user_role` enum, `users`, `sessions`, `clients`, `onboarding_events` - was created, the tests ran, and the database was dropped, nothing persisted). `make codegen` shows zero diff in `packages/shared` (the route stays out of the schema); `pnpm -r typecheck` and `pnpm -r build` both clean. The replay db tests run against the Neon ephemeral branch in CI once the `python-tests` Neon secrets land (tracked separately).

### 03/06/2026 - S1-20: Sentry error monitoring + PII scrubbing (api + dashboard)

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/observability/` package. `sentry.py` holds the disabled-by-default Sentry integration. `init_sentry(settings)` no-ops when `settings.sentry_dsn` is empty (local / CI / tests) - `sentry_sdk` is never even imported in that path, so nothing is ever sent; with a DSN it calls `sentry_sdk.init(...)` with `environment`, `release=os.getenv("RENDER_GIT_COMMIT") or None`, `traces_sample_rate`, `send_default_pii=False`, and registers `scrub_event` as both `before_send` and `before_send_transaction`. The FastAPI/Starlette integration auto-enables (fastapi installed) and the built-in `EventScrubber` is left on. Module import is side-effect free (no Settings constructed at import).
