- **Added**: PII scrubber (`scrub_event`) implementing the SHARED CONTRACT mirrored byte-for-byte in the dashboard slice (`apps/dashboard/src/lib/sentry-scrub.ts`): sentinel `[Filtered]`; a 25-key case-insensitive denylist; and three free-text regexes (email, loose international phone, and signed/bearer URL = any `http(s)` token hitting `pandadoc.com` or carrying a `token|signature|sig|expires|x-amz-` query param). The walk has TWO ZONES: inside DATA sub-trees (`request`, `extra`, `contexts`, `user`, `tags`, stack-frame `vars`, breadcrumb `data`) a denylisted key has its whole value replaced with `[Filtered]`; in Sentry's STRUCTURAL envelope (exception messages, breadcrumb messages, the transaction name) the denylist is NOT applied and strings are regex-redacted instead. Every string, in either zone, is additionally regex-redacted for email/phone/signed-URL.
- **Added**: three `Settings` fields in `config.py` - `sentry_dsn` (default ""), `sentry_environment` (default "local"), `sentry_traces_sample_rate` (default 0.0), each documented to note empty DSN disables Sentry and that Render env groups set staging/prod values.
- **Added**: `main.py` now calls `init_sentry(get_settings())` immediately after `configure_logging()` and before the `FastAPI(...)` app is constructed, plus a founder-gated, `include_in_schema=False` debug endpoint `GET /_debug/sentry` that raises a `RuntimeError` with HARDCODED FAKE email/transcript values so an operator can confirm capture + scrubbing in staging. Confirmed zero OpenAPI / generated-client drift (`make codegen` then `git diff --stat -- packages/shared` shows nothing) - the endpoint stays out of the schema.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_sentry_scrubbing.py` - 10 pure unit tests (no DB, no `bullet_api.main` import). Cover email scrubbing across message / exception value / stack-frame `vars` / `request.data` (asserting no raw `@example.com` survives JSON serialisation), phone, PandaDoc + R2 signed URLs, denylisted keys (incl. non-string values), case-insensitivity, non-PII preservation, structural-message regex-redaction (an email in the exception message becomes `[Filtered]` while the rest of the message stays readable, mirroring the `/_debug/sentry` raise), defensive mixed/None handling, and `init_sentry` no-op with empty DSN (asserts `sentry_sdk.get_client()` has no DSN and `is_active()` is False). All green.
- **Added**: `sentry-sdk[fastapi]>=2.0,<3.0` to `apps/api/pyproject.toml`; `uv lock` pins it at **2.61.1** in the root `uv.lock`.
- **Decision**: the scrubber is two-zone (data vs structural) precisely because the denylist key `value` collides with Sentry's exception-message key (`exception.values[].value`). A blanket denylist would turn every Sentry issue title into `RuntimeError: [Filtered]` and gut the feature; instead the denylist applies only inside data sub-trees, and structural messages are regex-redacted so an embedded email becomes `[Filtered]` while the message stays readable. `value` / `value_text` / `transcript` are still filtered wholesale wherever they appear as DATA keys (`extra`, `vars`, request body, `contexts`). Python and TS scrubbers are kept byte-aligned.
- **Discovery**: structural limitation documented in `sentry.py` - free-form transcript PROSE landing in a NON-denylisted string field cannot be pattern-matched (embedded email/phone are caught by regex, but a name/address mentioned in passing is not). The defence relies on keeping transcript-bearing fields on the denylist plus `send_default_pii=False` keeping transcript bodies out of events in the first place.
- **Added**: dashboard Sentry (`@sentry/nextjs` 10.56.0, pinned in `pnpm-lock.yaml`). New `apps/dashboard/sentry.server.config.ts` + `sentry.edge.config.ts`, `src/instrumentation.ts` (`register()` by `NEXT_RUNTIME` + `onRequestError`), `src/instrumentation-client.ts` (browser init + `onRouterTransitionStart`), `src/lib/sentry-scrub.ts` (the TS scrubber), and `src/app/global-error.tsx` (dark React error boundary calling `Sentry.captureException`). All three `Sentry.init` runtimes are DSN-gated (no-op when `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` is empty), `tracesSampleRate: 0`, with `scrubEvent` on `beforeSend`/`beforeSendTransaction`. `next.config.ts` is wrapped with `withSentryConfig` so the Render dashboard build uploads source maps (then deletes them, `deleteSourcemapsAfterUpload`) using build-time `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`/`SENTRY_ORG`/`SENTRY_PROJECT`; with no token the build still succeeds (upload skipped). An unlinked `src/app/debug/sentry/page.tsx` throws on demand to exercise the global-error -> Sentry -> source-map path.
- **Added**: Render + env wiring. `render.yaml`: `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT` in both `bullet-staging-env` (`staging`) and `bullet-prod-env` (`production`) groups; `SENTRY_DSN` (`sync: false`) on `bullet-api-staging`; `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` + build-time `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`/`SENTRY_ORG`/`SENTRY_PROJECT` on `bullet-dashboard-staging` (with a note that prod services get their own per-service DSNs when they land). `.env.example` gains a commented S1-20 block (empty DSNs = disabled locally; build-time-only vars noted).
- **Decision**: Sentry ships disabled-by-default - an empty DSN means local dev, CI, tests, and codegen send nothing. Real DSNs are pasted into Render once the account is upgraded. Errors-only to start (`traces_sample_rate` / `tracesSampleRate` = 0); performance tracing is one env value away. The deliberate error triggers (`GET /_debug/sentry`, founder-gated + hidden from OpenAPI; the unlinked `/debug/sentry` page) are kept permanently so the capture + scrub + source-map pipeline stays re-verifiable.
- **Discovery**: Bullet's existing Sentry account is on a plan that allows only ONE member, which blocks both inviting `team@izzyagents.ai` and the Team-tier source-map upload this card needs (per `docs/infrastructure.md` A5). The integration is complete and unit-tested now; the live "deliberate raise lands in Sentry with email `[Filtered]`" + "dashboard runtime error with a source-mapped stack" checks are deferred until the plan is upgraded. A client-action card was created on the Flow board to track the upgrade + DSN provisioning.
- **Verified**: 10/10 API scrubber tests pass; `ruff check` / `ruff format --check` clean on the new Python; zero `make codegen` drift (the `/_debug/sentry` endpoint stays out of the schema); dashboard `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm -r typecheck` + a tokenless `next build` all pass (the `/debug/sentry` route and `global-error` compile). Live Sentry verification is pending the account upgrade.

### 03/06/2026 - S1-22: PandaDoc webhook receiver + HMAC verification

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/webhooks/pandadoc.py` - the `POST /webhooks/pandadoc` receiver (FastAPI router, `prefix="/webhooks"`, registered in `main.py`). Reads the raw request body, verifies the HMAC signature from the `signature` query param against `PANDADOC_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and returns 401 before any DB write on failure, parses the JSON (400 on a malformed body), extracts the signed documents, then for each does `INSERT INTO onboarding_events (event_type, external_id, payload, verified_at) VALUES (:et, :eid, cast(:p AS jsonb), now()) ON CONFLICT (event_type, external_id) DO NOTHING RETURNING id`. A newly-inserted row emits one Inngest `pandadoc.signed` event (`{document_id, onboarding_event_id}`) and `db.commit()` runs only after the emit, so an emit failure rolls the row back and PandaDoc's retry re-runs cleanly. Returns `{"status": "accepted"|"duplicate"|"ignored", "events": N}`. Exposes the `get_pandadoc_secret()` dependency (overridable in tests).
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/events.py` - the Inngest `EventEmitter` seam, mirroring the email-client seam: `EventEmitter` Protocol, `InngestEventEmitter` (wraps the shared `inngest_client` via `await client.send(inngest.Event(name=..., data=...))`), `FakeEventEmitter` test double recording `sent: list[tuple[str, dict]]`, `get_event_emitter()` dependency, and `PANDADOC_SIGNED_EVENT = "pandadoc.signed"` (used as both the `onboarding_events.event_type` and the Inngest event name). Re-exported from `worker/__init__.py`. `webhooks/__init__.py` now re-exports `pandadoc_router`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/webhooks/pandadoc_core.py` - pure, dependency-free PandaDoc helpers (standard library only: `hmac`, `hashlib`). `verify_pandadoc_signature(raw_body, signature, secret)` returns True iff `signature` is the lowercase hex HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body keyed with the shared secret, compared in constant time via `hmac.compare_digest`; fails closed (False) when signature is None/empty or secret is empty. `extract_signed_documents(payload)` pulls the `document.completed` documents out of the posted event payload, returning frozen `SignedDocument(document_id, event)` records de-duplicated by document id (first wins) in order; tolerant of a single dict or a JSON array, and never raises on malformed input (returns `[]`). Exposes `PANDADOC_COMPLETED_STATUS = "document.completed"`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py` `pandadoc_webhook_secret` setting (`PANDADOC_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, default `""`), plus the var in `.env.example` and in both `bullet-staging-env` and `bullet-prod-env` Render env groups in `render.yaml` (`sync: false`). The handler fails closed when it is empty.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_pandadoc_core.py` - 15 plain-pytest unit tests (no `db` marker) covering correct/tampered/wrong-secret/None/empty/empty-secret/typo/case-changed signatures and completed-event extraction (single-dict, mixed-array, de-duplication, order preservation, malformed inputs). `apps/api/tests/test_pandadoc_webhook.py` - 5 `@pytest.mark.db` integration tests (httpx ASGITransport, `get_session`/`get_pandadoc_secret`/`get_event_emitter` overridden): valid+new persists one row with `verified_at` set and emits once; replay is idempotent (no new row, no second emit); tampered and missing signatures return 401 with no row and no emit; a non-completed event is acked and ignored.
- **Decision**: the receiver lives in the FastAPI backend (`apps/api`), not the Next.js dashboard. The `onboarding_events` table, the Inngest client, the `(event_type, external_id)` dedup pattern and the pytest harness are all Python here, and `main.py` already reserved "PandaDoc webhook" as a future route; PandaDoc posts to `bullet-api-staging.onrender.com/webhooks/pandadoc`. Confirmed with the user.
- **Decision**: the route is `include_in_schema=False`, keeping it out of the generated OpenAPI document so it does not drift the typed TS client (the dashboard never calls this route; PandaDoc does). No `make codegen` regeneration needed.
- **Decision**: a replay (conflict on the unique key) does not re-emit the Inngest event - the receiver fans out at-most-once per persisted row; downstream idempotency (`platform_actions.idempotency_key`) is defence-in-depth. The insert-then-emit-then-commit ordering is the cheap "no outbox yet" guarantee; full reconciliation via `processed_at` + a cron is future scope (S1-25+).
- **Decision**: no new Alembic migration and no new dependencies - the S1-08 `onboarding_events` table already carries every column and the `(event_type, external_id)` UNIQUE constraint, and `hmac`/`hashlib`/`json` are stdlib while `inngest`/`httpx` are already in `apps/api/pyproject.toml`.
- **Discovery**: confirmed against developers.pandadoc.com that the PandaDoc webhook signature scheme is still HMAC-SHA256 of the raw HTTP body (UTF-8), keyed with the subscription "Shared Key", delivered as the `signature` URL query parameter and compared as a lowercase hex digest. Payload is a JSON array of event objects; document id is at `data.id` and the fully signed/executed state is `data.status == "document.completed"`. This matches the S1-22 contract exactly - no discrepancy.
- **Verified**: `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean across `apps/api` (55 files); full Python suite 40 passed / 60 skipped without a DB; `pnpm -r typecheck` and `pnpm -r build` clean; the route registers and is confirmed absent from `app.openapi()['paths']`. The 5 `@pytest.mark.db` webhook tests + the 3 `onboarding_events` table-contract tests were then run live and ALL 8 passed against a throwaway local Postgres (docker is down and pgvector is unavailable on the native PG, so the full `alembic upgrade head` chain cannot run there; a temporary database was created with a faithful copy of the migration-0004 DDL plus a minimal `clients` FK target, the tests ran, and the database was dropped - nothing persisted). In CI the same tests run against the Neon ephemeral branch via the migration chain.

### 03/06/2026 - CI secrets action doc

- **Added**: `docs/GitHub Actions fix.md` - concise owner-action checklist for adding the three Neon repository secrets (`NEON_API_KEY`, `NEON_PROJECT_ID`, `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL`) that the `python-tests` CI job needs, plus verify/re-run steps. Packages the 02/06 secrets-gap discovery into a do-this list.

### 02/06/2026 - Scope flag: onboarding questionnaire rebuild (Phase 1 vs Phase 2)

- **Discovery**: client (Bullet) raised that they understood the onboarding questionnaire would be **rebuilt** as part of this engagement - a custom front-end intake form that improves the onboarding UX and then pushes the captured data into GoHighLevel on the back end. A document review confirms this is well-supported by the source record, and surfaces a genuine divergence from the currently locked Phase 1 plan:
  - **08/04/2026 meeting** (the focus-shift meeting): the entire pivot to onboarding automation was framed around rebuilding the intake. `meeting summary - 08-04-2026.txt:28` - John "switching back to... building an onboarding process that feeds into High Level... prioritizing a slicker intake process". `:38` - MVP = "replicating the client survey... integrating with High Level"; Joshua = "rebuilding the intake so that an AI agent is trained on all customer information". `meeting - 08-04-2026.txt:604` - Joshua: "we rebuild that whole form intake system... that interview process directly puts all the information into Claude". `:361` - "design out a new questionnaire and interview format".
  - **Loom OB-Phase-1 Rebuild Recommendations** (`docs/loom-video-summaries/OB-Phase-1.md:133`): "**GHL Survey -> Integrated React Form:** Build a custom dashboard form that pre-populates data from the PandaDoc webhook to avoid redundant data entry." A direct recommendation to rebuild the questionnaire as our own front-end form.
  - **21/04/2026 meeting** is where it was bucketed as **Phase 2**. `IzzyAgents & Bullet Digital Media - 2026_04_21...md:44` - Chris Dunn asked keep-GHL-portal vs build-custom-portal; "John Limber immediately supported building a custom portal... in phase two". The agnostic "front-end service where a gym owner plugs in details" vision was framed as the Phase 2 customer-facing product.
- **Discovery**: the divergence stems from conflating two distinct things in `docs/phase-1-plan.md` section 3.4. (a) A full custom-branded **client portal** (self-service customer-facing product) - reasonably deferred to Phase 2. (b) Rebuilding the **questionnaire/intake form itself** as a custom front-end that feeds GHL - which the 08/04 record and the OB-Phase-1 "Integrated React Form" recommendation treat as core onboarding-UX work. The plan deferred both together; the client's expectation is that (b) is in scope.
- **Open question (for scope review, not yet decided)**: confirm whether the custom front-end questionnaire (option b) should be pulled into Phase 1 scope, or remain a Phase 2 deliverable. No plan or board change has been made; flagged for John/Stephen + internal decision. Current Phase 1 board only has S2-12 (capture GHL OB V2 completion webhook), S2-13 (ingest portal data), S2-14 (use answers to default campaign type) - all of which assume the **existing** GHL survey stays as the data-entry surface.

### 02/06/2026 - S1-18: Login + email confirmation pages

- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/app/login/page.tsx` - the real `/login` page (replaces the S1-18 stub). Client component: email + password form (`Label`/`Input` with associated `htmlFor`/`id`, submit `Button`), posts via `login()`. On success redirects to a sanitised `?next` (relative-only via a `safeNext()` guard, defaults to `/clients`). 401 -> "Invalid email or password.", 429 -> "Too many attempts.", 403 (email not confirmed) -> error Alert plus a "Resend confirmation email" button calling `resendConfirmation()` with neutral, non-enumerating copy. `useSearchParams()` is isolated inside an inner component wrapped in `<Suspense>` so `next build` does not fail the static-prerender check.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/app/confirm/[token]/page.tsx` - new dynamic confirmation route. Client component reads the token via `useParams()`, calls `confirmEmail(token)` once on mount (a `useRef` guard prevents the React 19 StrictMode double-invoke from firing two requests), and renders a state machine: pending -> "Confirming your email..."; `confirmed`/`already_confirmed` -> success Alert + auto-redirect to `/login` after 1.5s + a manual "Go to login" button; 400 -> invalid; 410 -> expired; other -> generic error, each with a "Go to login" link.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/lib/auth.ts` - thin typed wrappers over the `@bullet/shared` client centralising HTTP-status -> user-message mapping: `login()`, `logout()` (best-effort, never throws), `confirmEmail()` (narrows `StatusResponse.status` to `confirmed`/`already_confirmed`), `resendConfirmation()` (always resolves). Returns discriminated `AuthResult`/`ConfirmResult` unions; no `any`.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/hooks/use-me.ts` - `useMe()` TanStack Query hook wrapping `GET /me` with `retry: false` so a 401 surfaces immediately as `isError` (the auth guard depends on this). Exports the `Me` type derived from the generated `components['schemas']['MeResponse']`, not hand-redefined.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/components/ui/{button,input,label,alert}.tsx` - minimal hand-rolled primitives (no shadcn/Radix dependency) built on the existing `cn()` + `class-variance-authority`, dark-mode tokens only (`bg-primary`, `border-border`, `text-muted-foreground`, etc.). `Button` (primary/secondary/ghost), `Input`, `Label` (forwardRef), `Alert` (error/success/info, `role="alert"` on error).
- **Changed**: `apps/dashboard/src/middleware.ts` - added `/confirm` to `PUBLIC_PATHS` so the confirmation link is reachable without a session cookie (it is the one authenticated-area exception alongside `/login` and `/api/healthz`). The matcher and presence-only cookie check are otherwise unchanged.
- **Added**: `apps/api/scripts/seed_e2e_users.py` - standalone idempotent seed for the E2E suite (NOT importable package code; no `src/` change, no new endpoint, no OpenAPI impact). Upserts a confirmed `login` user and an unconfirmed `confirm` user by email (`ON CONFLICT (email) DO UPDATE ... RETURNING id`), reuses the existing argon2 hasher (so logins verify against `auth/login.py`) and the production `generate_confirmation_token()`, and prints exactly one JSON line of credentials + a freshly minted token on stdout (logs to stderr).
- **Decision**: no public sign-up. Users are created by a system admin (name + email, generated password handed to the user); there is no self-registration endpoint or page. More secure (no external account creation) and matches Bullet's internal-tool model. The admin "create user" screen is a separate, future card and is out of scope for S1-18.
- **Decision**: `POST /auth/confirm/{token}` deliberately does not create a session (locked S1-14 behaviour, left unchanged). "Click the link and log in" is therefore two steps: the confirmation page confirms, then routes the user to `/login`.
- **Decision**: the authenticated-area guard is client-side, not in middleware. Edge middleware only checks the `session` cookie is present (it cannot cheaply validate the opaque token); the authenticated shell calls `GET /me` and redirects to `/login` on 401. This is what makes a deleted/stale session cookie get rejected even though it passes middleware.
- **Decision**: the confirmation E2E mints the token directly (via the seed script's `generate_confirmation_token`) rather than scraping a captured email, so S1-18 needs zero backend `src/` changes. The Playwright suite runs locally via `make e2e`; wiring it into GitHub Actions CI (Postgres service + both servers + browsers) is deferred to a follow-up card.
- **Verified**: clean `next build` (all routes compile: `/login` static, `/confirm/[token]` dynamic, `/clients` static), `pnpm -r typecheck` clean, `make codegen` shows NO drift (no endpoints added), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean on the seed script, and the full live `make e2e` run passes both specs (login -> /clients -> logout -> stale-cookie 401 rejection; confirm -> login -> /clients) against the real API + dashboard on a migrated Postgres.
- **Discovery**: the dashboard's `next lint` is non-functional repo-wide - no ESLint config is committed, so `next lint` drops into its interactive setup prompt and exits non-zero regardless of source. CI runs lint as `|| true` (soft) and the real TS gate is `tsc --noEmit` + `next build`, so this does not block S1-18, but the lint toolchain needs a flat ESLint config in a follow-up.

### 02/06/2026 - S1-18: Playwright E2E suite (auth flows against the real stack)

- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/playwright.config.ts` - `testDir: './e2e'`, single chromium project (`devices['Desktop Chrome']`), `baseURL: http://localhost:3000`, `trace: 'on-first-retry'`, `globalSetup: './e2e/global-setup.ts'`. `fullyParallel: false` + `workers: 1` because the seeded users are shared mutable state (the confirm user transitions unconfirmed -> confirmed mid-suite). `webServer` is a two-server ARRAY that Playwright boots and health-waits on: (1) API `uv run uvicorn bullet_api.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000` (cwd `../api`, health `http://127.0.0.1:8000/healthz`, env passes `DATABASE_URL` through + `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3000` + `RESEND_API_KEY=''`, `stdout: 'pipe'`); (2) dashboard `pnpm dev` (health `http://localhost:3000/login`, env `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:8000`). Both `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI`, `timeout: 120_000`.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/e2e/global-setup.ts` - default-exported async fn that runs the seed via `execFileSync('uv', ['run', 'python', 'scripts/seed_e2e_users.py'], { cwd: <apps/api>, env: process.env })`, takes the LAST non-empty stdout line (logs go to stderr), `JSON.parse`s it, and writes `e2e/.fixtures.json`. Throws a clear, actionable error if the seed fails to run or the JSON cannot be parsed.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/e2e/fixtures.ts` - typed `getFixtures()` reading `.fixtures.json` (`{ login: {email,password}, confirm: {email,password,token} }`). Called lazily inside each test body, never at import time, so `playwright test --list` (which collects specs but does NOT run globalSetup) can enumerate tests without the fixtures file existing yet.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/e2e/login.spec.ts` - "seeded user logs in, sees /clients, logs out, stale cookie rejected": logs in with the confirmed `login` fixture, asserts `/clients` + visible "Log out" + "Client list" copy, captures the `session` cookie BEFORE logout, logs out (back to `/login`), then re-injects the captured (now server-invalidated) cookie via `context.addCookies` and re-visits `/clients` - middleware passes (cookie present) but the shell's `/me` 401s and the client guard redirects to `/login`.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/e2e/confirmation.spec.ts` - "unconfirmed user confirms via the link, then logs in": visits `/confirm/<token>`, asserts the "Your email is confirmed" success Alert, waits for the ~1.5s auto-redirect to `/login`, then logs in with the now-confirmed `confirm` fixture and asserts `/clients`.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/package.json` - `@playwright/test@^1.60.0` devDependency + `"test:e2e": "playwright test"` script. `apps/dashboard/.gitignore` (new) ignores `/test-results/`, `/playwright-report/`, `/playwright/.cache/`, and `/e2e/.fixtures.json`.
- **Added**: root `Makefile` `e2e` target (`pnpm --filter @bullet/dashboard test:e2e`) + help line. Documents the preconditions: docker Postgres up, `make db-upgrade` applied, `DATABASE_URL` exported; Playwright then boots both servers and seeds the fixtures itself.
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @bullet/dashboard exec playwright test --list` enumerates both tests cleanly; dashboard `pnpm typecheck` passes (the `e2e/*.ts` files are in tsconfig scope). Full live run (Docker + DB) is left to the orchestrator.

### 02/06/2026 - S1-18: Authenticated shell + app header (auth guard chrome)

- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/components/authenticated-shell.tsx` (`'use client'`) - `AuthenticatedShell({ children })` client-side auth guard. Calls `useMe()`; while `/me` is in flight it renders a minimal dark, non-sensitive `Loading…` placeholder. On `isError` (a 401 surfaces immediately because `useMe` uses `retry: false`) a `useEffect` calls `useRouter().replace('/login')` and the component renders only the placeholder, so a stale/cleared session bounces to login instead of flashing the dashboard. When `user` is present it renders `<AppHeader user={user} />` then `{children}`.
- **Added**: `apps/dashboard/src/components/app-header.tsx` (`'use client'`) - `AppHeader({ user }: { user: Me })` dark top bar (`border-b border-border`, flex space-between): left wordmark "Bullet Digital Media", right shows `full_name` (fallback `email`) + muted `role` and a `Button variant="secondary"` "Log out". Logout handler disables the button + shows "Logging out…", awaits `logout()`, then `queryClient.removeQueries({ queryKey: ['me'] })` to drop the cached identity before `router.replace('/login')` so the guard cannot re-render stale auth.
- **Changed**: `apps/dashboard/src/app/clients/page.tsx` - existing `<main>` (Clients heading + `<HealthStatus/>` + "Client list -- implemented in S1-31" copy) now wrapped in `<AuthenticatedShell>`. Page stays a server component rendering a client component with children passed through; existing content unchanged. `pnpm --filter @bullet/dashboard typecheck` and `build` both pass; `/clients` compiles (static, 5.18 kB).

### 02/06/2026 - S1-17: OpenAPI -> TS client codegen + TanStack Query

- **Added**: `apps/api/scripts/dump_openapi.py` - imports the FastAPI app and writes `app.openapi()` to `packages/shared/openapi.json` (sorted + pretty-printed for byte-stable, drift-checkable output). Pure schema generation: no server, no DB connection. Requires `DATABASE_URL` present (any value) because importing `main` constructs `Settings`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/schemas.py` - shared Pydantic response/request models (`StatusResponse`, `HealthzResponse`, `VersionResponse`, `MeResponse`, `AdminPingResponse`, `ResendConfirmationRequest`). These give the OpenAPI document named component schemas with typed properties instead of the loose `additionalProperties` object a bare `dict[str, str]` return produces.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py`, `auth/login.py`, `auth/confirmation.py`, `auth/sessions.py` - every endpoint now declares a `response_model` and returns a model instance. Purely additive: the JSON on the wire is unchanged; only the documented schema gains precision. `POST /auth/resend-confirmation` body moved from a bare `dict[str, str]` to the validated `ResendConfirmationRequest` (`email: EmailStr`); a malformed/missing email now returns 422 (was a manual 400, which no test covered).
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - added `CORSMiddleware` (`allow_credentials=True`, origins from `Settings.cors_allow_origins_list`) so the browser dashboard (different origin) can call the API with the `session` cookie. Credentialed CORS forbids the `*` wildcard, hence the explicit origin allowlist.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py` - `cors_allow_origins` setting (comma-separated, env `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`, default `http://localhost:3000`) plus a `cors_allow_origins_list` property that splits/cleans it.
- **Added**: codegen pipeline in `packages/shared` - deps `openapi-fetch` (^0.17) + dev `openapi-typescript` (^7.13); `codegen` script generates `src/generated/api-types.ts` from `openapi.json`. Committed artifacts: `packages/shared/openapi.json` and `packages/shared/src/generated/api-types.ts`. Hand-written `src/client.ts` exports `createApiClient(baseUrl)` (openapi-fetch, `credentials: "include"`) and `POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 7000`; `src/index.ts` re-exports the client + generated `paths`/`components`/`operations` types.
- **Added**: dashboard data layer - `@tanstack/react-query` + `@bullet/shared` (workspace) deps; `next.config.ts` `transpilePackages: ['@bullet/shared']` (shared ships TS source); `src/app/providers.tsx` (`QueryClientProvider`, polling opt-in per query, not global); `src/lib/api.ts` (client instance from `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`); `src/components/health-status.tsx` (sample `/healthz` query polled every 7s, `data` typed `{ status: string }` end-to-end, no `any`), mounted on `clients/page.tsx`. Root `layout.tsx` wraps children in `<Providers>`.
- **Added**: `Makefile` `codegen` target (dump OpenAPI then generate TS, with `INNGEST_DEV=1` + dummy `DATABASE_URL` so it runs with no docker/DB); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` `codegen` job that regenerates and fails on `git diff` drift, with `typescript-checks` gated on it. `.env.example` + `render.yaml` gain `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` / `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`.
- **Decision**: codegen stack is `openapi-typescript` + `openapi-fetch` + TanStack Query (minimal, type-only generation, strict-clean) over `orval` / `@hey-api/openapi-ts` (per-endpoint hook/SDK generation, heavier).
- **Decision**: dashboard reaches the API by direct cross-origin fetch + FastAPI CORS, not a Next.js rewrite proxy. `render.yaml` already declared `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`, signalling direct calls; CORS is the more transparent pattern for cookie auth across known origins.
- **Decision**: generated client artifacts (`openapi.json` + `api-types.ts`) are committed and verified by a CI drift check, not generated at deploy time. The Render dashboard service is Node-only (no Python/uv), so deploy-time codegen is impossible there; committing + drift-checking is also what enforces "the build breaks if the dashboard drifts from the API contract."
- **Fixed**: `apps/api/tests/conftest.py` now sets `INNGEST_DEV=1` (via `os.environ.setdefault`) so importing `bullet_api.main` succeeds in tests/codegen. Since S1-19 added `inngest.fast_api.serve(...)` to `main.py`, importing the app raised `SigningKeyMissingError` in Inngest's default cloud mode (no signing key in CI/local) - this broke collection for every test that imports the app.
- **Discovery**: CI has been red on `main` since 22/05/2026 (the S1-19 batch), with two distinct pre-existing failures, neither caused by S1-17: (1) the `python-checks` job fails at `ruff format --check apps/api` - the committed Python is not format-clean under the ruff version pinned in `uv.lock` (0.15.12), which collapses many manually-wrapped strings; (2) the `python-tests` job `needs: [python-checks]`, so it never ran - which is why the S1-19 Inngest import break (see Fixed above) stayed latent and undetected. S1-19's own verification only imported `worker.client`, never `main`, so it missed the `serve()` import failure.
- **Fixed**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` - `neondatabase/delete-branch-action@v5` pinned to a non-existent version (latest major is `v3`; `delete-branch-action` versions independently from `create-branch-action`, which is correctly on `v5`). GitHub resolves every action at job setup, so the whole `python-tests` job failed at "Set up job" before running anything. This was masked until now because `python-tests` never started (gated behind the failing `python-checks`). Repinned to `@v3`.
- **Discovery**: after the three fixes above, CI is green on 4 of 5 jobs (pre-commit, python-checks, the new codegen drift check, typescript-checks). The 5th, `python-tests`, still fails: the repo has **no GitHub Actions secrets configured** (`gh secret list` is empty), so `NEON_API_KEY` / `NEON_PROJECT_ID` / `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL` are all blank, the ephemeral Neon branch is never created, and `DATABASE_URL` reaches `alembic upgrade head` empty. This is a repo-settings gap (owner action: add the three secrets under Settings -> Secrets and variables -> Actions), not a code issue. Until then `python-tests` cannot run; the suite passes locally and the non-DB subset is covered by the other jobs.

### 28/05/2026 - Project board migrated from StrikeFlow to Flow

- **Changed**: Project management board for Bullet Digital Media moved from StrikeFlow (`c01081f2-c27c-4a8c-b7c5-0b2857254cd9`) to Flow (`b78ad970-8628-4325-aaa8-4b9b1763c789`, board name "Bullet Digital Media Onboarding"). All 10 source lists were recreated on Flow in identical order (Planned, Backlog, Sprint 4, Sprint 3, Sprint 2, Sprint 1, Next Up, In Progress, To Review, Completed); Flow's default "Notes" list is retained at position 0 as a Flow-only extra.
- **Added**: 93 cards migrated to Flow with verbatim titles and descriptions, preserving top-to-bottom order within every list (Sprint 4: 16 cards, Sprint 3: 14, Sprint 2: 27, Sprint 1: 16, Completed: 20).
- **Added**: 64 notes migrated verbatim (1 comment on S2-22 plus 63 activity/QA notes spread across the 20 Completed cards). Note authorship and original timestamps were not preserved per migration plan; all migrated notes carry today's timestamp under the migration runner.
- **Discovery**: Flow auto-stamps a `"Card created in <list>"` activity note on every card at creation time, so the Flow snapshot shows 157 total notes (93 auto + 64 migrated). This is Flow-native behaviour, not migration noise.
- **Discovery**: The StrikeFlow snapshot inventory understated the Completed list by 2 cards (S1-02 and S1-01) and overstated total notes (118 vs the true 64). The Completed-list migration agent verified card count against a fresh `boards_get_snapshot` before acting, which caught the discrepancy. Lesson: always live-verify counts against the API, not against a cached inventory.
- **Decision**: The original StrikeFlow board is retained as a read-only backup; it is not archived or deleted. The Flow board is now the source of truth for sprint state.
- **Decision**: The `flow` MCP server was already registered in user-scope Claude Code config under the existing token; no config or credentials change was required for this migration.

### 22/05/2026 - S1-19: Inngest setup (prod + staging + local)

- **Added**: `inngest==0.5.18` (+ `jcs==0.2.1`) to `apps/api/pyproject.toml` dependencies via `uv add "inngest>=0.4,<1.0"`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/__init__.py` - new `worker` sub-package.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/worker/client.py` - `_make_client()` builds an `inngest.Inngest` client with `app_id="bullet-api"`. Signing key and event key are injected from settings only when non-empty (local dev uses the Inngest dev server which requires neither). `noop_function` is a `@inngest_client.create_function` registered on the `bullet/noop` event - serves as connectivity smoke test. `FUNCTIONS` list is the single import point for `main.py`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py` - three new settings: `inngest_signing_key`, `inngest_event_key`, `inngest_serve_path` (default `/api/inngest`). All default to empty/safe values for local dev.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - imports `inngest.fast_api` and calls `inngest.fast_api.serve(app, inngest_client, FUNCTIONS)` to mount the serve endpoint at `/api/inngest`. Inngest Cloud calls this path to invoke registered functions. Local dev routes to the same endpoint via the Inngest dev server (docker-compose port 8288, `-u http://host.docker.internal:8000/api/inngest`).
- **Changed**: `render.yaml` `bullet-worker-staging` - upgraded from placeholder sleep to a logging health process. Comment explains Inngest functions are served via the FastAPI HTTP endpoint, not this worker dyno. Real job logic lands in S1-25+.
- **Verified**: `uv run python -c "from bullet_api.worker.client import FUNCTIONS; print('OK:', len(FUNCTIONS), 'functions')"` prints `OK: 1 functions`. `ruff check src/` clean.
- **Decision**: Inngest functions are served via the existing FastAPI web service (`/api/inngest`), not a dedicated worker process. Inngest Cloud calls the HTTP endpoint; the worker dyno in render.yaml is reserved for future out-of-band jobs that cannot run inside a request/response cycle.

### 22/05/2026 - S1-21: Render env groups + secret hygiene

- **Changed**: `render.yaml` `bullet-staging-env` and `bullet-prod-env` - added all Phase 1 secrets: `EMAIL_TOKEN_SECRET`, `DATABASE_SSL_MODE` (hardcoded `require` in both envs), `RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM`, `EMAIL_CONFIRMATION_BASE_URL`, `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY`, `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY`, `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `R2_BUCKET_NAME`. All secrets are `sync: false` (pasted into Render dashboard, never committed). `DATABASE_SSL_MODE` forces `require` for both staging and prod, overriding the `prefer` default in `config.py`.
- **Changed**: `render.yaml` `bullet-dashboard-staging` - upgraded from `type: static` HTML placeholder to a full `type: web` Next.js service (`runtime: node`, `rootDir: apps/dashboard`, `buildCommand: pnpm install && pnpm build`, `startCommand: pnpm start`, `healthCheckPath: /api/healthz`). Consumes `bullet-staging-env` group plus `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` pointing at the staging API. Wires with S1-16 (Next.js scaffold).
- **Decision**: `DATABASE_SSL_MODE` is explicitly declared as `require` in the Render env groups rather than relying on the `prefer` default. This means a dev override cannot silently downgrade TLS in staging or prod.
- **Decision**: R2 env vars (transcript storage) declared now in S1-21 so they are in place before S1-27 lands, avoiding a second env-group edit pass at that point.

### 22/05/2026 - S1-33: GitHub Actions CI workflow

- **Added**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` - Full PR/push CI workflow with four parallel jobs: `pre-commit` (gitleaks + hooks), `python-checks` (ruff lint + format check), `typescript-checks` (tsc + eslint best-effort), and `python-tests` (pytest with per-PR Neon branch).
- **Added**: `.github/CODEOWNERS` - Requires review from `@tsizzybots` for all files.
- **Added**: `.github/pull_request_template.md` - PR template with Summary, StrikeFlow card link, test plan checklist, and local lint/typecheck checklist.
- **Changed**: `ci.yml` trigger updated from `pull_request: branches: [main]` to `pull_request: types: [opened, synchronize, reopened]` for correct PR event coverage.
- **Changed**: `ci.yml` Python job split into `python-checks` (lint only, runs in parallel with TS checks) and `python-tests` (runs after `python-checks` passes) to give faster feedback on lint failures before running the full test suite.
- **Changed**: Local Postgres service container replaced with per-PR Neon ephemeral branches using `neondatabase/create-branch-action@v5` / `neondatabase/delete-branch-action@v5`. Branch is always torn down (`if: always()`) even on test failure.
- **Decision**: `continue-on-error: true` on Neon branch creation means tests still run against `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL` fallback if the Neon API is temporarily unavailable, keeping CI unblocked.
- **Decision**: mypy disabled for now (TODO comment in workflow); will be enabled once mypy config is added to `apps/api/pyproject.toml`.
- **Decision**: eslint runs with `|| true` best-effort flag during dashboard conversion; will be tightened once `eslint-config-next` is fully configured.
- **Added**: Three GitHub secrets documented in workflow header comments: `NEON_API_KEY`, `NEON_PROJECT_ID`, `NEON_STAGING_DATABASE_URL` - must be set in repo settings before CI can run Python tests.

### 22/05/2026 - S1-15: POST /auth/logout + session lifecycle tests

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/sessions.py` - `POST /auth/logout` endpoint. Reads the raw session token from the `session` cookie, computes its sha256 hash, deletes the matching row from `sessions`, clears the browser cookie via `response.delete_cookie`, and returns `{"status": "ok"}`. The `get_current_user` dependency is declared alongside the raw Cookie parameter so unauthenticated calls (no cookie, expired session) receive 401 before any DB work is attempted.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_sessions.py` - 4 tests: (a) logout deletes the session row, (b) same cookie is rejected by GET /me after logout, (c) an expired session is rejected at GET /me with 401, (d) logout with no cookie returns 401. All seed real user and session rows; all pass against Neon staging (22/05/2026, 38s).
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/__init__.py` - imports `logout_router` from `sessions.py` and adds it to `__all__`.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - includes `logout_router` so the route is registered on the ASGI app.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-14: Resend email confirmation flow

- **Added**: `itsdangerous>=2.2,<3.0` and `httpx>=0.27,<1.0` API dependencies. itsdangerous signs the time-limited confirmation token (no users-table migration needed - the token is stateless); httpx is the HTTP client used by `ResendEmailClient`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py` settings: `email_token_secret` (signing key, must be high-entropy in env), `resend_api_key`, `email_from` (defaults to `onboarding@bulletdigitalmedia.com` per Q-01 06/05/2026), `email_confirmation_base_url` (the dashboard URL the user clicks). Defaults are local-dev safe; staging/prod Render env groups override.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/email/__init__.py` + `client.py` - `EmailClient` Protocol, `EmailMessage` dataclass, `ResendEmailClient` (httpx against Resend's `/emails` REST endpoint - fails loudly when `RESEND_API_KEY` is empty so a Render env-group misconfiguration cannot silently drop mail), `FakeEmailClient` for tests, `get_email_client()` FastAPI dependency.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/confirmation.py`:
  - `generate_confirmation_token(user_id)` and `_decode_confirmation_token(token)` using `itsdangerous.URLSafeTimedSerializer` with the `bullet-email-confirmation-v1` salt and a 24h `max_age` enforced at verification time.
  - `send_confirmation_email(user_id, email, email_client)` builds the link (`<base>/{token}`) and hands an `EmailMessage` to the injected client. Caller injects the client so tests can use `FakeEmailClient`.
  - `POST /auth/confirm/{token}` flips `email_confirmed=true` and stamps `email_confirmed_at`. Idempotent for already-confirmed users (200 with `status=already_confirmed`). Expired token → 410 with "Confirmation link expired" detail. Bad signature / unknown user → 400 with a generic "Invalid confirmation token" message so the endpoint doesn't leak user existence.
  - `POST /auth/resend-confirmation` with body `{email}`. Always returns 200 with `status=sent_if_unconfirmed` regardless of whether the email matched a real row or whether that row was already confirmed - prevents the endpoint from being a free user-enumeration oracle. Only sends when the email matched an unconfirmed user.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - includes the new `confirmation_router`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_confirmation.py` - 8 tests covering the full flow plus the S1-13 contract that an unconfirmed user can't log in is already covered there. Tests inject `FakeEmailClient` via `app.dependency_overrides[get_email_client]` and assert on `client.sent`.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `pytest tests/test_confirmation.py -v` - 8 passed in 54.92s.
- **Decision**: stateless signed tokens (itsdangerous) over a DB-stored token. Avoids a users-table migration; itsdangerous handles the timestamp + signature verification atomically; rotating `EMAIL_TOKEN_SECRET` (or the `_TOKEN_SALT` constant) invalidates every outstanding token without touching DB rows.
- **Decision**: `POST /auth/resend-confirmation` returns the same response regardless of whether the email is known. Existence-oracle paths are a recurring attack surface; closing this one now is cheap.
- **Decision**: production Resend client refuses to send when `RESEND_API_KEY` is empty rather than silently no-op'ing. Failure-to-send should be loud so it's caught at deploy time, not after a customer didn't get an email.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-13: argon2id login + brute-force lockout

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/brute_force.py` - `BruteForceTracker` sliding-window per-IP failure counter. Defaults: 5 attempts in 15 minutes triggers a 15-minute lockout. Clock is injectable so tests can advance the lockout window without `time.sleep(900)`. Module-level singleton accessed via `get_tracker` dep so tests can swap in a fresh, clock-driven instance per test.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/login.py` - `POST /auth/login` route. argon2id verifies the supplied password against `users.password_hash`; on success mints `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`, stores `sha256(token)` in `sessions` (with caller IP and User-Agent), updates `users.last_login_at`, and sets `Set-Cookie: session=<raw>; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Max-Age=604800`. Brute-force lockout is checked before the DB lookup so a locked-out attacker can't time-probe valid emails. `email_confirmed=false` blocks login with 403 (the contract S1-14 will fulfil). `X-Forwarded-For` is honoured for caller-IP detection so Render's front-proxy reports the original client.
- **Added**: `email-validator>=2.2,<3.0` dependency - required by pydantic's `EmailStr` field on the login body.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - includes the new `login_router`.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/tests/conftest.py` - `async_session` now binds to a per-test outer transaction with `join_transaction_mode="create_savepoint"`. Any `commit()` inside the test or handler under test only releases a savepoint; the outer transaction rolls back at teardown so the live-Neon test pattern stays clean.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_login.py` - 7 tests: valid creds issue HttpOnly+Secure+SameSite=Lax cookie + persist a sessions row; invalid password → 401; unknown email → 401; unconfirmed email → 403; 5 failures from an IP → 6th attempt returns 429 even with valid creds; lockout expires after the 15-minute window (clock advanced via injection, no sleep); lockout is per-IP (locked IP must not lock a different IP).
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `pytest tests/test_login.py -v` - 7 passed in 61.30s. `ruff check src tests alembic` clean.
- **Decision**: in-memory brute-force tracker for now. Render starter plan is single-replica and restarts only on deploy, so the simpler implementation is safe for Sprint 1. The file is the swap point when the API scales out (S2+) - a Redis- or Postgres-backed equivalent ships in the same module.
- **Decision**: lockout check runs before the user lookup. Order matters - if the user lookup ran first, a locked-out attacker could time-probe valid emails (faster failure when row not found vs. row found + lockout check). Constant-time-ish behaviour from the attacker's perspective.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-12: FastAPI app shell + role dependencies

- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/logging_config.py` - stdlib-only structured JSON logging. Each LogRecord renders as one-line JSON with `timestamp` (UTC ISO-8601), `level`, `logger`, `message`, plus any `extra={...}` fields passed by the caller. Idempotent installation so test re-entry and worker restarts do not stack handlers. uvicorn.error / uvicorn.access flow through the same handler for unified output on Render.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/dependencies.py` - `CurrentUser` dataclass, `get_current_user` FastAPI dep (reads `session` cookie, sha256-hashes, joins sessions→users with `expires_at > now()`), `require_role()` factory, and the four named role gates `require_founder` / `require_pd` / `require_am` / `require_engineer`. Hash matches PRD §4.9 / S1-15 plan: only sha256(token) is ever stored; raw tokens live only in the cookie. 401 for missing/expired/unknown cookie, 403 for wrong role.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/auth/__init__.py` re-exporting the public surface.
- **Changed**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/main.py` - now installs JSON logging on import, declares `/version` (returns `__version__`), `/me` (returns the current user, behind `get_current_user`), and `/admin/ping` (smoke endpoint for the `require_founder` gate). FastAPI's default OpenAPI 3.1 emission is preserved at `/openapi.json`. App version surfaced from `bullet_api.__version__`.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_auth_dependencies.py` - 9 tests using `httpx.AsyncClient` + `ASGITransport` (not fastapi.testclient TestClient, which spins its own anyio thread and triggers "Future attached to a different loop" against the test's asyncpg session). Covers `/healthz`, `/version`, 401 unauthenticated, 401 unknown cookie, 401 expired session, 200 founder, 403 AM, `/me` profile, OpenAPI 3.1 invariants.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `pytest tests/test_auth_dependencies.py -v` - 9 passed in 35.23s. `ruff check src tests alembic` - all clean.
- **Discovery**: fastapi.testclient.TestClient runs the ASGI app on a separate anyio thread with its own event loop. Mixing it with an `async_session` fixture that holds an asyncpg connection bound to the pytest loop raises `RuntimeError: Future attached to a different loop`. Workaround / convention: use `httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app))` for tests that share a DB session with the request handler.
- **Discovery**: asyncpg cannot bind a Python `str` to a `(:exp)::interval` cast; it expects a `datetime.timedelta` for native interval values. Adopted convention: compute the absolute `expires_at` as `datetime.now(UTC) + timedelta(...)` and bind that timestamp directly, rather than the interval.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-11: idempotent team seeder

- **Added**: `argon2-cffi>=23.1,<26.0` dependency (`apps/api/pyproject.toml`). argon2id is the password hash used for every user row in `users.password_hash`; the seeder lands today, the login verifier in S1-13.
- **Added**: `apps/api/src/bullet_api/scripts/__init__.py` and `apps/api/src/bullet_api/scripts/seed_team.py` - idempotent seeder for the four named users. John Limber + Stephen Taylor (founder), Max + Luchiano (performance_director). Uses `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (email) DO NOTHING RETURNING id` so re-runs produce the same state. Generates ~96-bit url-safe temp passwords per user, hashes via argon2id, sets `email_confirmed=false` so seeded users must complete the S1-14 confirmation flow before login.
- **Added**: `make db-seed-team` Makefile target.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_seed_team.py` - 4 `@pytest.mark.db` tests using a `test_` email prefix so they never collide with real team rows in staging. Covers idempotency (4 rows after two runs, not 8), argon2id verification of generated passwords, `email_confirmed=false` invariant, and role assignments matching the brief.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `pytest tests/test_seed_team.py -v` - 4 passed in 34.92s. `ruff check src tests alembic` clean.
- **Decision**: `seed_team()` accepts an optional `session=` parameter so tests can run it inside the standard rollback fixture; production CLI uses the no-arg form which manages its own session and commits. Also accepts `users=` so tests can use a `test_`-prefixed roster without touching the canonical `TEAM_USERS` constant.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-10: remaining tables migration (documents, research_results, client_assets, users, sessions, audit_log)

- **Added**: Alembic migration `0006_create_remaining_tables` covering PRD §§4.5-4.10 in one transaction. Five new ENUMs (`document_kind`, `research_result_kind`, `client_asset_type`, `client_asset_status`, `user_role`). `users.email` is `citext` with the `UNIQUE` constraint - case-insensitive collisions are enforced at the DB level. `sessions.token_hash` is `TEXT UNIQUE` (sha256 of the cookie value, never the raw token). `sessions.ip` and `audit_log.ip` use Postgres' native `INET` type rather than `TEXT` so IPv4/IPv6 lookups are first-class.
- **Added**: deferred FK from `client_knowledge.captured_by → users(id)` with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, added at the end of `0006`'s upgrade(). Declared as a plain UUID in 0003 because users didn't yet exist; the FK is now in place. (Downgrade drops the constraint before dropping users so the chain remains reversible.)
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_remaining_tables.py` - 9 live-DB tests: users email UNIQUE, case-insensitive collision, user_role enum rejection, sessions token_hash UNIQUE, four enum rejections (document_kind, client_asset_status, research_result_kind, client_asset_type), and a positive + negative test of the deferred client_knowledge.captured_by FK.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `alembic upgrade head` clean (0005 → 0006). `alembic downgrade 0005_create_platform_actions` clean (all 6 tables and 5 enums dropped; deferred FK released first). Re-upgrade clean. `pytest tests/test_remaining_tables.py -v` - 9 passed in 50.73s.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-09: platform_actions table migration

- **Added**: Alembic migration `0005_create_platform_actions` per PRD §4.4. Two enums: `platform_action_platform` (16 values across hubspot, pandadoc, ghl, asana, stripe, xero, timely, slack, gsheets, gdocs, gdrive, gmail, gcal, meta, resend, firecrawl) and `platform_action_status` (pending, in_progress, success, failed, dead_lettered). `idempotency_key text NOT NULL UNIQUE` is the workhorse that keeps duplicate Inngest fan-outs from firing - the writer code will `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and then read back the existing row. FK to clients (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) and a nullable FK to onboarding_events (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) so synthetic/reconciliation actions can exist without an event. Indexes: `client_id`, `(client_id, platform, action)`, `status`, `started_at DESC`, plus the auto-index on `idempotency_key` from the UNIQUE constraint.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_platform_actions_table.py` - 4 live-DB tests covering insert (with `retry_count` defaulting to 0), duplicate idempotency_key rejection, pending → in_progress → success status transitions, invalid platform enum rejection.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `alembic upgrade head` clean. `pytest tests/test_platform_actions_table.py -v` - 4 passed in 25.87s.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-07 + S1-08: client_knowledge and onboarding_events migrations

- **Added**: `0003_create_client_knowledge` per PRD §4.2. Source enum (`sales_call`, `agreement`, `portal`, `research`, `kickoff`, `manual`), `vector(1536)` embedding column (declared via an inline `UserDefinedType` so no pgvector Python dep is required for migrations), `jsonb` value with a GIN `jsonb_path_ops` index, ivfflat ANN index over embedding using `vector_cosine_ops` with `lists=100`. `client_id` FK has `ON DELETE CASCADE` so wiping a client also wipes its knowledge profile. `captured_by` is declared as a plain nullable UUID without an FK constraint - the FK to `users(id)` is added in 0006 (S1-10) once users exists.
- **Added**: `0004_create_onboarding_events` per PRD §4.3. `client_id` is nullable FK to clients (NULL until the PandaDoc signing event creates the client; orchestrator backfills the column). `(event_type, external_id) UNIQUE` is the idempotency key for webhook replays; NULL external_id rows do not collide (Postgres treats NULLs as distinct under UNIQUE, which is the behaviour we need for synthetic internal events).
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_client_knowledge_table.py` - 4 live-DB tests covering GIN+ivfflat index existence, valid vector(1536) insert, source-enum rejection.
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_onboarding_events_table.py` - 3 live-DB tests covering insert, duplicate (event_type, external_id) rejection, NULL external_id non-collision.
- **Verified (Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**: `alembic upgrade head` clean (`0002` → `0003` → `0004`). `alembic downgrade 0002_create_clients` clean (both tables and `client_knowledge_source` enum dropped). Re-upgrade clean. `pytest tests/test_client_knowledge_table.py tests/test_onboarding_events_table.py -v` - 7 passed in 36.31s against `STAGING_DATABASE_URL_POOLED`.
- **Discovery**: SQLAlchemy's `text()` bind-parameter parser does not handle `:name::pg_type(N)` cleanly when the parameter is followed by a Postgres double-colon cast (`::vector(1536)`, `::jsonb`, etc.). The bind marker `:vec` was left unresolved in the rendered SQL. Workaround: use the SQL-standard `cast(:vec AS vector(1536))` / `cast(:val AS jsonb)` form. Adopted as the convention for all later DB-touching tests that need an explicit Postgres type cast.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-06: clients table migration

- **Added**: Alembic migration `0002_create_clients` (`apps/api/alembic/versions/0002_create_clients.py`). Creates the `clients` table per PRD §4.1 with the two enum types `campaign_flow_type` (`low_ticket_checkout`, `high_ticket_consultation`) and `current_step` (`sales_call`, `agreement`, `signed`, `portal`, `kickoff`, `build`, `live`), 15 platform-ID text columns (`hubspot_contact_id` ... `slack_thread_ts`), self-referencing FK on `parent_client_id` with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, and the four indexes listed in the PRD (`email`, `current_step`, `created_at DESC`, `parent_client_id`). `id` defaults to `gen_random_uuid()` (Postgres 13+ built-in, available on Neon PG 16.12). `email` is `citext` so case-insensitive lookups work without per-query lower().
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_clients_table.py` - 4 live-DB tests against the PRD contract. Each runs inside the per-test rollback fixture so test rows never persist.
- **Verified (against Neon staging, 18/05/2026)**:
  - `alembic upgrade head` (head → 0002) clean.
  - `alembic downgrade 0001_create_extensions` clean - both enum types and the table fully gone (`\dt` + `pg_type` confirm).
  - `alembic upgrade head` re-applies clean (round-trip safe).
  - `pytest tests/test_clients_table.py -v` → 4 passed against `STAGING_DATABASE_URL_POOLED`.
  - `ruff check src tests alembic` clean.
- **Discovery**: SQLAlchemy emits a duplicate `CREATE TYPE` when an explicit `postgresql.ENUM(...).create(bind)` is paired with a column whose type is a *different instance* of `sa.Enum(name=..., create_type=False)` for the same name; passing `create_type=False` on the generic `sa.Enum` is not enough to suppress the emit. Fix: build the `postgresql.ENUM(..., create_type=False)` instance once, call `.create()` on it, then reuse the same object as the column type. This pattern is now the convention for every future migration that needs an enum.

### 18/05/2026 - S1-05a: Neon URL compat fix for asyncpg

- **Fixed**: `bullet_api.config.get_async_database_url()` now strips libpq-only query params (`sslmode`, `channel_binding`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `sslpassword`, `sslcrl`, `sslcrldir`, `sslcompression`, `gssencmode`, `krbsrvname`, `gsslib`) before returning the URL. Previously the query string passed through unchanged, so Neon's canonical URL (`?sslmode=require&channel_binding=require`) crashed asyncpg at connect time with `TypeError: connect() got an unexpected keyword argument 'sslmode'`. Verified `alembic upgrade head` against `STAGING_DATABASE_URL_DIRECT` now completes cleanly. (`apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py`)
- **Added**: `Settings.database_ssl_mode` (env var `DATABASE_SSL_MODE`) - typed `Literal["disable","allow","prefer","require","verify-ca","verify-full"]`, default `"prefer"`. Applied through `connect_args={"ssl": ...}` on the SQLAlchemy async engine in `bullet_api/db/session.py` and on the test-pool engine in `tests/conftest.py`. `"prefer"` works against both local docker Postgres (no TLS, falls back to plain) and Neon (TLS mandatory, auto-upgraded); Render env groups for staging / prod should force `"require"` or stricter so a mis-configured local override cannot disable TLS in those environments. (`apps/api/src/bullet_api/config.py`, `apps/api/src/bullet_api/db/session.py`, `apps/api/tests/conftest.py`, `.env.example`)
- **Added**: `apps/api/tests/test_config.py` - 8 unit tests covering the URL rewrite: Neon canonical URL stripped, both `postgresql://` and `postgres://` short schemes rewritten, already-async URLs preserved with query cleaned, unknown schemes returned unchanged, no-query idempotency, case-insensitive key matching, and the rest of the libpq-only deny-list (`sslrootcert`, `gssencmode`, etc.). All pass; non-libpq params like `application_name` survive the round-trip.
- **Verified**: `pytest -v` runs 12 tests, all pass (the 8 new config tests + 2 DB smoke tests against `STAGING_DATABASE_URL_POOLED` + 2 baseline). `ruff check src tests` clean. `alembic current` against Neon staging direct URL returns `0001_create_extensions (head)`; `psql` confirms both `vector` and `citext` extensions present.
- **Discovery**: asyncpg as of 0.30 accepts ssl modes as strings (`"disable"|"allow"|"prefer"|"require"|"verify-ca"|"verify-full"`) on the `ssl=` kwarg, matching libpq vocabulary. Documented for future env-group config.

### 18/05/2026 - Repo name and Render service name doc corrections

- **Discovery**: the GitHub repo rename from `tsizzybots/Bullet-Digital-Media-Onboarding` to `tsizzybots/bullet_digital_media` recorded as a Decision under 11/05/2026 (`docs/CHANGELOG.md` line 120) and as Added under the same date never actually executed on GitHub. Confirmed today via `gh repo view`: only `tsizzybots/Bullet-Digital-Media-Onboarding` exists; the new name returns "Could not resolve to a Repository". A push against the new URL today failed with exit 128 "Repository not found"; rolling the remote URL back to the original name made the push succeed. The historical CHANGELOG line is left in place as a record of what was decided at the time; this entry supersedes it.
- **Discovery**: similarly, the Render service for the client progress dashboard is named `Bullet-Digital-Media-Progress` (slug `bullet-digital-media-progress`, `srv-d7s18ajbc2fs738mgei0`, URL `https://bullet-digital-media-progress.onrender.com`) - not `bullet-progress` as documented in several places. The package name in `progress-site/package.json` is `bullet-progress`, which is the source of the doc drift, but the Render service was named differently when it was created via "New Static Site" in the dashboard.

Rationale: rename is reversible later if wanted, but every doc currently referencing the wrong name is actively misleading future contributors and Claude sessions. Faster to align docs to reality than to chase a rename that did not happen.

- **Decision**: the canonical Render workspace for this project is the **client-owned** `Agents's workspace` (`tea-d80mkougvqtc73dmah20`, owner `agents@bulletdigitalmedia.com`), not the IzzyAgents workspace (`tea-cunci5popnds73d4n8g0`) where S1-03 originally created the env groups and S1-04 wired the staging services. All Phase 1 services (`bullet-api-*`, `bullet-worker-*`, `bullet-cron-*`, `bullet-dashboard-*`) and env groups (`bullet-staging-env`, `bullet-prod-env`) live in the client workspace going forward.

- **Discovery**: Render's MCP surface exposes no `create_env_group` action. Env groups can only be created via (a) Blueprint sync auto-creating them from `envVarGroups:` in `render.yaml`, (b) the Render dashboard manually, or (c) the Render REST API directly. The Blueprint-sync path was chosen for this remediation.

The `render.yaml` `bullet-progress` block declared since 04/05/2026 was aspirational; the actually-deployed static site in IzzyAgents is named `Bullet-Digital-Media-Progress` (slug `bullet-digital-media-progress`, `srv-d7s18ajbc2fs738mgei0`, URL `https://bullet-digital-media-progress.onrender.com`) and was created via the "New Static Site" flow with its own per-service GitHub connection. It auto-deploys on every push to `main` independent of `render.yaml`. Implication: removing the `bullet-progress` block from `render.yaml` has zero operational impact on the existing service; no Blueprint disconnect step is required before pushing.

The Blueprint connection that would have instantiated them was never made. Implication: S1-04 service provisioning is still pending - the upcoming Blueprint sync in the client workspace will create those four services fresh, not migrate them.

Top-of-file comment now documents the canonical workspace target (`tea-d80mkougvqtc73dmah20`) and clarifies that the IzzyAgents-side `Bullet-Digital-Media-Progress` deploys via its own per-service GitHub connection rather than this Blueprint.
